# noisy v1 design

> Owns: the Gate 4 architecture for noisy v1 as synthesized from design A (`design-v1.md`), design B (`design-challenger.md`), the seven review lenses, and the two judges: module map and ownership, the core TypeScript contracts, data flow from import to export, analysis scheduling and memory release, resolutions and the phone memory budget, the renderer and its passes, committed look semantics and defaults, export, persistence and the scene file with its version rule, failure and recovery, compatibility, dependencies with ownership-cost evaluations, rejected alternatives (including what was taken from or rejected in each source design), empirical unknowns with their smallest tests, the owner's decisions with the two questions still open, and the implementation packages in dependency order.
> Does not own: requirements (`spec.md`), the decision ledger (`decisions.tsv`), research evidence (`research-*.md`, `experiments/`), the source designs it synthesizes (`design-v1.md`, `design-challenger.md`), the finding-by-finding dispositions (`reviews/round1.md` to `reviews/round5.md`), or the verification plan (`verification.md`).
> Once implementation starts, the repository's `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` and `docs/DECISIONS.md` take over the durable parts of this file; this file stays as the planning record.

Revised 2026-10-01 again, as a bounded fix pass against the audit of the seventh revision (`reviews/round7.md` part 6, findings `R7-n`): it derives both normal pairs from 32-bit float depth, the depth plane and a background depth filled on the CPU, by a label-aware weighted plane fit inside the one pass that writes the RGBA8 normals texture, whose 8-bit pair now stores zero exactly, and restates P7.1's tolerances from a simulation, with the per-plane statistic G12 takes (R7-1); measures lighting at each fragment's frame point, which pass 1 now writes into a fourth G-buffer attachment that pass 2 reads and that adds nothing to the frame's peak (R7-14), and says that `relief` does not enter the shadow march (R7-2); states the no-blending-into-float rule as this design's own choice (R7-17); adds the dev-only `labelTextureAt` (R7-4), one mask-sampling rule for the CPU and the GPU (R7-6), a plate-free fixture for pass 1's color row (R7-5), the export module's typed sink (R7-7), the tsconfig rules P0.1 needs (R7-8), a minimum master short side (R7-19), and even master sides among `parseDocument`'s checks (R7-20); and moves `core/color.ts`'s first conversions to P2d.1 (R7-3), keeps the dev lever out of exports (R7-9), removes three forward references and leaves the plan's fourth to its re-key (R7-10), restates the fixture rule (R7-13), the freeze's trigger (R7-12), the seed's point (R7-18), and P4.1's lazy-refinement clause (R7-16), exempts the frozen Finder re-zips from the fixtures' regeneration rule (R7-15), and corrects two evidence labels (R7-21); it adds no pass, worker, runtime dependency, or module.

The lead added one item to that pass from its noticed list (`reviews/round7.md` part 7, `R7-32`): the background depth is filled as each hole component's ring plane plus a push-pull of the residual, so planar surroundings are reproduced exactly and the background pair no longer departs from a sloped surface around an object; it adds the pure `render/fill.ts` and no pass, worker, target, dependency, or module.

## 1. The design in one page

noisy is a static, client-only TypeScript web app served from the Mac today and from an AWS static host at release, used in iPhone Safari and on the laptop.
It has one pure domain core, five leaf owners, one session facade, and a Preact UI that talks only to the facade.

- `core` owns the immutable `SceneDocument`, its schema and migrations, the reducer with undo, and every pure derivation including `reconcile`, the function that lists which analysis products are missing or stale.
  It has no browser dependency and runs under `node:test`.
- `analysis` owns the three models, the ONNX Runtime Web (plain wasm) workers, the model cache, and the memory-release mechanism.
  Heavy stages (depth, SAM encoder, MI-GAN plate) run back to back inside one short-lived **sequence worker** that reuses one wasm heap and is terminated when its list ends; only the small SAM decoder stays resident, and only while the Objects tab is active.
- `render` owns a WebGL2 renderer, one per page, a pure function of (decoded frame assets, the render scene, a frame index) that draws at master size into the preview canvas, caches a quarter-grid simplification in texture space, and composites the plate and object layers with soft edges, banded lighting, palette quantization, outlines, symbol cells, grain, and crunch.
- `store` owns IndexedDB with typed results, per-scene assets, tool caches, the device record, and the per-scene Web Lock; `scenefile` owns the `.noisy` zip.
- `session` composes a small kernel (document, undo, autosave, lock, subscribe), an asset cache (the open scene's asset bytes and their decoded forms), an analysis driver (reconcile-then-run, sequence planning, liveness markers, visibility-aware watchdogs, taps), a render scheduler (dirty coalescing, playback, render mode), and the export functions behind one `EditorSession` facade and one `SceneLibrary` facade.

The photo becomes a 2048 px long-edge PNG **master** at import, with both sides even so that the MP4 encoder takes its frames unscaled (section 12, R7-C); the picked file's bytes are kept beside it as an opaque asset, with a per-scene Drop original action in the Library (Q2, settled).
Depth is stored at model resolution, masks at 1024 px refined against the master, the plate at master size.
The preview renders at master size and is CSS-scaled, and the loop's 4% zoom lives in the geometry pass camera, so the still preview and the PNG are the same pixels, and the playing preview and the MP4's frames are the same pixels before H.264 encodes them; what survives the encoder is checked for grain and for color (section 14, R5-FRESH-8).
A loop renders through the zoomed camera only when its effective orbit is above 0; a loop without camera orbit keeps the still's framing, so a grain-only loop is exactly the still with moving grain (Q19, settled; R4-FRESH-6).
On the phone the look appears within about a second of import in a reduced pass set, and depth, the auto subject, and the full pass set arrive when the heavy sequence ends, about 10 to 11 s; the Mac renders the full pass set from the first frame.

Consequential decisions, each argued below:

1. Reconcile-then-run analysis with one heavy sequence worker per sequence and a resident decoder (B's control model, PHONE-2's worker shape, A's plate gate on motion); `reconcile` returns a plan in dependency order, so a new photo's depth and encoder stages are one sequence in one worker (R4-FRESH-1); plate refills are deferred while the owner is working in the Objects tab, and an inpaint-only sequence neither evicts the decoder nor lowers the preview (AUDIT-2).
2. The SAM encoder is the budget-defining stage at about 0.8 GB of wasm and about 1.0 GB of process RSS (VERIFIED on the Mac with the phone's binary and thread count); the phone's content-process kill point is bracketed at about 1.15 to 1.5 GB (INFERRED from the bench harness's own allocations, section 8.1), whose lower end touches the encoder estimate, so P1's kill-point and encoder-headroom rows run first, with a memory ballast that is written and checksummed so the margin cannot pass vacuously (R3-AUDIT-1); their result has three outcomes, and both a failure and a thin margin take Q9 to the owner (FRESH-1, R5-FRESH-6); the depth input is bounded by token count, so the encoder stays the largest stage at every aspect ratio (R5-FRESH-4).
3. Immutable document, patch-style edits with clamping through one ranges table, produced fields written only by analysis, undo over inverse edit lists so analysis products are never undone and every reducer side effect is reversible (B's model with JUDGE-A-3, JUDGE-B-6, JUDGE-B-7, JUDGE-A-12, AUDIT-3 applied).
4. Typed store results with `damaged`, `newer-version`, and `unavailable` states surfaced in the Library and in the session's save state; a newer or damaged record exports verbatim as a scene file the newer build reopens, and unknown keys at a known version make a record damaged, never rewritten (A's surface with STATE-2, PHONE-4, JUDGE-A-4, AUDIT-4, R5-FRESH-9 applied).
5. WebGL2 renderer, one per page with its programs compiled at boot (R4-FRESH-13), with cached label-aware simplification computed on a quarter-size working grid so that a rerun's cost is bounded by construction (R4-FRESH-2), depth and id written unblended, plate depth filled behind objects, lighting folded into tone before quantization, saturation as a monotone ramp to the gamut cusp that leaves neutral entries alone (R5-FRESH-1), subject-pinned palette entries, full-Oklab palette assignment, and every block-structured effect computed on the output pixel grid because the loop zoom is a camera parameter, not a present-pass crop; frame-space textures are sampled only in the geometry pass, so every later pass reads a G-buffer whose depth, id, and normal are registered by construction under zoom, parallax, and object motion (R3-AUDIT-3) (B's pass architecture with A's cache and the render findings applied, FRESH-2, FRESH-9).
6. Assets are written and committed before any document references them, so no unsaved-asset set exists; a scene is created in one transaction that carries its record, its assets, and its thumbnail, so an import commits whole or leaves nothing (R4-FRESH-8); orphans are swept at one moment only, at open, by the store from the stored record's own references (R5-AUDIT-3); the session keeps the open scene's asset bytes in memory, so `exportScene` assembles entirely from memory and works while storage is unavailable (FAIL-2, FAIL-5, FRESH-5).
7. Scene file is a zip with a central-directory reader tolerant of Finder re-zips, fixed timestamps and entry order, self-describing assets, an integer `version`, total migrations, and newer-version refusal on both files and local records.
8. Visibility is a scheduler input: a hidden tab annotates its liveness marker and pauses the watchdog, cancels its heavy job only when a short grace elapses on time while still hidden (a callback that fires late means the process was suspended, and the job simply continues, R3-FRESH-2), and reruns silently on return; watchdogs measure visible time over the session and inference phases only, with model fetch outside the clock; the cooldown after a terminated worker is keyed to the largest stage it ran (AUDIT-5).
9. Liveness markers tied to a per-tab Web Lock, one slot per worker kind (R4-FRESH-5), detect repeated foreground kills of one stage on one scene and block only that stage; a kill inside the hidden grace or a reload never counts (FRESH-7).
10. Low render mode is a phone-only policy applied from the moment an encoder run is planned until its sequence ends (R4-FRESH-1), set by P1; the Mac always renders the full pass set, and the GL levers act on the GPU process, not on the content-process peak (FRESH-3).
11. Four runtime dependencies, all approved (Q1): `onnxruntime-web`, `preact`, `zod`, `mediabunny`; zip, GIF, and the WebGL2 harness are in-repo.
12. An export is an exclusive phase of the session: it starts only when analysis is quiet, and until it settles the session ignores input, the driver starts no worker and applies no product, and hiding the tab cancels it, so every frame of a loop is drawn from one unchanging scene and no analysis worker ever runs beside the video encoder (R5-FRESH-3).

## 2. Inputs this design stands on

Binding: every `user-owned` row and the `gate2` and `gate3` rows of `decisions.tsv`, the "Design constraints" section of `research-iphone-bench.md`, and `spec.md`.

Measured evidence the consequential decisions rest on:

- VERIFIED (phone bench, iPhone 16 Pro, iOS 26, `experiments/iphone-bench/summary-2026-09-28.txt`): plain wasm works for depth (3.3 s on 24 MP), SAM 2.1 tiny encoder (5.7 to 5.8 s), decoder (80 to 100 ms), MI-GAN at 2048 px (0.9 to 1.0 s); every ORT WebGPU run crashed the tab; MI-GAN at 24 MP crashed on its second inference; ORT used 2 threads; the page reported `webgl2: true`, WebGPU present, a 41 GB quota, `persisted: false`.
- VERIFIED (Mac, same ORT 1.30.0 wasm binary and 2 threads, `experiments/phone-lens/README.md`): wasm linear-memory high-water marks are depth 290 MB (348 MB on a third run), encoder 794 to 796 MB, decoder 76 to 91 MB, MI-GAN 474 MB at 2048 x 1536, 530 MB at 12 MP, 557 MB at 24 MP; no ORT session option or thread count moves the encoder below 791 MB.
  Process footprints on the Mac: encoder 890 MB Safari WebContent RSS and 987 MB Chrome renderer RSS; MI-GAN 659 MB; one worker running depth, encoder, and decoder back to back with `session.release()` between them peaked at 1055 MB RSS and reused the heap (290 MB after depth, 791 MB after the encoder).
  Wasm figures transfer to the phone (STRONGLY SUPPORTED); process footprints transfer as WebKit behavior only (INFERRED).
- CONFLICTING (two Mac Safari measurements with different metrics): `experiments/phone-lens/README.md` shows WebContent RSS draining over 7 s after a depth worker's `terminate()` and staying at 775 to 835 MB for 13 s after an encoder worker's, while `experiments/worker-release-footprint/README.md` shows `phys_footprint`, the metric jetsam uses, back at 58 to 64 MB within 1 to 2 s of a depth worker's `terminate()` and a 0.5 s terminate-then-respawn peaking no higher than one cycle.
  The encoder's footprint drain was not measured.
  Section 7 adopts the worker shape that is right under either reading and makes the cooldown constant a measured value from P1.
- VERIFIED (Mac Safari 26.6.2, `experiments/worker-release-footprint/`): six spawn-run-terminate cycles with flat peaks, threads back to 3 to 6 within about 10 s of every terminate, `numThreads: 1` 1.6x slower; release by termination is VERIFIED for macOS Safari and INFERRED for iOS 26 (CLAIM-2).
- INFERRED (PHONE-6 as re-derived in FRESH-1, from the bench harness's own allocations at the 24 MP MI-GAN crash): the phone killed the tab somewhere between about 1.15 and 1.5 GB of touched content-process memory, not "near 1.3 to 1.5"; section 8.1 shows the arithmetic; MI-GAN's own peak is input-size-insensitive, so the 2048 cap stands on texture memory and the target look, not on the model.
  The bench ran ORT on the main thread (`experiments/iphone-bench/bench.js` imports the bundle in the page module and creates no Worker), so ORT inside a dedicated worker with nested pthread workers, the topology of section 7, has not run on the phone (U1).
- VERIFIED (Mac, JavaScriptCore shell and node, `experiments/ballast-footprint/README.md`): a 512 MB typed array adds nothing to `phys_footprint` while it is allocated and untouched (4.8 MB under JavaScriptCore) and all of itself once each page is touched, by a read or a write (518 MB).
  INFERRED for iOS; this is why P1's memory ballast is written, not merely allocated (R3-AUDIT-1).
- VERIFIED (`experiments/render-review/`): VideoToolbox H.264 retains 15% of per-pixel per-frame grain amplitude at 25 Mbps (`h264_grain.txt`); with 2 px grain cells held 2 frames, the design's default shape, 42.8% at 25 Mbps and 46.9% at 28 Mbps without crunch and 46.5% at 28 Mbps through a JPEG q66 proxy for the default crunch 0.42 (`h264_grain4.txt`); hold 3 gives 46.7% (2 px) and 55.0% (3 px) at 28 Mbps, and 3 px cells retain 55 to 63% (`h264_grain3.txt`, `h264_grain4.txt`); a 640 x 480 GIF with live grain is 282 to 350 KB per frame (`gif_size.txt`); Oklab chroma scaling at constant lightness cannot reach a cartoon blue from a pale sky (`oklab_gamut.txt`), and the shipped saturation rule's curve is tabulated per reference color in `oklab_gamut2.txt` (FRESH-9).
  These are Mac VideoToolbox numbers; the phone's encoder is U12.
- VERIFIED (`experiments/zip-rezip/results.txt`): Finder's Compress on a scene folder adds a leading directory, `__MACOSX/` entries, and data descriptors on deflated entries.
- VERIFIED (`experiments/node-strip-types/`): Node 25 type stripping rejects type-only imports without `type` and rejects `enum`.
- VERIFIED (`experiments/harmony-fit/`, arithmetic on the rule as written, round 6): the harmony fit's candidate set finds the exact minimum over 300 random anchor sets against a brute-force search, two anchors 118 degrees apart fit `triadic` alone, and two anchors 60 degrees apart tie `analogous` and `split-complementary` so the entries' movement decides (section 10 Harmony, R6-DEF-1).
- VERIFIED (`experiments/version-literal/`, TypeScript 5.9.3, round 6): a `const` tuple's `length` gives `SCENE_VERSION` a literal type that follows the migration table, so the version can be derived from the table without loosening `SceneDocument.version` (5.2, R6-DEF-6); VERIFIED for TypeScript 7.0.2 too, by the interrogation's reviewers A and C, who type-checked the same shape under 7.0.2 with `zod` 4.6.5 (`reviews/interrogation-2026-09-30.md`, R7-21).
- VERIFIED (`experiments/product-fit-import-timing/results.jsonl`, headless Chrome 154 on M3): decode 83 to 91 ms, downscale 75 to 78 ms, PNG encode 95 to 114 ms, first draw at about 300 ms for a 12 MP JPEG.
- VERIFIED (phone-lens probe, Safari 26.6.2 and Chrome 154): `createImageBitmap(blob, { resizeWidth, resizeHeight, resizeQuality: 'high', imageOrientation: 'from-image' })` returns a 2048 x 1536 bitmap from a 12 MP JPEG in 79 to 165 ms; `new VideoFrame(source)` works from a 2D `OffscreenCanvas`, an `ImageBitmap`, and a WebGPU `OffscreenCanvas` in both; `isConfigSupported` accepts `avc1.640032` and `avc1.640033` at 2048 x 1536; `CompressionStream('deflate-raw')`, Web Locks, `navigator.canShare({ files })` present in both.
  `new VideoFrame(canvas)` from a WebGL2 `HTMLCanvasElement`, which is what section 14 uses, is VERIFIED on macOS WebKit by the interrogation's reviewer C, who encoded ten such frames at 2048 x 1536 and 28 Mbps in a WKWebView and decoded them to the same flat colors within 1 of 255, and stays INFERRED on the phone until P1.5's U13 row (FRESH-8, R7-21); the probe served design A and had no WebGL row, so P1 adds one and section 14 names the fallback.
  That frame declared null color primaries, transfer, and matrix while the decoder configuration declared BT.709 primaries, the sRGB transfer, and full range, which is an input for P8.2's color row (section 14, R7-21).
- VERIFIED (CLAIM-1): MI-GAN's maintainer added `LICENSE-WEIGHTS` (MIT, Picsart AI Research) on 2026-09-14 and closed issue 25; the Co-Mod-GAN derivative question was declined as a legal question.
- VERIFIED (CLAIM-4): TypeScript 7.0.2's `lib.dom.d.ts` includes WebGPU; moot under WebGL2 but recorded.
- VERIFIED (Mac M3, headless Chrome 154, `experiments/simplify-cost/`, rounds 4 and 5): one rerun of a label-aware anisotropic Kuwahara (three iterations, with its downsample, structure tensor, difference of Gaussians, and master-size upsample) on a quarter-size working grid costs 5 to 11 ms per source texture at the default radius and 12 to 42 ms at the maximum, against 1.6 to 1.7 s for the same filter at master size at the default radius and 88 to 132 ms on a half-size grid; at `simplify` 0 the quarter-grid pipeline returns the master exactly; and a rerun scissored to a changed object's rectangle, with per-pass margins, equals a whole-frame rerun byte for byte and takes 2 to 9 ms.
  The ring, identity, and dirty-rectangle results hold unchanged with the integer label lookups of the round-5 audit (R5-CA-6, runs CA6-1 to CA6-3); that session's first timing runs were taken under load and ran slower, its two runs with the load down fell inside the ranges above, and no figure here is restated (the spike's README).
  Round 5 reran all of it with the downsample made label-aware, which removes the background color a plain box put inside object edges, at no measurable cost (9.2, R5-AUDIT-2).
  INFERRED for the phone (two to three times slower); P1.5 measures it (R4-FRESH-2).
- VERIFIED (`experiments/glyph-rule/`, arithmetic on the rules as written): on a gray ramp at the default `bands` 4 and `amount` 0.3, the round-3 glyph rule draws all 82 glyph cells with ink equal to paper and still draws 6 at `amount` 0; the round-4 rule draws none of either (R4-FRESH-3).
- VERIFIED (headless Chrome 154, `experiments/idb-atomic-import/`): one readwrite IndexedDB transaction over a scenes and an assets store carrying a 50 MB blob commits in 75 ms, and an abort or a failing last request leaves no record and no asset; INFERRED for WebKit from the specification (R4-FRESH-8).
- VERIFIED (Mac M3, headless Chrome 154, the phone's ORT wasm binary, the q8 depth model, 2 threads, `experiments/depth-token-bound/`, round 5): with the short side at 518 px the depth stage's wasm high-water grows with the aspect ratio, 348 MB at 4:3 (518 x 686, 1813 tokens), 359 MB at 16:9, 539 MB at 2.65:1, and 1103 MB at 4:1 (518 x 2072, 5476 tokens, 21 s), which is above the encoder's 794 to 796 MB; with the input bounded to 1813 tokens every aspect measures 277 to 348 MB and 3.5 to 5.1 s.
  INFERRED for the phone; P1.4 carries a panorama row (R5-FRESH-4).
- VERIFIED (arithmetic on the rules as written, `experiments/render-review/neutral_rule.txt` and `light_tint.txt`, round 5): at the default `saturation` the round-4 saturation rule gave every neutral palette entry a vivid color at a hue that is noise (asphalt `#464648` became `#5035ae`, a cloud `#f5f6fa` became `#abb7ed`), its neutral assignment rule rendered a black shadow and a clipped white 0.22 to 0.30 L apart and a gray ramp in three or four tones, and its light tint took a bright green to gray under the default white light at a contribution of 1.
  Under the round-5 rules of section 10 those neutrals are unchanged, black and white render 0.57 to 0.80 L apart, a gray ramp spans 0.84 to 1.00 L at every `colors`, and a white light leaves chroma alone (R5-FRESH-1, R5-FRESH-2).
  The round-5 audit extended both files (R5-CA-1, R5-CA-2).
  On the same synthetic palette the closing revision's harmony fallback anchor was the overcast-sky entry, of chroma 0.0112, and became a different entry when one 8-bit step took the sky's chroma to 0.0100; ranking by weight times chroma weight picks the same full-weight entry on both sides.
  Over every band variant of eight reference surfaces under three colored lights, clamping the shifted color per channel brought two bands of one entry within 0.01 L of each other or out of order in 9 of 288 cases and put the written hue up to 64 degrees off the shifted hue, where the gamut bound of section 10 does neither (0 of 288, at most 1.6 degrees).
  Both files clamp each band variant's lightness to 0..1 (`light_tint.py` then spreads the clamped ones 0.02 apart); under the band-window rule of section 10 Bands (round 7, R7-B7), black and white render 0.52 to 0.78 L apart at `bands` 4, a gray ramp spans 0.84 to 0.89 L at `colors` 2 to 12 with its count of tones still growing, and the gamut bound keeps 0 of 288 cases collapsed with the hue at most 2.2 degrees off (VERIFIED arithmetic, `reviews/round7.md` B7).
- VERIFIED (the WebCodecs specification, WebKit and Chromium source, and a headless Chrome 154 measurement on the M3, by the interrogation's verifier on 2026-09-30, `reviews/interrogation-2026-09-30-verifiers.json`): an H.264 encoder configured at a size other than a frame's visible size scales the frame ("The encoder MUST scale any VideoFrame whose [[visible width]] differs from this value"); WebKit's `encode` hands VideoToolbox the full frame and keeps `visibleRect` as metadata only, and VideoToolbox's default scaling mode stretches; a 2048 x 1365 canvas frame into a 2048 x 1364 encoder came back with a middle-row error of 18.76 against 0.75 for the even control, and 21.33 with `visibleRect`; WebKit's only H.264 configuration checks are even dimensions and a level cap of 5.2.
  This is why section 12 makes both master sides even and section 14 has no size fallback (R7-C).
- VERIFIED from source (WebKit main and its ANGLE Metal backend: `EXTColorBufferFloat.cpp` exposes the extension whenever ANGLE reports it, and the Metal format table marks the 32-bit float formats color-renderable on every Apple GPU family) and by Apple's own triage of WebKit bug 240919 (May 2022), STRONGLY SUPPORTED by web3dsurvey's iOS rows (`EXT_color_buffer_float` 100%, `EXT_color_buffer_half_float` 99.98%, `EXT_float_blend` and `OES_texture_float_linear` about 54%, absent below A17 Pro), interrogation verifiers, 2026-09-30: iOS Safari exposes `EXT_color_buffer_float` in WebGL2; a June 2024 forum statement to the contrary is contradicted by the source; INFERRED for the owner's iPhone until P1.5's probe reads it.
  Section 9.1 still accepts either float color-buffer extension, as a hedge that costs nothing because every float target is 16-bit (R7-F1).
- VERIFIED (scratch project, round 7, `reviews/round7.md` A1 and E): under TypeScript 7.0.2 with `@types/node` 25.9.8, `preact` 10.29.8, `zod` 4.6.5, and `mediabunny` 1.60.0, the three tsconfig projects of section 5 each type-check, a program holding `dom` and `webworker` together fails with duplicate declarations, a `node:test` file without `types: ["node"]` fails with TS2591, and the app project needs `skipLibCheck` and the `esnext.disposable` lib because `mediabunny`'s typings reference `@types/dom-webcodecs`, which duplicates TypeScript's own WebCodecs declarations, and use `Disposable`; `esbuild` 0.28.2 bundling `mediabunny` leaves 4 `console.` calls without `drop` and none with `drop: ['console', 'debugger']`.
- VERIFIED (arithmetic: a Node simulation of the candidate derivations, run in the session scratchpad of round 7's audit pass and recorded in `reviews/round7.md` part 6, R7-1): on P7.1's tiles, with the depth asset at 686 x 518 in f32, normals from a label-aware weighted plane fit over a 7 x 7 window at depth resolution read every tile's `N.L` within 0.0015 of its target through the 8-bit pair, the fit itself within 0.00001, where differencing the 16-bit refined depth at master size gave worst errors of 0.013 to 0.080 over 80 positions per tile, and 0.036 to 0.136 with a 16-bit smoothed copy, of the order round 7's part 5 found.
  On a gentle dome with no model noise the fit left no texel in the wrong `banded3` level away from a level's edge, where the 16-bit path put 1.7% of texels there; with white noise of 0.0001, 0.0003, and 0.001 in disparity the fit put 0.3%, 1.5%, and 6.9% there and the 16-bit path 3.2%, 8.6%, and 28.7%.
  The depth model's own noise on the owner's photos is UNKNOWN, so the owner's sheet J5 of `verification.md` still judges the result on real photos (U18).
- VERIFIED (arithmetic: a Node simulation of the whole chain, from the f32 depth plane through the plane fit, the 8-bit pair, pass 1's filtered read, and the G-buffer's second write to pass 4's crease rule, run in the session scratchpad of the last fix-up and recorded in `reviews/round7.md` part 7, R7-24): on three back-layer planes meeting in creases of 20 and 35 degrees beside a disc object, at masters of 2048 x 1536, 1024 x 768, and 512 x 384 over one 686 x 518 depth plane, the crease rule of section 10 Outline draws along the 35-degree crease a line exactly 2, 3, and 6 px wide at those outline widths on every row, centered within 0.8 px of the crease, and nothing along the 20-degree crease or anywhere else; a 31-degree crease draws and a 26-degree one does not.
  Comparing neighbouring pixels, the reading the rule replaces, never draws a crease: neighbours differ by at most 4.3 degrees across the 35-degree crease at 2048 x 1536 and 8.2 at 1024 x 768.
  Without the rule's flat-sides condition a surface bending steadily at 1.2 or 2 degrees per px filled a band 31 to 77 px wide with outline, and without its one-id condition the rule drew 697 to 3,354 px of line inside the disc along its rim; with both it drew neither.
  INFERRED for real depth maps, whose noise and rims the owner judges on J1 and J5.
- STRONGLY SUPPORTED (WebKit posts and bugs cited in PHONE-3, PHONE-4, PHONE-10, CLAIM-6, CLAIM-7, CLAIM-10): iOS suspends hidden pages and reclaims background tabs first; iOS 17.4 to 18.x dropped IndexedDB connections after backgrounding; Safari deletes script-writable storage after seven days of Safari use without interaction with the site, Home Screen web apps with "Open as Web App" on are exempt and have isolated IndexedDB; `persist()` protects against quota eviction, not the ITP deletion.

## 3. Usage, the owner's view and the caller's view

Two screens.
The Library lists scenes as thumbnails of their current look with Import photo, Import scene, and per-scene Open, Export scene, Drop original, Delete, plus a storage line (used bytes, of which kept originals, protection state, last export per scene).
The Editor shows the live canvas above a panel with six tabs: Look, Objects, Symbols, Motion, Lights, Export; a bottom sheet on the phone, a side column on the laptop.
The Look tab carries the palette's swatch strip (Q4 a): tapping an unpinned swatch opens the Oklch picker and stores a palette target for that entry's cluster, which anchors the harmony like a recolored object; a subject's pinned swatch has no cluster, so tapping it opens that object's recolor picker instead (section 10 Palette targets, R6-2).

Flow on the phone right after shooting: open noisy from the Home Screen, tap Import photo, pick the shot.
Within about a second the master shows under the default look (palette, bands, grain) while a status chip reads "Analyzing: depth, then objects"; about ten seconds later the outlines sharpen, the auto subject is an object, and every control is live.
A tap makes another object; a swatch or an object's color picker recolors and re-harmonizes; Motion turns on a loop; Lights drops a light where the owner taps.
Export renders, then a Share button hands the file to the share sheet.

Flow on the laptop: Import scene from a `.noisy` AirDropped from the phone, or drop a photo or a `.noisy` onto the window, do the detailed object and light work, Export scene back.

Call sites, written before the types so the types serve them:

```ts
// ui/Library.tsx: importing a photo makes a scene without waiting for analysis.
const created = await library.importPhoto(file);
if (!created.ok) return showImportError(created.error);   // heic-unsupported | undecodable | too-small | too-large | store; the HEIC, size, and megapixel refusals in section 12's sentences (R7-28)
navigate({ screen: 'editor', id: created.value });

// ui/Editor.tsx: opening a scene starts the session; analysis reconciles in the background. The canvas belongs to the page's one renderer (section 9.1).
const opened = await openSession({ sceneId, deps });
if (!opened.ok) return showOpenError(opened.error);        // open-elsewhere | missing | damaged | newer-version | unavailable
const session = opened.value;
mount(deps.renderer.canvas);
session.subscribe((state) => rerender(state));             // doc, save, analysis, renderer, mode, canUndo, canRedo, activity
session.edit({ kind: 'style', patch: { saturation: 0.8 } });   // clamped, coalesced per field, autosaved, redrawn
session.edit({ kind: 'fry', fry: 0.9 });                        // the macro rewrites the fried fields through one curve

// ui/ObjectsTab.tsx: a tap selects or creates an object, then recolors it. The Objects tab shows the still, so a canvas point is a frame point.
const at = session.viewToFrame(point);                     // null only while a loop frame is showing (section 9.3)
if (at === null) return session.play(false);               // the touch aimed at a moving frame: it stops the loop onto the still and selects nothing
const tap = await session.tap({ kind: 'pick', at });         // before the encoder has finished, the promise stays pending until the decoder applies the tap (R7-D4)
if (tap.kind === 'created' || tap.kind === 'selected') {
  session.edit({ kind: 'object.set', id: tap.object, patch: { recolor: { kind: 'target', color: { l: 0.79, c: 0.27, h: 142 } } } });
}
// ui/StatusChip.tsx: "Getting objects ready" while state.analysis.decode is queued behind the encoder; nothing reads it from the tap's result (R7-D4)

// ui/ExportTab.tsx: two steps, because iOS needs the file ready inside the tap.
const job = session.exportLoop({ kind: 'mp4' });           // the session's export phase (section 14): waits until analysis is quiet, then renders into the canvas, encodes, muxes; cancel
job.progress((p) => showExportProgress(p));                // { phase: 'waiting-analysis' | 'rendering' | 'finishing', fraction }
const result = await job.result;
if (result.ok) shareButton.onclick = () => shareOrDownload(result.value);

// ui/ExportTab.tsx: a GIF reports its estimated size before the owner commits (Q8, R7-D3)
const gif = session.exportLoop({ kind: 'gif', longEdge: 480, delayCs: 10 });
gif.progress((p) => (p.phase === 'estimate' ? showEstimate(p.bytes, gif.confirm, gif.cancel) : showExportProgress(p)));   // the job holds its export phase at 'estimate' until confirm() or cancel()
```

## 4. Module map and ownership

```
src/
  main.tsx              boot: capability gate, tab id, liveness sweep, the page's one renderer and analysis service, then the Library
  app/                  the composition root's types beside main.tsx: the diagnostic event union, the environment snapshot, and the dev-only test surface (R7-B3)
  core/                 PURE: ids, rasters, scene types, ranges, defaults, schema, migrations, edits and reducer, undo, derivations (layers, hole, plate need, reconcile, clamps), color math, palette resolution, motion math
  photo/                photo import: decode with resize, orientation, HEIC detection, master PNG, thumbnail, color and tone statistics
  analysis/             model manifest and cache, worker scripts (sequence, decode), worker protocol, pre and post processing, mask refinement, known-answer checks
  render/               WebGL2 harness, derived textures, passes, style resolution to uniforms, the background depth fill (`fill.ts`, pure, R7-32), glyph atlas, memory mode, context loss, residency accounting
  export/               PNG still, MP4 (WebCodecs plus Mediabunny), GIF (palette sampling, LZW worker), share or download
  store/                IndexedDB connection holder, typed store, scene lock, orphan sweep, device record, embeddings LRU
  scenefile/            zip reader and writer, pack and unpack, hash verification, typed errors
  session/              kernel.ts, assets.ts, analysis-driver.ts, render-scheduler.ts, export.ts, editor-session.ts (facade), library.ts (facade)
  ui/                   Preact: Library, Editor, tabs, canvas pointer handling, status chip, banners, dialogs
  platform/             capabilities, build id, tab id, visibility source, clock, the generic diagnostics ring buffer (no domain type, R7-B3)
tools/                  build.mjs, serve.mjs (headers.json; in dev mode the fault state and the results sink of section 19; in production mode the production bundle and nothing else, Q21), start.mjs (the owner's start command, Q21), fetch-models.mjs, drive-chrome.mjs, phone-harness/
headers.json            the one response-header contract (dev server now, AWS response-headers policy later)
models.lock.json        pinned model files: source URL at a commit, SHA-256, bytes, license, served path
```

Dependency direction: `ui -> session -> { core, photo, analysis, render, export, store, scenefile, platform }`; every leaf depends on `core` and `platform` only, except `export`, which also depends on `render`'s public `Renderer` type.
`platform` declares no domain type: its diagnostics are generic over the event type, so it imports nothing but `core`'s primitives (the branded ids of 5.1, for the build id and the tab id) (R7-B3).
The one exception to the direction is the **composition root**, `main.tsx` and `app/`, which the rest of this file also calls the app shell: it imports every module, because it creates the page's one renderer, analysis service, store, and diagnostics and declares the types that name every module's types (the diagnostic event union, the environment snapshot, and the dev-only test surface of 5.10); nothing imports `app/` but `main.tsx`, and nothing imports `main.tsx`.
The import-graph test of the build floor asserts exactly this rule (`verification.md` B6).

| Module | Owns (single owner) | Exposes | Must not know |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `core` | document shape, ranges, defaults, invariants, migrations, reducer and undo, derivations, `reconcile`, color and motion math | types, `parseDocument`, `parseDocumentWith`, `peekVersion`, `applyEdit`, `applyProduce`, `derive.*`, `color.*`, `motion.*`, `fryCurve` | any I/O, GPU, worker, clock |
| `photo` | turning a user file into a master, thumbnail, and asset statistics; the 256 px thumbnail of a master | `decodePhoto`, `thumbnailOf` | storage layout, models |
| `analysis` | model files, ORT, workers, memory release, the record of the last heavy termination, known-answer checks, refinement | `createAnalysisService`, `AnalysisService`, `AnalysisError` (declared in `analysis/protocol.ts`, which P1.1 builds, R7-10), `MODELS` | the document, storage layout (it never imports `store`; the driver receives embeddings through `onEmbeddings`, caches them, and hands them back through `openDecoder`, AUDIT-8, R3-AUDIT-2), what is missing (that is `core.reconcile`) |
| `render` | GPU objects, passes, style-to-uniform resolution, glyphs, memory mode, residency | `createRenderer`, `Renderer`, `resolveStyle` | the persisted document shape, storage, timing of frames |
| `export` | encoders, muxing, file assembly, share | `exportStill` and `exportLoop`, each taking a `DiagnosticSink<ExportEvent>` that `session/export.ts` passes from `SessionDeps.diag` (R7-7), `shareOrDownload` | the document, rendering math |
| `store` | IndexedDB schema and connection, asset bytes, tool caches, device record, scene lock | `SceneStore`, `withSceneLock` | rendering, models, the file format |
| `scenefile` | zip codec, manifest, hash verification | `readSceneFile`, `writeSceneFile` | IndexedDB |
| `session` | the open scene's lifecycle: kernel, analysis driver, render scheduler, export orchestration, library operations | `openSession`, `EditorSession`, `SceneLibrary` | GPU objects, worker internals, IndexedDB internals |
| `ui` | view state (tab, sheet height, selection, tap mode) and DOM | screens | internals of any module |
| `platform` | capabilities, build id, tab id, visibility, clock, the diagnostics ring buffer | `capabilities`, `createDiagnostics`, `Diagnostics<E>`, `DiagnosticSink<E>`, `Clock`, `VisibilitySource` | domain: every type it declares is generic or platform-level (R7-B3) |
| `app` (composition root, with `main.tsx`) | the page's composition: the one renderer, analysis service, store, and diagnostics; the diagnostic event union and the environment snapshot; the dev-only test surface | nothing: only `main.tsx` imports it; `window.__noisyTest` in dev builds only | none: it may import every module, the one exception to the dependency direction (R7-B3) |

The session module is five files with their own tests behind one facade (ENG-3, both judges).
The kernel owns the document, the undo stacks, the autosave schedule, the scene lock, and `subscribe`; the asset cache (`session/assets.ts`) owns every asset the open document or its history references, as bytes plus the decoded forms the renderer reads and the 1024 px guide the decoder refines against, filled at open and by every `putAsset` the session performs (FRESH-5); the analysis driver owns the reconcile loop, sequence planning, liveness markers, watchdogs, visibility handling, tap segmentation, the open scene's embeddings (held in memory and mirrored to `store.cache`), the decoder handle, the last decode's three candidate masks (Q5 a), and the auto-subject composition; the render scheduler owns the `RenderScene` projection, dirty coalescing, playback, whether the canvas shows a loop frame or the still, and the render mode; `export.ts` takes a kernel snapshot, the asset cache, the renderer, and the session's diagnostics sink, which it hands to export's functions so that the export module logs its own events through a sink typed for them (5.9, 5.10, R7-7); the facade owns the session's activity (editing, playing, or exporting) and with it the export phase of section 14 (R5-FRESH-3).

State ownership:

| State | Owner | Mutated by | Lifetime |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `SceneDocument` of the open scene | session kernel (in memory) | `applyEdit` and `applyProduce` only | until the scene closes; persisted by the store |
| Undo and redo stacks of inverse edits | session kernel | kernel | until the scene closes |
| Activity: editing, playing, or exporting | session facade | `play`, `exportStill`, `exportLoop`, and the end of an export job | until the scene closes; not persisted |
| Asset bytes (source, master, plate, depth, masks) | `store` | never; content-addressed and immutable | until an open-time sweep finds them unreferenced by the stored record (13.2) |
| Thumbnail (256 px JPEG) | `store`, as a field of the scenes record | `createScene` (the master at 256 px) and `saveThumbnail` (the current look, at close and after an export) | with the record; never an asset and never in a scene file (R4-FRESH-7) |
| Asset cache: bytes and decoded forms (ImageBitmap, planes, the 1024 px guide) of every asset the open document or its history references | session asset cache | filled at open and by every session `putAsset`; entries dropped when neither the document nor the history references them | while the scene is open |
| In-flight analysis, workers, liveness markers (one slot per worker kind) | analysis driver via `AnalysisService` | the driver only | per sequence, per worker |
| Last heavy termination (time and largest stage run) | `AnalysisService`, one per page | the service, when a sequence worker that ran a stage is terminated | the page's lifetime; every session's driver reads it when it plans, so a scene switch keeps the cooldown (R4-FRESH-4) |
| SAM embeddings | analysis driver (the open scene's, in memory, from `onEmbeddings` or the first cache read); the decode worker holds its own copy; `store.cache` (device-local LRU bounded at 400 MB, about 24 scenes) | the analysis driver | driver copy until the scene closes; cache LRU by bytes, evicted first under quota |
| The last decode's three candidate masks (about 2.4 MB) and the object they belong to | analysis driver | each decode replaces them | until the next decode or close; never persisted, so a `cycle` on another object decodes its stored selection first (Q5 a) |
| GPU programs, textures, targets | `Renderer`, one per page, created by the app shell at boot (R4-FRESH-13) | the renderer only | programs for the page's lifetime; scene textures from `load` until `unload`, and within that while referenced by the latest render or by a cache key |
| Model files | `analysis/models.ts` via Cache Storage | download plus SHA-256 check | until `models.lock.json` changes |
| Crash counts per (scene, op, build) | `localStorage` via the analysis driver | liveness sweep at startup; Retry clears | until the build changes |
| Device record (`persistGranted`, install hint shown) | `store` | the Library | forever |
| Selection, tab, tap mode, sheet height | `ui` | UI events | not persisted |

Raw bytes become domain types at exactly four boundaries: `photo` (user image files), `store` and `scenefile` (records and zip entries, both through `core.parseDocument`), `analysis` (worker replies become planes and statistics), and the analysis driver (`localStorage` liveness markers, whose type it owns since `platform` declares no domain type, 5.10, R7-B3).
## 5. Core contracts

All types are `readonly`, ids are branded, external data is `unknown` until a parse function types it, and every fallible boundary returns a `Result` instead of throwing.
No `enum` and no `namespace` anywhere, so Node's type stripping runs `core` (ENG-7); `tsconfig.base.json` sets `verbatimModuleSyntax`, `erasableSyntaxOnly`, `allowImportingTsExtensions` with `noEmit`, `strict`, `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`, `module: "preserve"` with `moduleResolution: "bundler"`, `target: "es2024"`, `jsx: "react-jsx"` with `jsxImportSource: "preact"`, `types: []` so no ambient typings leak in, and `skipLibCheck`.
Three projects extend it, because `lib: dom` and `lib: webworker` declare the same globals differently and cannot share one program (R7-A1):

- `tsconfig.json`, the app: `lib: ["es2024", "esnext.disposable", "dom", "dom.iterable"]`, every file under `src/` except the worker entries.
- `tsconfig.worker.json`, the three worker entries, named `src/**/*-worker.ts` (`analysis/sequence-worker.ts`, `analysis/decode-worker.ts`, `export/gif-worker.ts`): `lib: ["es2024", "webworker"]`.
  Every module a worker entry imports is checked under that lib too, so worker-reachable modules (`core`, `analysis/protocol.ts`, the pre and post processing) use no DOM-only global.
  Worker-global calls (`self.postMessage` with or without a transfer list, the message handler, `self.close`) live only in the three entries, so every other module is written against globals both libs declare, and the app and test projects, which check it under `dom`, accept it too; a worker-only helper outside an entry would fail `tsconfig.json` (TS2769 on a transfer list, R7-8).
- `tsconfig.test.json`, the node tests under `test/`: `lib: ["es2024", "esnext.disposable", "dom", "dom.iterable"]` and `types: ["node"]`, from `@types/node` (Q1 as amended on 2026-10-01), so `node:test` files type-check beside the modules they import, some of which name DOM types.
  P0.1 writes one smoke test under `test/`, so this project has inputs from the first commit; `tsc` fails a project with none (TS18003, R7-8).
  Because this project checks every module a test reaches under Node's typings too, main-thread code that sets timers calls `window.setTimeout` and `window.clearTimeout`, never the bare globals, whose return type `@types/node` widens to `NodeJS.Timeout` (the real `Clock` of 5.10, R7-8).

The build floor's type check runs `tsc -p` on each of the three; `skipLibCheck` and `esnext.disposable` are there because `mediabunny`'s typings reference `@types/dom-webcodecs`, whose WebCodecs declarations duplicate TypeScript's own, and use `Disposable` (VERIFIED in a scratch project under TypeScript 7.0.2, section 2).
The schema library is zod (approved, Q1; section 18); `core/types.ts` exports the `z.infer` types of `core/schema.ts`, so the schema is the single owner of the document's shape (ENG-8 f).
Numeric ranges live once in `core/ranges.ts`; the schema, the reducer's clamping, and the sliders read that table, and a test asserts the schema's ranges equal it (JUDGE-A-12).

### 5.1 Identifiers, results, rasters (`core/ids.ts`, `core/result.ts`, `core/raster.ts`)

```ts
export type Brand<T, B extends string> = T & { readonly __brand: B };
export type SceneId = Brand<string, 'SceneId'>;      // UUID v4, minted at import
export type ObjectId = Brand<string, 'ObjectId'>;    // UUID v4
export type LightId = Brand<string, 'LightId'>;      // UUID v4
export type AssetId = Brand<string, 'AssetId'>;      // 64 lowercase hex: SHA-256 of the asset bytes
export type ModelId = Brand<string, 'ModelId'>;      // '<name>@<sha256 hex>' from models.lock.json (PERSIST-3)
export type BuildId = Brand<string, 'BuildId'>;      // '<commit>-<yyyymmdd>'
export type TabId = Brand<string, 'TabId'>;          // UUID v4 per page load
export type NonEmpty<T> = readonly [T, ...T[]];

export type Result<T, E> = { readonly ok: true; readonly value: T } | { readonly ok: false; readonly error: E };

export type Size = { readonly width: number; readonly height: number };              // integers >= 1
export type Rect = { readonly x: number; readonly y: number; readonly width: number; readonly height: number }; // master pixels
export type ImagePoint = { readonly x: number; readonly y: number };                  // frame fractions, (0,0) top-left
export type Oklab = { readonly L: number; readonly a: number; readonly b: number };
export type Oklch = { readonly l: number; readonly c: number; readonly h: number };  // h in degrees

export type PlaneFormat = 'u8' | 'f32';
export type PlaneRef = { readonly asset: AssetId; readonly format: PlaneFormat; readonly size: Size };  // raw little-endian, row-major, no header
export type ImageRef = { readonly asset: AssetId; readonly size: Size };            // PNG, sRGB, opaque
export type Plane<F extends PlaneFormat> = { readonly format: F; readonly size: Size; readonly data: F extends 'f32' ? Float32Array : Uint8Array };
export type Rgb8 = { readonly size: Size; readonly data: Uint8Array };              // 3 bytes per pixel
```

Planes are self-describing through `PlaneRef` (format and size live in the document), so no in-band header needs a version, and a new plane encoding is a new `PlaneFormat` member, never a rewrite of old bytes (STATE-1).

### 5.2 The scene document (`core/types.ts`, shape owned by `core/schema.ts`)

```ts
// core/migrate.ts owns the version: the table's length is the version, so a bump without a migration, or a migration without a bump, cannot be written (Q18, R6-DEF-6)
export type Migration = (json: unknown) => unknown;                       // total; version v to v + 1
export const MIGRATIONS = [] as const satisfies readonly Migration[];    // empty through development, so the version is 0 (Q18, settled); [identityOntoFrozen] at the freeze, then one entry per shape change (13.4 rule 4)
export const SCENE_VERSION = MIGRATIONS.length;                            // 0 now; typed as the tuple's literal length, so SceneDocument.version stays a literal type (VERIFIED under TypeScript 5.9.3, `experiments/version-literal/`, and under 7.0.2 by the interrogation's reviewers A and C, R7-21); 13.4 rule 1 accepts any non-negative integer (R3-AUDIT-5)

export type SceneDocument = {
  readonly format: 'noisy-scene';
  readonly version: typeof SCENE_VERSION;
  readonly id: SceneId;
  readonly title: string;                       // 1..80 chars, rendered as text only
  readonly createdAt: string;                   // ISO 8601 UTC
  readonly updatedAt: string;
  readonly seed: number;                        // uint32; grain and every deterministic noise
  readonly frame: Frame;
  readonly analysis: Analysis;
  readonly style: Style;
  readonly symbols: Symbols;
  readonly motion: Motion;
  readonly lighting: Lighting;
  readonly objects: readonly SceneObject[];     // creation order; at most 32; draw order is derived
  readonly lights: readonly Light[];            // at most 4
  readonly provenance: { readonly writer: BuildId; readonly importedOn: string };  // build id and user agent at import
};

export type Frame = {
  readonly source: SourceRef | null;            // the picked file's bytes, never decoded after import; null once the Library's Drop original has run (Q2)
  readonly master: ImageRef;                    // oriented sRGB PNG, long edge <= 2048, both sides even (section 12, R7-C); master.size is the frame coordinate system
  readonly original: { readonly name: string; readonly mediaType: string; readonly size: Size };
};
export type SourceRef = { readonly asset: AssetId; readonly mediaType: string; readonly byteLength: number };

export type Analysis = {                        // clip-wide first, then the one frame (a photo is a clip one frame long)
  readonly colors: NonEmpty<ColorCluster>;      // 1 to 16 k-means clusters in Oklab, weight-descending, weights sum to 1, every weight above 0; computed at import, fewer than 16 on a photo with fewer distinct colors (section 12, R6-2)
  readonly tone: ToneStats;                     // Oklab L percentiles of the master
  readonly depth: DepthProduct | null;          // null until produced
  readonly plate: PlateProduct | null;          // null until motion needs it
  readonly autoSubject: 'pending' | 'done';     // done whether or not an object was created (JUDGE-B-11); the need is never listed while objects exist (FRESH-11); the owner's first object sets it done through a product (R4-FRESH-11)
};
export type ColorCluster = { readonly lab: Oklab; readonly weight: number };
export type ToneStats = { readonly p01: number; readonly p05: number; readonly p50: number; readonly p95: number; readonly p99: number };
export type DepthProduct = {
  readonly model: ModelId;
  readonly map: PlaneRef;                       // f32 at the depth input size of the rule below (686 x 518 for 4:3, 1190 x 294 for 4:1), inverse depth normalized 0..1, larger is nearer
  readonly stats: DepthStats;                   // over the whole frame; the camera anchor when no subject exists
};
export type DepthStats = { readonly p10: number; readonly median: number; readonly p90: number };   // nearest-rank percentiles: the value at rank ceil(p n) in ascending order, so the median of an even count is the lower middle value, and fillHole's median and the depth stage's agree (R7-32)
export type PlateProduct = {
  readonly model: ModelId;
  readonly image: ImageRef;                     // master size PNG: the master with the union hole filled
  readonly forMasks: readonly AssetId[];        // sorted mask asset ids the fill was computed for
};

export type Style = {
  readonly fry: number;             // 0..1: the macro dial's last position; never read by the renderer (section 10)
  readonly colors: number;          // integer 2..12: palette size
  readonly saturation: number;      // 0..1: 0 keeps the clusters' chroma, 1 sits on the gamut cusp at the moved lightness, monotone between (section 10)
  readonly harmony: Harmony;
  readonly paletteTargets: readonly PaletteTarget[];   // sorted by cluster, unique clusters, at most 16: the owner's colors for palette entries (Q4 a, section 10 Palette targets)
  readonly bands: number;           // integer 2..6: tone bands per palette color, also the lighting bands
  readonly simplify: number;        // 0..1: simplification radius 0..24 master px, filtered on the quarter-size working grid (section 10)
  readonly outline: number;         // 0..1: width 0..6 master px and strength
  readonly subjectClarity: number;  // 0..1: how much of the fry subject objects are spared
  readonly grain: number;           // 0..1: luma sensor noise amplitude
  readonly colorNoise: number;      // 0..1: chroma noise amplitude
  readonly grainSize: number;       // integer 1..4 master px per grain cell
  readonly grainHold: number;       // integer 1..3 loop frames each grain frame is held (Q7: default 2)
  readonly crunch: number;          // 0..1: 8 x 8 DCT quantization strength
  readonly clipping: number;        // 0..1: highlight clipping knee
};
export type HarmonyTemplate = 'analogous' | 'complementary' | 'split-complementary' | 'triadic';
export type Harmony =
  | { readonly kind: 'off' }
  | { readonly kind: 'auto'; readonly strength: number }              // the template is chosen at resolve time by the rule of section 10 Harmony and is never stored (Q4 b)
  | { readonly kind: HarmonyTemplate; readonly strength: number };   // strength 0..1; the Advanced picker forces one template
export type PaletteTarget = { readonly cluster: number; readonly color: Oklch };   // cluster: an integer index into analysis.colors, 0 to colors.length - 1 (at most 15), stable across devices because the clusters are computed once at import (R6-2)

export type Symbols = {
  readonly enabled: boolean;        // on: one cell grid covers the frame
  readonly cellSize: number;        // even integer 6..48 master px
  readonly amount: number;          // 0..1: tone from the extremes inward
};
export type Motion = {
  readonly enabled: boolean;        // camera orbit on; also forced on by any object motion (FIT-9)
  readonly orbit: number;           // 0..1 of parallaxClamp
  readonly loopSeconds: number;     // 2..6; frameCount(motion, fps) = round(loopSeconds * fps): 30 fps for preview and MP4, the GIF's own rate for GIFs (AUDIT-12)
  readonly focus: number | null;    // disparity 0..1 that stays still; null = the subject's median, else the frame median
};
export type Lighting = {
  readonly ambient: number;         // 0..1: brightness kept where no light reaches
  readonly relief: number;          // 0.25..4: the height field's scale for the normals (9.2, section 10 Lighting); it moves no point, so distances, directions, and shadows do not depend on it (R7-2)
};

export type SceneObject = {
  readonly id: ObjectId;
  readonly name: string;
  readonly subject: boolean;                          // spared by subjectClarity; the camera anchor
  readonly recolor: Recolor;
  readonly symbols: 'slider' | 'always' | 'never';
  readonly motion: ObjectMotion;
  readonly thickness: number;                         // 0.05..2 of the object's height: shadow-casting depth
  readonly produced: ObjectProduced;                  // written only by analysis or by object.refine (JUDGE-B-6)
};
export type ObjectProduced = {
  readonly model: ModelId;
  readonly selection: Selection;                      // re-runnable on any device
  readonly mask: PlaneRef;                            // u8 soft mask, long edge 1024, refined against the master
  readonly bounds: Rect;                              // bbox of mask >= 128 in master pixels
  readonly depth: DepthStats | null;                  // under the mask; null until depth exists
  readonly color: ObjectColor;                        // clip-wide statistics under the mask (FRAME-1, JUDGE-A-9)
};
export type Selection =                               // re-runnable: the same points and the same candidate index reproduce the mask on any device
  | { readonly kind: 'taps'; readonly points: NonEmpty<TapPoint>; readonly candidate: number }
  | { readonly kind: 'auto-subject'; readonly seed: TapPoint; readonly candidate: number };   // candidate: which of the decoder's three masks, 0..2 in score order; 0 unless the owner cycled or an earlier candidate was empty (Q5 a, 7.1 item 2, R6-3); the automatic subject stores 0, and a cycle on it keeps the kind and the seed and stores the new index (R7-D5)
export type TapPoint = { readonly at: ImagePoint; readonly label: 'include' | 'exclude' };
export type ObjectColor = { readonly mean: Oklab; readonly clusters: NonEmpty<ColorCluster> };  // 2 or 3 clusters, weights sum to 1
export type Recolor = { readonly kind: 'none' } | { readonly kind: 'target'; readonly color: Oklch };
export type ObjectMotion = { readonly kind: 'none' } | { readonly kind: 'wobble' | 'bob' | 'sway'; readonly amount: number };   // amount 0..1

export type Light = {
  readonly id: LightId;
  readonly at: ImagePoint;          // x and y may lie in -0.5..1.5 to place a light off-frame
  readonly depth: number;           // 0..1 in disparity: 0 as far as the farthest point, 1 as near as the nearest
  readonly color: Oklch;            // c 0..0.25, h 0..360; the tint of section 10 reads c and h only, and l (0..1) is the picker swatch's own lightness (R6-OBS-2)
  readonly intensity: number;       // 0..2
  readonly radius: number;          // 0.05..2 of the long edge: falloff distance
  readonly rays: number;            // 0..1
  readonly shadows: boolean;
  readonly softness: number;        // 0..1
};
```

Invariants the type cannot express, checked by `parseDocument` and preserved by the reducer: `frame.master.size` has an even width and an even height (section 12; a document from another build or a hand-made fixture with an odd side fails `invalid`, R7-20); object and light ids unique; `forMasks` sorted; `focus` in range; `objects.length <= 32`, `lights.length <= 4`; `paletteTargets` sorted by cluster with unique integer clusters below `analysis.colors.length` (R6-2); `selection.candidate` in 0..2; every `AssetId` referenced resolves at the store or file boundary (checked there, not in `core`).

The depth input size is one pipeline rule, the same on every device (R5-FRESH-4).
The master's short side maps to 518 px and the long side follows the aspect, both rounded to multiples of the model's 14 px patch; when that is more than `DEPTH_MAX_TOKENS` = 1813 patches (37 x 49, the 518 x 686 input of a 4:3 master, the case every earlier measurement used), both sides are scaled by `sqrt(DEPTH_MAX_TOKENS / patches)` and rounded down to multiples of 14.
`analysis.depthInputSize(master.size)` is that rule and the depth map has exactly that size, so `DepthProduct.map.size` is defined for every aspect: 686 x 518 at 4:3, 784 x 434 at 16:9, 1190 x 294 at 4:1.

```ts
// core/schema.ts: the one parse chain the store and the scene file share (13.4)
export type ParseError =
  | { readonly kind: 'not-a-scene' }
  | { readonly kind: 'newer-version'; readonly version: number; readonly writer: BuildId | null }
  | { readonly kind: 'invalid'; readonly detail: string };            // a failed migration, a missing or invalid required field, or unknown keys, whose paths the detail lists (13.4 rule 5)
export function peekVersion(json: unknown): Result<{ readonly version: number; readonly writer: BuildId | null }, { readonly kind: 'not-a-scene' }>;
/** Pure factory: the parse chain for a given migration table, whose length is the version it parses to; `newer-version` and the strict schema's version literal both come from that length. */
export function parseDocumentWith<const M extends readonly Migration[]>(migrations: M): (json: unknown) => Result<Omit<SceneDocument, 'version'> & { readonly version: M['length'] }, ParseError>;
export const parseDocument: (json: unknown) => Result<SceneDocument, ParseError> = parseDocumentWith(MIGRATIONS);   // peek, migrate, strict parse; an unknown key fails the parse and nothing is dropped; the store, the codec, and the session import only this bound form, and the node test of 13.4 rule 4 passes the synthetic chain to the factory directly (R6-4)
```

### 5.3 Edits, products, the reducer, and undo (`core/edits.ts`, `core/reduce.ts`, `core/history.ts`)

```ts
export type Edit =                                                     // user actions; undoable
  | { readonly kind: 'title'; readonly title: string }
  | { readonly kind: 'style'; readonly patch: Partial<Omit<Style, 'paletteTargets'>> }   // may carry fry so a fry inverse is one edit; the renderer never reads fry (AUDIT-3); palette targets have their own edit so each swatch coalesces on its own
  | { readonly kind: 'fry'; readonly fry: number }                     // macro: rewrites the fried fields through fryCurve (section 10)
  | { readonly kind: 'symbols'; readonly patch: Partial<Symbols> }
  | { readonly kind: 'motion'; readonly patch: Partial<Motion> }
  | { readonly kind: 'lighting'; readonly patch: Partial<Lighting> }
  | { readonly kind: 'palette.target'; readonly cluster: number; readonly color: Oklch | null }   // sets or clears one palette target (Q4 a); its inverse carries the previous value; a `style` patch never carries paletteTargets
  | { readonly kind: 'object.add'; readonly object: SceneObject }      // from a tap; its mask asset is already stored
  | { readonly kind: 'object.set'; readonly id: ObjectId; readonly patch: Partial<Omit<SceneObject, 'id' | 'produced'>> }
  | { readonly kind: 'object.refine'; readonly id: ObjectId; readonly produced: ObjectProduced }   // selection and mask together (JUDGE-B-7)
  | { readonly kind: 'object.remove'; readonly id: ObjectId }
  | { readonly kind: 'light.add'; readonly light: Light }
  | { readonly kind: 'light.set'; readonly id: LightId; readonly patch: Partial<Omit<Light, 'id'>> }
  | { readonly kind: 'light.remove'; readonly id: LightId };

export type Produce =                                                  // analysis results; applied by the driver, never on the undo stack
  | { readonly kind: 'depth'; readonly depth: DepthProduct }
  | { readonly kind: 'plate'; readonly plate: PlateProduct }
  | { readonly kind: 'object.depth'; readonly id: ObjectId; readonly depth: DepthStats }   // state-based: whenever analysis.depth exists, the driver applies one for each object whose produced.depth is null, at open and after every edit or product it applies (R7-D8)
  | { readonly kind: 'auto-subject'; readonly found: SceneObject | null };                  // sets autoSubject done; adds the object with subject true; null records that no guess is wanted, which the driver also applies when the owner's first object lands (R4-FRESH-11)

/** Pure. Clamps and coerces every number through core/ranges.ts, keeps the invariants of 5.2, stamps updatedAt, and returns the inverse edit list: applied in order to the result, it restores the input document except updatedAt. */
export function applyEdit(doc: SceneDocument, edit: Edit, now: string): { readonly doc: SceneDocument; readonly inverse: NonEmpty<Edit> };
/** Pure. Folds applyEdit over a list; the kernel uses it to apply an undo or redo entry as one step. */
export function applyEdits(doc: SceneDocument, edits: NonEmpty<Edit>, now: string): { readonly doc: SceneDocument; readonly inverse: NonEmpty<Edit> };
/** Pure. Products never fail validation because the driver built them from typed replies; stamps updatedAt; a product the document has moved past is a no-op (rules below). */
export function applyProduce(doc: SceneDocument, p: Produce, now: string): SceneDocument;
/** Coalescing key: 'style:saturation', 'fry', 'palette.target:<cluster>', 'object.set:<id>:recolor', 'light.set:<id>'; consecutive edits with one key within 600 ms form one undo step. */
export function coalesceKey(edit: Edit): string;
/** Pure. Q2's "Drop original": frame.source becomes null and updatedAt is stamped. The Library applies it to a closed scene (5.9); it is not an Edit, so it has no inverse and enters no history (R5-FRESH-7). */
export function dropSource(doc: SceneDocument, now: string): SceneDocument;

export type History = { readonly undo: readonly HistoryEntry[]; readonly redo: readonly HistoryEntry[] };   // bounded at 50 entries
export type HistoryEntry = { readonly key: string; readonly at: number; readonly forward: NonEmpty<Edit>; readonly inverse: NonEmpty<Edit> };   // a coalesced entry keeps the first inverse and the last forward
export function push(history: History, entry: HistoryEntry): History;
export function referencedAssets(history: History): ReadonlySet<AssetId>;   // masks inside object.add, object.remove inverses, and object.refine on either stack
```

Rules the reducer enforces:

- Patches only touch user fields; `produced` changes only through `Produce` or `object.refine`, so a slider moved during an in-flight refine can never revert a mask (JUDGE-B-6).
- Undo applies an inverse edit list and touches no analysis product; a removed object comes back with its `produced` intact because the inverse `object.add` carries it, its mask asset is still stored because nothing is swept while a scene is open (13.2), and its bytes are still in the asset cache because the history references them (FIT-5, JUDGE-A-3, JUDGE-B-7).
  Undo is Cmd-Z on the Mac and a button on the phone; the history is never persisted (Q16, settled: undo stays).
- Every side effect of an edit is captured by a trailing inverse edit, applied after the primary inverse so the last edit wins (AUDIT-3): setting an object's motion to a non-`none` kind sets `motion.enabled` to `true` (FIT-9), so its inverse is `[object.set { motion }, motion { enabled }]`; the first `object.add` when no subject exists marks its object `subject: true` (Q5 b, settled; reversible in the Objects tab), so its inverse is `[object.remove]`; `palette.target` replaces one entry of `paletteTargets`, keeping the list sorted, so its inverse is one `palette.target` with the previous color or null; `fry` rewrites `colors`, `saturation`, `simplify`, `bands`, `grain`, `colorNoise`, `crunch`, `clipping` through `fryCurve(fry)` and stores `fry`, so its inverse is one `style` patch carrying the nine previous values.
  `object.remove` has no side effect, so its inverse is `[object.add]`: round 7 deleted the rule that reset a `motion.focus` derived from the removed subject, because `focus` is a bare disparity, the document records no derivation (the only test would have been float equality with a median that a refine can move), no control sets focus from an object, and a stored disparity stays meaningful after its object is gone (R7-D6).
  P2a's round-trip test applies every fixture edit, then its inverse list, and asserts the document is restored except `updatedAt`.
- `palette.target` with a cluster that is not an integer, is below 0, or is at or above `analysis.colors.length` changes nothing (a photo with fewer than 16 distinct colors has fewer clusters, section 12, R6-2), and one whose color is out of range is clamped like any number; `object.refine` with a `selection.candidate` outside 0..2 changes nothing (Q4 a, Q5 a).
- Products the document has moved past are no-ops (FRESH-11): `auto-subject` is dropped, with `autoSubject` still set to `done`, when any object exists or `autoSubject` is already `done`, so a guess that lands after the owner's first tap never makes a second subject or an overlapping duplicate; `object.depth` for an unknown id changes nothing; `plate` whose `forMasks` differs from the current sorted mask set changes nothing (section 15); `depth` replaces an existing product.
  Edits never touch `analysis` fields, so the inverse round-trip stays exact; the matching scheduling rule is in `reconcile` (5.4), which never lists `auto-subject` while objects exist.
  The owner's first object closes the question for good (R4-FRESH-11): right after the first user-created `object.add` applies while `autoSubject` is `pending`, the driver applies `Produce { kind: 'auto-subject', found: null }`, which sets `done` and adds nothing.
  It is a product, not an edit's side effect, so the round-trip stays exact and nothing enters the undo stack; without it `autoSubject` stayed `pending` whenever the owner's tap beat the guess, and removing or undoing that object made a guessed subject appear that no tap had made.
  P2a tests each rule.
- Per-object depth statistics are state, not an event (R7-D8): whenever `analysis.depth` exists, the driver applies `object.depth` for every object whose `produced.depth` is null, computed from the depth map under that object's mask, at open and again after every edit or product it applies.
  Round 6 applied `object.depth` only when depth landed after a mask, so a tap decoded during a depth retry (rule 1 of 7.1 lets one decode beside a depth-only sequence) whose `object.add` applied just after depth landed kept `produced.depth` null for good: it sorted nearest in `layers()`, and as the subject it sent `anchorDisparity` to the frame median, and neither a reopen nor `reconcile` repaired it.
  The rule is the driver's, so the reducer is unchanged: an `object.depth` for an unknown id still changes nothing, and one for an object that already has statistics replaces them.

### 5.4 Derivations and reconcile (`core/derive.ts`)

```ts
export type Layer = { readonly kind: 'plate' } | { readonly kind: 'object'; readonly object: SceneObject };
/** Plate first, then objects far to near by produced.depth.median (null sorts nearest); an object whose bounds lie inside another's by more than half its area draws after it. */
export function layers(doc: SceneDocument): NonEmpty<Layer>;
/** The label an (x, y) frame point belongs to: the last layer in layers() whose mask >= 0.5 there, else the plate. The renderer's id buffer, the hit test, and label-aware simplification all use this rule.
 *  A mask's value at a frame point (u, v) is its bilinear interpolation at texel centers: for a w x h mask the point lies at (u w - 0.5, v h - 0.5) in texel coordinates, the four nearest texels, clamped to the edge, are weighted bilinearly, and the value is compared with 0.5, that is 128 of 255 (R7-6).
 *  Pass 1's alpha test reads the same value through a LINEAR sampler with CLAMP_TO_EDGE, which the soft edges need for coverage; GPU filtering is not bit-exact, so within one mask texel of a 0.5 crossing the GPU may pick either of the two layers this rule gives on either side (9.3). */
export function labelAt(doc: SceneDocument, masks: ReadonlyMap<ObjectId, Plane<'u8'>>, at: ImagePoint): ObjectId | null;
export function holeMask(doc: SceneDocument, masks: ReadonlyMap<ObjectId, Plane<'u8'>>, size: Size): Plane<'u8'>;   // union at >= 0.5 by labelAt's sampling rule at each output texel's center, dilated by 1% of the long edge
export function plateNeeded(doc: SceneDocument): boolean;    // objects non-empty, and (effectiveOrbit(motion) > 0 or any object motion), and sorted mask ids !== plate.forMasks; a loop at orbit 0 with no object motion moves no layer under the identity camera, so it reveals no hole and needs no plate (R6-8, R7-B7)
export function frameCount(motion: Motion, fps: number): number;   // round(loopSeconds * fps); every periodic term is an integer number of cycles over this count, so a loop closes at any fps (AUDIT-12, FRESH-10)
export function parallaxClamp(doc: SceneDocument): number;   // largest orbit offset (frame fractions) so no displacement exceeds PARALLAX_MARGIN per side
export function objectMotionBound(doc: SceneDocument, id: ObjectId): { readonly x: number; readonly y: number };   // amount scaled toward 0 as bounds approach the frame edge (JUDGE-A-16)
export function anchorDisparity(doc: SceneDocument): number | null;   // motion.focus, else the subject's depth median, else depth.stats.median, else null
export function assetRefs(doc: SceneDocument): ReadonlySet<AssetId>;   // every asset the document references: source, master, depth map, plate, masks; the store's sweep and dangling check, the file manifest, and the asset cache read it

export type AnalysisOp = 'depth' | 'encode' | 'decode' | 'inpaint';
export type Intent = { readonly objectsActive: boolean; readonly tapPending: boolean };   // objectsActive: the UI's, through setIntent; tapPending: the driver's own, true while a tap's promise waits on missing embeddings (5.9, R7-D4)
export type CacheIndex = { readonly embeddings: boolean };   // whether the driver holds embeddings for frame.master.asset, in memory or in this device's cache
export type Need =
  | { readonly kind: 'depth' }
  | { readonly kind: 'encode' }                              // tool cache, not a document product
  | { readonly kind: 'auto-subject' }
  | { readonly kind: 'plate'; readonly forMasks: readonly AssetId[] };
/** Pure, clockless. Lists what is missing or stale, in order, excluding needs whose op is blocked. Timing (debounce, cooldown, visibility) belongs to the driver. */
export function reconcile(doc: SceneDocument, cache: CacheIndex, blocked: ReadonlySet<AnalysisOp>, intent: Intent): readonly Need[];
export const NEED_OPS: Readonly<Record<Need['kind'], readonly AnalysisOp[]>>;   // depth:[depth], encode:[encode], auto-subject:[encode, decode], plate:[inpaint] (STATE-3, ENG-8 b)

// core/motion.ts: the loop crop is written here and nowhere else (R3-AUDIT-6)
export const PARALLAX_MARGIN = 0.02;                         // frame fraction hidden on each side of a loop: the owner's "about 4%" crop
export const LOOP_ZOOM = 1 / (1 - 2 * PARALLAX_MARGIN);      // 1.0417: a loop with camera orbit shows the central 96% per axis, so a displacement of PARALLAX_MARGIN never uncovers the frame edge
export function effectiveOrbit(motion: Motion): number;      // motion.enabled ? motion.orbit : 0; the loop camera is LOOP_ZOOM about the frame center when this is above 0 and the identity when it is 0, so a loop without camera orbit keeps the still's framing (Q19 a)
```

Order and conditions (R4-FRESH-1).
`reconcile` returns a plan in dependency order, `depth`, `encode`, `auto-subject`, `plate`, in which every listed need's inputs either exist already or are listed before it:

- `depth` when `analysis.depth` is null.
- The wish for a guess is kept apart from whether the guess can run yet: `subjectWanted` is `autoSubject` pending, `objects` empty, and depth either present or listed.
- `encode` when the cache lacks embeddings and (`subjectWanted`, or `intent.objectsActive`, or `intent.tapPending`).
- `auto-subject` when `subjectWanted` and (embeddings exist or `encode` is listed).
- `plate` when `plateNeeded(doc)`.

A need whose ops include a blocked op is omitted, and "listed" above means listed after that omission, so a blocked or failed `depth` unlists the guess and the encoder run that existed only for it; the driver shows the op as `blocked-after-crashes` or `failed`.
A freshly imported document therefore yields `[depth, encode, auto-subject]`: the driver runs the heavy prefix `[depth, encode]` as one sequence in one worker (7.1) and performs `auto-subject` through the decoder once that sequence has ended and depth exists.
The round-3 text listed `auto-subject` only once depth existed and `encode` only when `auto-subject` was listed, two conditions that referred to each other and that returned `[depth]` alone for a fresh import, which split every import into two workers with a cooldown between them.
Without depth there is no automatic subject: `subjectWanted` is false, neither `encode` nor `auto-subject` is listed until the owner shows intent, `autoSubject` stays `pending`, and the owner's first tap defines the subject (R3-FRESH-1).
The owner's first object ends the guess for good: `auto-subject` requires an empty object list, a guess already decoding when the tap lands is dropped by the reducer rule in 5.3 (FRESH-11), and the driver's `found: null` product then sets `autoSubject` to `done`, so deleting or undoing that object never lists the need again (R4-FRESH-11).

### 5.5 Analysis service and worker protocol (`analysis/service.ts`, `analysis/protocol.ts`)

```ts
export interface AnalysisService {
  /** Runs an ordered list of heavy stages in one sequence worker, posting each product as it lands; the worker is terminated when the list ends, on cancel, or on failure. */
  runSequence(scene: SceneId, stages: NonEmpty<HeavyStage>, input: SequenceInput, on: SequenceListener, signal: AbortSignal): void;
  /** Spawns the resident decode worker from embeddings and a guide the caller supplies, terminating any decoder already alive. Both are copied to the worker; the service keeps neither. The handle is the only way to decode, so a decode without embeddings cannot be written (R3-AUDIT-2). */
  openDecoder(input: DecoderInput): Decoder;
  cancelHeavy(): void;                                     // terminates the sequence worker at once (hidden, close)
  fetchModels(ops: readonly AnalysisOp[], signal: AbortSignal, onProgress: (model: ModelId, fraction: number) => void): Promise<Result<void, AnalysisError>>;   // outside any job clock; 'decode' fetches the decoder's model before openDecoder
  readonly alive: { readonly heavy: boolean; readonly decoder: boolean };
  /** The last sequence worker this page terminated after it had run at least one stage; null before that. One service exists per page, so a driver composed for the next scene reads the termination its predecessor caused (7.1 rule 3, R4-FRESH-4). */
  readonly lastHeavyTermination: HeavyTermination | null;
}
export type HeavyTermination = { readonly atMs: number; readonly largest: HeavyStage['kind'] };   // clock time of terminate(); the largest stage that worker ran
```
