```ts
export function createAnalysisService(deps: { readonly clock: Clock; readonly diag: DiagnosticSink<AnalysisEvent> }): AnalysisService;   // called once per page by the composition root; AnalysisEvent is this module's part of the event vocabulary (5.10, R7-B3)
export type DecoderInput = { readonly scene: SceneId; readonly embeddings: SamEmbeddings; readonly guide: Rgb8 };   // guide = the master at long edge 1024, from the session's asset cache
export interface Decoder {
  readonly scene: SceneId;
  /** Runs one decode, waiting for the worker's init first; an init failure is this call's `worker` error. After release() it resolves `cancelled`. */
  decode(points: NonEmpty<TapPoint>, signal: AbortSignal): Promise<Result<DecodeResult, AnalysisError>>;
  release(): void;                                         // terminates the worker; idempotent (Objects tab left, idle, an on-time hidden grace, an encoder-bearing sequence, after the automatic subject's decode unless the tab is active or a tap is pending, an export leaving its wait, close; 7.1 item 2, section 14 rule 3, R6-DEF-7)
}
export type HeavyStage =
  | { readonly kind: 'depth' }
  | { readonly kind: 'encode' }
  | { readonly kind: 'inpaint'; readonly hole: Plane<'u8'> };
export type SequenceInput = { readonly master: () => Promise<ImageBitmap> };   // a fresh bitmap per stage, transferred to the worker
export const DEPTH_MAX_TOKENS = 1813;                       // 37 x 49 patches of 14 px: the 518 x 686 input of a 4:3 master (5.2, R5-FRESH-4)
export function depthInputSize(master: Size): Size;        // pure: the depth stage's input size and therefore the depth map's (5.2)
export type SequenceListener = {
  readonly onPhase: (stage: HeavyStage['kind'], phase: 'session' | 'infer', fraction: number | null) => void;
  readonly onDepth: (map: Plane<'f32'>, stats: DepthStats) => void;
  readonly onEmbeddings: (embeddings: SamEmbeddings) => void;   // the driver keeps them for the open scene and mirrors them to store.cache; the service holds nothing beyond the live worker (AUDIT-8)
  readonly onPlate: (image: Rgb8) => void;
  readonly onFailed: (stage: HeavyStage['kind'], error: AnalysisError) => void;
  readonly onDone: () => void;
};
export type DecodeResult = { readonly candidates: readonly [Candidate, Candidate, Candidate] };   // SAM's three masks in score order, each refined at 1024; a tap takes the first non-empty one and the automatic subject index 0, and the driver keeps the last result so a repeated tap can cycle (Q5 a); a candidate may be empty, no texel at or above 128, and the driver's rule for that is in 7.1 item 2 (R6-3)
export type Candidate = { readonly mask: Plane<'u8'>; readonly score: number };
export type AnalysisError =                                          // declared in analysis/protocol.ts, which P1.1 builds with the model loader that returns the download and integrity variants, and re-exported here as part of the module's public face (R7-10)
  | { readonly kind: 'download'; readonly model: ModelId; readonly status: number | 'offline' | 'stalled' }
  | { readonly kind: 'integrity'; readonly model: ModelId }
  | { readonly kind: 'timeout'; readonly op: AnalysisOp; readonly visibleMs: number }
  | { readonly kind: 'worker'; readonly op: AnalysisOp; readonly message: string }
  | { readonly kind: 'quota' }
  | { readonly kind: 'cancelled' };                                  // no 'waiting' here: without embeddings no Decoder exists, and a tap waits in the driver with its promise pending (5.9, R7-D4)

// analysis/protocol.ts, private to the module; every message is parsed on receipt because a worker is a boundary
export type SequenceRequest =
  | { readonly type: 'init'; readonly ortBase: string; readonly threads: number | 'default' }
  | { readonly type: 'run'; readonly stage: 'depth' | 'encode'; readonly image: ImageBitmap }
  | { readonly type: 'run'; readonly stage: 'inpaint'; readonly image: ImageBitmap; readonly hole: Plane<'u8'> }
  | { readonly type: 'end' };                                        // release the last session and self-close
export type SequenceReply =
  | { readonly type: 'phase'; readonly stage: HeavyStage['kind']; readonly phase: 'session' | 'infer'; readonly fraction: number | null }
  | { readonly type: 'depth'; readonly map: Plane<'f32'>; readonly stats: DepthStats; readonly timings: StageTimings }
  | { readonly type: 'embeddings'; readonly embeddings: SamEmbeddings; readonly timings: StageTimings }
  | { readonly type: 'plate'; readonly image: Rgb8; readonly timings: StageTimings }
  | { readonly type: 'failed'; readonly stage: HeavyStage['kind']; readonly message: string };
export type DecodeRequest =
  | { readonly type: 'init'; readonly ortBase: string; readonly embeddings: SamEmbeddings; readonly guide: Rgb8 }   // guide = master at long edge 1024
  | { readonly type: 'decode'; readonly jobId: number; readonly points: NonEmpty<TapPoint> };
export type DecodeReply =
  | { readonly type: 'decoded'; readonly jobId: number; readonly result: DecodeResult; readonly timings: StageTimings }
  | { readonly type: 'failed'; readonly jobId: number; readonly message: string };
export type StageTimings = { readonly sessionMs: number; readonly inferMs: number; readonly wasmHighWaterBytes: number };
export type SamEmbeddings = { readonly model: ModelId; readonly tensors: readonly [SamTensor, SamTensor, SamTensor] };
export type SamTensor = { readonly name: string; readonly dims: readonly number[]; readonly data: Float32Array };
```

The sequence worker loads ORT once, creates and releases one session per stage, reports its wasm high-water mark per stage (the `WebAssembly.Memory` wrapper from `experiments/phone-lens/worker.js`), and closes itself on `end`; the service also calls `terminate()` so nested pthread workers end even if the self-close hangs (CLAIM-2).
The auto-subject heuristic is pure, tested under node, and composed by the driver from one decode's first candidate, which the product stores with `candidate` 0; it needs depth, and nothing seeds a subject without it (R3-FRESH-1).
Its rule is written here because Q6's keep-or-drop call (U15) measures exactly this heuristic, so a failed U15 means this rule failed (R7-D2):

```ts
// analysis/subject.ts
export function seedFromDepth(depth: Plane<'f32'>): TapPoint;   // the seed rule below, at the depth map's own resolution; always an 'include' point at the center of one texel of the depth map (R7-18)
export function acceptSubject(candidate: Candidate): boolean;   // the gate below, on the candidate's own 1024 px mask; round 6's size argument is gone, since the mask carries its size
```

- **The seed.** On the depth map (inverse depth, larger is nearer, 5.2) of `w x h` texels, the **near set** is every texel whose value is at or above the value at index `floor(0.85 n)` of the `n` values sorted ascending, the nearest 15% or a little more where values tie.
  The set is eroded by a 7 x 7 square, 3 texels each way, with texels outside the map counting as outside the set; when the erosion leaves no texel, the uneroded set is used, so a thin near object can still seed.
  Of what remains, the largest 4-connected component by texel count is taken, a tie going to the component whose first texel in row-major order comes first.
  The component's centroid `c` is the mean of its texel centers, texel `(x, y)` having its center at `(x + 0.5, y + 0.5)`, and the texel holding it is `(floor(c.x), floor(c.y))`.
  The seed is the center of that texel when it belongs to the component, and otherwise the center of the component's texel whose center is nearest `c` (Euclidean, ties in row-major order), so a crescent or a ring still seeds inside itself; either way the seed is a texel's center and never the centroid itself, so both branches return the same kind of point and a node row can assert it exactly, and it is returned in frame fractions, `((x + 0.5) / w, (y + 0.5) / h)` for that texel's integer `(x, y)` (R7-18).
  This is the challenger's rule (`design-challenger.md` section 6.4), which the synthesis dropped without saying so.
- **The gate.** `acceptSubject` accepts the candidate when its coverage, the share of its 1024 px mask's texels at or above 128, is at least 2% and at most 60%, and it touches at most two of the four frame edges, where a mask **touches** an edge when a texel at or above 128 lies in that edge's outermost row or column of the mask.
  An empty candidate has coverage 0 and is rejected, so the tap rule of 7.1 item 2 does not apply to it (R6-3).
  No SAM score threshold is part of the gate, and section 15 no longer says "score": no measurement supports a value (the challenger's 0.7 was INFERRED), and an unmeasured constant would change what U15 judges; P4.2's ten-photo row records candidate 0's score beside the owner's verdict on each photo, so a threshold can be added from evidence if the wrong guesses turn out to be the low-scoring ones.

Known-answer checks (`analysis/checks.ts`) port the bench's mask IoU, depth gap, and inpaint outside-unchanged rules (TEST-5).

### 5.6 Renderer inputs (`render/types.ts`)

```ts
export type FrameAssets = {
  readonly master: { readonly id: AssetId; readonly bitmap: ImageBitmap };
  readonly depth: { readonly id: AssetId; readonly plane: Plane<'f32'> } | null;
  readonly plate: { readonly id: AssetId; readonly bitmap: ImageBitmap } | null;
  readonly masks: readonly { readonly object: ObjectId; readonly id: AssetId; readonly plane: Plane<'u8'> }[];
};
/** The session's projection of the document: everything the look needs and nothing about persistence. */
export type RenderScene = {
  readonly size: Size;                                   // master size, the canonical grid
  readonly seed: number;
  readonly style: Omit<Style, 'fry'>;
  readonly symbols: Symbols;
  readonly motion: Motion & { readonly clamp: number; readonly anchor: number | null };
  readonly lighting: Lighting;
  readonly palette: ResolvedPalette;                     // from core/color.ts, pure (section 10)
  readonly layers: NonEmpty<RenderLayer>;                // from derive.layers, with per-object stats and bounds
  readonly lights: readonly Light[];
};
export type RenderLayer =
  | { readonly kind: 'plate' }
  | { readonly kind: 'object'; readonly id: ObjectId; readonly subject: boolean; readonly recolor: Recolor; readonly symbols: SceneObject['symbols']; readonly motion: ObjectMotion; readonly motionBound: { x: number; y: number }; readonly thickness: number; readonly bounds: Rect; readonly depth: DepthStats | null; readonly color: ObjectColor };
export type RenderRequest = {
  readonly scene: RenderScene;
  readonly time: { readonly kind: 'still' } | { readonly kind: 'loop'; readonly frame: number; readonly frameCount: number };   // frame may equal frameCount (phase 1.0) for the closure test (TEST-9); the loop's LOOP_ZOOM is a camera parameter of the geometry pass, applied when the scene's effective orbit is above 0 (section 9.3, Q19)
  readonly output: RenderOutput;
};
export type RenderOutput =
  | { readonly kind: 'master' }                          // preview, PNG, MP4: the canvas at master size, Present is a blit
  | { readonly kind: 'gif'; readonly size: Size };       // box-downsampled after pass 5, then the camera stage on the GIF grid (Q8, settled: 480 px long edge by default)
export type RenderMode =
  | { readonly kind: 'full'; readonly divisor: 1 | 2 }   // divisor 2 is the integer-divisor preview fallback (section 8.2); export always renders divisor 1
  | { readonly kind: 'low' };                            // passes 3, 6 without crunch, and 7, from the master (section 9.3); a phone policy from the moment an encoder run is planned until its sequence ends
export interface Renderer {                              // one per page, created by the app shell at boot; sessions load and unload scenes (9.1, R4-FRESH-13)
  readonly canvas: HTMLCanvasElement;                    // the shell's canvas, mounted by the Editor screen; 1 x 1 while no scene is loaded
  load(assets: FrameAssets): void;                       // sets the scene's assets: uploads and derives refined depth, plate depth (its hole filled on the CPU in f32), the two R32F depth textures the normals pass reads (9.2, R7-1), and the mask array. Called at open and again whenever an asset changes (depth landing, a mask, a plate): everything is keyed by asset id (9.2), so a repeat call uploads and rebuilds only what changed (R5-FRESH-7); from P6a.1, where the CPU fill lands, a change of the depth, or of the masks while depth exists, also refills the hole and uploads the filled background depth, one R32F texture at depthInputSize, and nothing else beside the changed asset, and a plate change uploads the plate alone (R7-29); each refill logs render.derived with the fill's time (R7-23). The label texture, the normals (whose plane fit is label-aware, R7-1), and the simplify cache depend on layer order too, so render rebuilds them when the request's ordered list of (object id, mask asset id) differs from the one they were built for (9.2, R7-D7); every rebuild of the normals, for a changed asset or a changed order, runs inside the first full-mode render after the change, before pass 1, and reports its time as that render's normals entry (9.2, R7-23)
  unload(): void;                                        // drops every scene texture and target and keeps the programs; the session calls it on close
  render(request: RenderRequest): void;                  // draws into the canvas; the still and a loop at effective orbit 0 use the identity camera, a loop with orbit the LOOP_ZOOM camera (section 9, Q19)
  read(): Promise<Result<ImageData, RendererError>>;     // the last rendered frame
  setMode(mode: RenderMode): void;                       // section 8
  resident(): ResidentReport;                            // bytes by format and size, for tests and diagnostics (TEST-8); the canvas back buffer under its own key, canvas (R7-27)
  readonly report: GlReport;                             // what createRenderer found on this context (9.1), for the environment snapshot (5.10, R7-F1)
  readonly status: RendererStatus;
  onStatus(listener: (status: RendererStatus) => void): () => void;
  dispose(): void;                                       // page teardown and tests only
}
export type RendererStatus = { readonly kind: 'ready' } | { readonly kind: 'recovering'; readonly losses: number } | { readonly kind: 'failed'; readonly reason: string };
export type RendererError = { readonly kind: 'context-lost' } | { readonly kind: 'out-of-memory' } | { readonly kind: 'failed'; readonly reason: string };
export type ResidentReport = { readonly totalBytes: number; readonly byKey: readonly { readonly key: string; readonly bytes: number }[] };   // one entry per pool key, written `<format> <width>x<height>` (an array texture adds x<layers>, so `R8 1024x768x32`), holding the bytes of every texture and target under it, so `RGBA8 2048x1536` holds the master-size RGBA8 targets alone; and one entry `canvas` for the canvas back buffer, width x height x 4 bytes of the drawing buffer, which no pool key holds; totalBytes is the sum of every entry, the back buffer included, and the compositor's copies are not the renderer's to see (8.2, R7-27)
export type Capabilities = {                             // the requirement table of 9.1 (R7-F1)
  readonly required: readonly (readonly [string, ...string[]])[];   // each entry is met when getExtension returns non-null for one of its names, tried in order; the names it enables are the ones the renderer uses
  readonly optional: readonly string[];                  // enabled when present, never needed for correctness
  readonly renderable: readonly { readonly name: string; readonly attachments: readonly ('RGBA8' | 'R16F' | 'RG16F' | 'RGBA16F')[] }[];   // framebuffers that must be FRAMEBUFFER_COMPLETE at boot
  readonly limits: Readonly<Record<string, number>>;
};
export type GlReport = {                                 // what createRenderer found on this context: the environment snapshot's webgl2 field and R4's fixtures (5.10, R7-F1)
  readonly extensions: readonly string[];                // getSupportedExtensions()
  readonly enabled: readonly string[];                   // the required and optional names getExtension returned non-null for
  readonly complete: Readonly<Record<string, boolean>>;  // each renderable check by name
  readonly readFormat: { readonly format: number; readonly type: number } | null;   // IMPLEMENTATION_COLOR_READ_FORMAT and _TYPE with an R16F attachment bound: recorded, never relied on
  readonly limits: Readonly<Record<string, number>>;     // the context's value for every limit the table names
};
export function createRenderer(canvas: HTMLCanvasElement, diag: DiagnosticSink<RenderEvent>): Result<Renderer, { readonly kind: 'no-webgl2' | 'missing-extension' | 'limit' | 'incomplete'; readonly detail: string; readonly report: GlReport | null }>;   // called once per page by the composition root; compiles every program, the low-mode ones first; report is null only without a WebGL2 context
export const CAPABILITIES: Capabilities;                 // section 9.1
export function resolveStyle(scene: RenderScene): StyleUniforms;   // pure TypeScript, tested under node (TEST-3); includes the per-id table of 9.3
export function surfaceNormal(slope: { readonly x: number; readonly y: number }, relief: number): { readonly x: number; readonly y: number; readonly z: number };   // pure reference of the normal rule of section 10 Lighting: slopes in disparity per long edge; the node row of P7.1 and the tiles fixture's generator use it (R7-D1)
export function surfaceSlope(depth: Plane<'f32'>, master: Size, at: ImagePoint, counts: (x: number, y: number) => boolean): { readonly x: number; readonly y: number } | null;   // pure reference of the slope rule of 9.2 (R7-1): the weighted least-squares plane through the texels of the 7 x 7 window of `depth` around the frame point `at` that `counts` accepts (for the surface pair the texels carrying the point's label, for the background pair every texel), placed in the lighting space of `master`, so the slopes are in disparity per long edge at any plane size; null where the rule gives the flat normal; the node rows of G12 and P7.1 and the oracle of their browser rows use it
export function lightDirection(point: { readonly at: ImagePoint; readonly disparity: number }, light: Pick<Light, 'at' | 'depth'>, size: Size): { readonly x: number; readonly y: number; readonly z: number };   // unit vector from a frame point to a light in the lighting space of section 10, under every camera, since lighting is measured at each fragment's frame point and the camera moves nothing (R7-D1, R7-14)
// core/color.ts: ResolvedPalette lists the entries in order with, per entry, either the source clusters it merged and the target in effect if any (the lowest-numbered targeted cluster's, section 10 Palette targets) or, for a pinned entry, the object it belongs to (R6-2), plus its resolved Oklch and its `bands` variants; it names the harmony template it used (`template: HarmonyTemplate | null`, null when harmony is off); the renderer's LUT, the swatch strip, and the Advanced picker's "Auto (triadic)" label all read it from SessionState.palette (Q4)
```

### 5.7 Store (`store/`)

```ts
export type StoreError =
  | { readonly kind: 'quota' }
  | { readonly kind: 'missing' }
  | { readonly kind: 'damaged'; readonly detail: string }
  | { readonly kind: 'newer-version'; readonly version: number; readonly writer: BuildId | null }
  | { readonly kind: 'unavailable'; readonly detail: string }     // connection lost twice (PHONE-4)
  | { readonly kind: 'io'; readonly detail: string };
export type SceneSummary = {
  readonly id: SceneId; readonly title: string; readonly updatedAt: string; readonly thumbnail: Blob | null; readonly byteLength: number;   // thumbnail: a field of the record, null only in a record another build wrote without one (R4-FRESH-7)
  readonly lastExportedAt: string | null;
  readonly sourceBytes: number | null;                               // frame.source.byteLength, null once dropped or in a record that does not parse; the Library sums it into the storage line's "of which originals" (Q2)
  readonly state: { readonly kind: 'ok' } | { readonly kind: 'damaged'; readonly detail: string } | { readonly kind: 'newer'; readonly version: number; readonly writer: BuildId | null };   // damaged covers unknown keys at a known version, whose paths the detail lists (13.4 rule 5)
};
export interface SceneStore {                                        // trusts its caller to hold the scene lock for writes (section 13)
  list(): Promise<readonly SceneSummary[]>;                          // never throws; peeks version, then runs the strict parse that tells ok from damaged
  load(id: SceneId): Promise<Result<SceneDocument, StoreError>>;     // migrates through core.parseDocument
  loadVerbatim(id: SceneId): Promise<Result<VerbatimRecord, StoreError>>;   // the stored JSON untouched, for exporting a newer or damaged record (AUDIT-4)
  createScene(scene: NewScene, mode: 'create' | 'replace'): Promise<Result<void, StoreError>>;   // one readwrite transaction over scenes and assets: every asset, the record, and its thumbnail commit together or not at all; 'create' adds, so an existing id aborts it; 'replace' first deletes that id's record and asset range in the same transaction (R4-FRESH-8)
  save(doc: SceneDocument): Promise<Result<void, StoreError>>;       // document field of an existing record only; assets must already be committed
  saveThumbnail(id: SceneId, thumbnail: Blob): Promise<Result<void, StoreError>>;
  putAsset(scene: SceneId, id: AssetId, blob: Blob, mediaType: string): Promise<Result<void, StoreError>>;   // idempotent, one transaction
  getAsset(scene: SceneId, id: AssetId): Promise<Result<Blob, StoreError>>;
  listAssets(scene: SceneId): Promise<Result<readonly { readonly id: AssetId; readonly mediaType: string; readonly byteLength: number }[], StoreError>>;
  sweepOrphans(scene: SceneId): Promise<Result<number, StoreError>>;   // one readwrite transaction over scenes and assets: reads the stored record and, only when it is at the current version and parses, deletes every asset in its key range that core.assetRefs of it does not name; otherwise deletes nothing (13.2, R5-AUDIT-3)
  delete(scene: SceneId): Promise<Result<void, StoreError>>;         // record, asset key range, and that scene's cache entries in one transaction
  markExported(scene: SceneId, at: string): Promise<Result<void, StoreError>>;
  readonly cache: EmbeddingsCache;
  device(): Promise<DeviceState>;
  saveDevice(state: DeviceState): Promise<Result<void, StoreError>>;
  usage(): Promise<{ readonly usedBytes: number; readonly quotaBytes: number | null; readonly cacheBytes: number }>;
  evictCaches(): Promise<void>;                                      // embeddings first; called by the session's quota recovery (ENG-8 d)
}
export interface EmbeddingsCache {                                   // LRU by bytes, bounded at embeddingsCacheBytes (400 MB, about 24 scenes at 17 MB); keyed by (scene, master asset, encoder ModelId) (FRESH-12)
  get(scene: SceneId, master: AssetId, model: ModelId): Promise<SamEmbeddings | null>;
  put(scene: SceneId, master: AssetId, embeddings: SamEmbeddings): Promise<void>;
  has(scene: SceneId, master: AssetId, model: ModelId): Promise<boolean>;
}
export type VerbatimRecord = { readonly json: unknown; readonly version: number; readonly writer: BuildId | null };   // peekVersion's reading of a record this build cannot or should not parse
export type NewScene = {
  readonly document: SceneDocument;
  readonly assets: ReadonlyMap<AssetId, { readonly blob: Blob; readonly mediaType: string }>;   // hashed and built before the transaction opens, so nothing inside it awaits anything but IndexedDB
  readonly thumbnail: Blob;                                          // 256 px JPEG of the master; the first close replaces it with the current look
};
export type DeviceState = { readonly persistGranted: boolean | null; readonly installHintShownAt: string | null };
export function openStore(diag: DiagnosticSink<StoreEvent>): SceneStore;   // called once per page by the composition root (5.10, R7-B3)
/** Web Locks. Acquires with ifAvailable, so a held lock fails with open-elsewhere at once and nothing ever waits on it, then holds for fn's lifetime (a session passes a promise that settles on close). Every write to an existing scene's record or assets runs under it (FAIL-4, AUDIT-9); creating a scene under a new id needs none, because nothing can see the id before its one transaction commits (R4-FRESH-8). */
export function withSceneLock<T>(id: SceneId, fn: () => Promise<T>): Promise<Result<T, { readonly kind: 'open-elsewhere' }>>;
```

### 5.8 Scene file (`scenefile/`)

```ts
export type SceneFileJson = {
  readonly format: 'noisy-scene';
  readonly writer: BuildId;                                          // the build that wrote this zip
  readonly document: unknown;                                        // this build's SceneDocument at SCENE_VERSION on the normal path; a newer or damaged record's stored JSON verbatim on the rescue path (AUDIT-4); readers parse it through core.parseDocument either way
  readonly manifest: readonly { readonly id: AssetId; readonly path: string; readonly mediaType: string; readonly byteLength: number }[];
};
export type SceneFilePayload =
  | { readonly kind: 'parsed'; readonly document: SceneDocument }    // exportScene from a session; exportSceneFile of an ok record
  | { readonly kind: 'verbatim'; readonly record: VerbatimRecord };  // exportSceneFile of a newer or damaged record: json written untouched, manifest from listAssets over the whole key range
export type SceneFileError =
  | { readonly kind: 'not-a-scene' }
  | { readonly kind: 'too-large'; readonly bytes: number }
  | { readonly kind: 'newer-version'; readonly version: number; readonly writer: BuildId | null }
  | { readonly kind: 'corrupt'; readonly detail: string }            // zip structure, schema (unknown keys included), migration, hash or length mismatch, missing asset
  | { readonly kind: 'repacked-unsupported'; readonly detail: string };   // encryption, ZIP64, more than one leading directory
export function writeSceneFile(payload: SceneFilePayload, assets: ReadonlyMap<AssetId, { readonly blob: Blob; readonly mediaType: string }>, writer: BuildId): Promise<Blob>;   // a verbatim payload's entry timestamps come from the record's updatedAt when it parses as a string, else the epoch
export function readSceneFile(file: Blob): Promise<Result<{ readonly document: SceneDocument; readonly writer: BuildId; readonly assets: ReadonlyMap<AssetId, { readonly blob: Blob; readonly mediaType: string }> }, SceneFileError>>;
```

### 5.9 Session (`session/`)

```ts
export type SessionState = {
  readonly doc: SceneDocument;
  readonly palette: ResolvedPalette;                                 // core.color.resolvePalette(doc), derived once per document change and shared with the render scene; the swatch strip and the harmony picker read it (Q4)
  readonly save: { readonly kind: 'saved' } | { readonly kind: 'pending' } | { readonly kind: 'failed'; readonly error: StoreError };
  readonly analysis: Readonly<Record<AnalysisOp, StageStatus>>;   // a tap waiting on missing embeddings shows as decode 'queued', which is what the chip's "Getting objects ready" reads (R7-D4)
  readonly renderer: RendererStatus;
  readonly mode: RenderMode;
  readonly canUndo: boolean; readonly canRedo: boolean;
  readonly activity: 'editing' | 'playing' | 'exporting';   // playing: the canvas shows loop frames. editing: it shows the still (never started, stopped, or the Objects tab is active, 9.3). exporting: the export phase of section 14, where the canvas shows the job's frames and input is ignored (R5-FRESH-3)
};
export type StageStatus =
  | { readonly kind: 'done' } | { readonly kind: 'not-needed' } | { readonly kind: 'queued' }
  | { readonly kind: 'waiting-cooldown'; readonly untilMs: number }
  | { readonly kind: 'paused-hidden' }
  | { readonly kind: 'running'; readonly phase: 'download' | 'session' | 'infer'; readonly fraction: number | null }
  | { readonly kind: 'failed'; readonly error: AnalysisError }
  | { readonly kind: 'blocked-after-crashes'; readonly crashes: number };
export type TapIntent =
  | { readonly kind: 'pick'; readonly at: ImagePoint }              // select the object under the point, else create one
  | { readonly kind: 'new'; readonly at: ImagePoint }               // always create
  | { readonly kind: 'refine'; readonly object: ObjectId; readonly at: ImagePoint; readonly label: 'include' | 'exclude' }
  | { readonly kind: 'cycle'; readonly object: ObjectId };            // a repeated tap on the selected object: the next non-empty one of the decoder's three candidate masks, as an object.refine; `selected` when the other two are empty (Q5 a, 7.1, R6-3)
export type TapResult =
  | { readonly kind: 'selected' | 'created' | 'refined'; readonly object: ObjectId }
  | { readonly kind: 'empty' }                                      // every candidate of the decode was empty (no texel at or above 128): the document is unchanged and the Objects tab says nothing was found there (7.1 item 2, R6-3)
  | { readonly kind: 'blocked-after-crashes'; readonly crashes: number }
  | { readonly kind: 'failed'; readonly error: AnalysisError | StoreError };   // a tap waiting on embeddings settles 'failed' with the encoder's error if the encode stage fails, with 'cancelled' if the session closes; round 6's 'waiting' variant is gone (R7-D4)
export interface EditorSession {
  readonly state: SessionState;
  subscribe(listener: (state: SessionState) => void): () => void;
  edit(edit: Edit): void;
  undo(): void; redo(): void;
  tap(intent: TapIntent): Promise<TapResult>;                        // made before embeddings exist, the tap is queued and its promise stays pending until the decoder applies it, in tap order, or it settles 'blocked-after-crashes' or 'failed' (R7-D4); made during an export it settles 'failed' with 'cancelled' at once (section 14 rule 1)
  setIntent(intent: Pick<Intent, 'objectsActive'>): void;            // objectsActive from the UI's tab; while it is set, playback is stopped and the canvas shows the still; tapPending is the driver's own (5.4, R7-D4)
  retry(op: AnalysisOp): void;                                        // clears failed and crash counts for that op on this scene
  play(on: boolean): void;                                            // playing shows loop frames; not playing shows the still, never a frozen loop frame (9.3, Q19 settled)
  viewToFrame(view: ImagePoint): ImagePoint | null;                   // the identity while the canvas shows the still; null while it shows a loop frame or an export's frames, where no single mapping covers every layer (R3-FRESH-3)
  exportStill(): ExportJob<File>;
  exportLoop(target: { readonly kind: 'mp4'; readonly bitrateMbps?: number }): ExportJob<File>;
  exportLoop(target: { readonly kind: 'gif'; readonly longEdge: number; readonly delayCs: number }): GifExportJob;   // GIF delays are centiseconds; fps = 100 / delayCs; the Export tab's defaults are longEdge 480 and delayCs 10 (Q8); the job pauses at its estimate (R7-D3)
  exportScene(): Promise<Result<File, ExportError>>;                 // from the in-memory document and the asset cache; needs no store read (FAIL-2, FRESH-5)
  close(): Promise<void>;                                             // flush, cancel an export and analysis, settle every pending tap 'failed' with 'cancelled', await worker termination and in-flight asset writes, thumbnail, unload the renderer, release lock (13.2)
}
/** exportStill and exportLoop put the session in its export phase until the job settles (section 14, R5-FRESH-3): input is ignored (edit, undo, redo, play, and setIntent do nothing; exportScene and a second export resolve `cancelled`, and tap resolves `failed` with the `cancelled` error), the job waits in phase 'waiting-analysis' until analysis is quiet, which includes every tap that was pending before the call being applied (R7-D4), and from then on the driver starts no worker and applies no product and the scheduler draws only the job's frames. cancel() and close() settle it `cancelled`; hiding the tab settles it `hidden`, in every phase, the GIF's estimate pause included. */
export type ExportProgress =
  | { readonly phase: 'waiting-analysis' | 'rendering' | 'finishing'; readonly fraction: number }
  | { readonly phase: 'estimate'; readonly bytes: number; readonly frameCount: number };   // GIF only: the palette and frame 0 are built; the job holds its export phase until confirm() or cancel() (section 14, R7-D3)
export type ExportJob<T> = { readonly progress: (cb: (p: ExportProgress) => void) => void; readonly cancel: () => void; readonly result: Promise<Result<T, ExportError>> };
export type GifExportJob = ExportJob<File> & { readonly confirm: () => void };   // confirm() at the estimate renders and encodes the rest with the same palette; before or after the estimate it does nothing (R7-D3)
export type ExportError =
  | { readonly kind: 'encoder-unsupported'; readonly detail: string }   // refused by isConfigSupported, or an encoder error mid-export, with the encoder's message; nothing is retried at another bitrate (section 14)
  | { readonly kind: 'renderer'; readonly error: RendererError }
  | { readonly kind: 'hidden' }                                       // the tab was hidden while the job ran; no file is produced and the Export tab says why (R5-FRESH-3)
  | { readonly kind: 'cancelled' };                                  // cancel(), close(), or a call made while another export runs; exportScene needs no store read, so no store error exists here (FRESH-5)
export type OpenError = { readonly kind: 'open-elsewhere' } | StoreError;
export function openSession(args: { readonly sceneId: SceneId; readonly deps: SessionDeps }): Promise<Result<EditorSession, OpenError>>;
export type SessionDeps = {                                          // every collaborator injected (TEST-2)
  readonly store: SceneStore; readonly analysis: AnalysisService; readonly renderer: Renderer;   // analysis and renderer are the page's one of each, created by the composition root
  readonly clock: Clock; readonly ids: { scene(): SceneId; object(): ObjectId; light(): LightId };   // Clock and VisibilitySource are platform's (5.10, R7-F4)
  readonly visibility: VisibilitySource; readonly diag: DiagnosticSink<SessionEvent | ExportEvent>; readonly build: BuildId; readonly tab: TabId;   // diag: the session logs its own events and session/export.ts passes the same sink to export's exportStill and exportLoop, whose parameter is DiagnosticSink<ExportEvent>; the root's one Diagnostics<DiagnosticEvent> satisfies both (5.10, R7-7)
  readonly timing: TimingTable; readonly policy: RenderPolicy;
};
export type TimingTable = {
  readonly saveDebounceMs: number;        // 300
  readonly undoCoalesceMs: number;        // 600
  readonly plateDebounceMs: number;       // 1500
  readonly hiddenGraceMs: number;         // 4000: how long a hidden tab keeps its heavy job before cancelling it (7.1 rule 7, AUDIT-5)
  readonly hiddenGraceLateMs: number;     // 2000: a grace callback later than this on the wall clock was frozen with the process, so it re-arms instead of cancelling (7.1 rule 7, R3-FRESH-2)
  readonly cooldownMs: Readonly<Record<HeavyStage['kind'], number>>;   // after a terminated worker, keyed by the largest stage it ran: depth 5000, encode 20000, inpaint 5000 until P1 measures (section 7); a worker that ran no stage has no cooldown
  readonly decoderIdleMs: number;         // 120000
  readonly watchdogMs: Readonly<Record<AnalysisOp, number>>;   // visible time over session plus infer: depth 60000, encode 90000, decode 10000, inpaint 60000
  readonly fetchStallMs: number;          // 30000
  readonly contextLossWindowMs: number;   // 60000, three losses inside it fail the renderer
  readonly embeddingsCacheBytes: number;  // 400 MB (FRESH-12)
};
/** Constants the phone measurements set; chosen per device class from platform.capabilities.deviceClass (FRESH-3, AUDIT-1). */
export type RenderPolicy = {
  readonly modeDuringEncode: RenderMode['kind'];         // 'full' | 'low', applied while an encode need is listed or an encoder-bearing sequence is alive (7.1 rule 2). phone: 'low' until P1's GL rows say otherwise; desktop: 'full'; depth-only and inpaint-only sequences are always 'full'; "no GL context" is a P1 measurement, not a policy value (R3-FRESH-5)
  readonly previewDivisor: 1 | 2;                        // phone: 1 until the render gate (U5b) says otherwise; desktop: 1; export ignores it; the simplify stage has no policy lever, its working grid is a pipeline constant (9.2, R4-FRESH-2)
  readonly frameSource: FrameSource;                     // export's type (5.10): how an MP4 frame leaves the canvas (section 14): 'direct' on both device classes unless P1.5's U13 row shows that a WebGL2 canvas does not make a good frame there, then the first path in this order that does; macOS WebKit made a good direct frame in the interrogation (reviewer C, a WKWebView), the rest is INFERRED until that row (R7-D9)
};

export interface SceneLibrary {
  list(): Promise<readonly SceneSummary[]>;
  importPhoto(file: File): Promise<Result<SceneId, PhotoImportError>>;
  importSceneFile(file: File, onSameScene?: 'replace' | 'keep-both'): Promise<Result<SceneId, SceneFileError | { readonly kind: 'same-scene-exists'; readonly localUpdatedAt: string; readonly incomingUpdatedAt: string } | { readonly kind: 'open-elsewhere' } | StoreError>>;
  exportSceneFile(id: SceneId): Promise<Result<File, { readonly kind: 'open-elsewhere' } | StoreError>>;   // closed scenes, under the scene lock for the duration of its reads (13.3, R5-FRESH-7); an ok record writes a parsed payload; a newer or damaged record writes its stored JSON verbatim with every asset in its key range (AUDIT-4), which the Library labels "Export raw data"
  dropOriginal(id: SceneId): Promise<Result<void, { readonly kind: 'open-elsewhere' } | StoreError>>;   // Q2's "Drop original": a closed scene, under the scene lock: load, save(core.dropSource(doc)), then sweepOrphans, so the bytes are freed at once; not undoable, behind a confirm (R5-FRESH-7)
  delete(id: SceneId): Promise<Result<void, { readonly kind: 'open-elsewhere' } | StoreError>>;
  storage(): Promise<{ readonly usedBytes: number; readonly quotaBytes: number | null; readonly cacheBytes: number; readonly persisted: boolean; readonly standalone: boolean }>;
  requestPersist(): Promise<boolean>;
}
export type PhotoImportError = { readonly kind: 'heic-unsupported' } | { readonly kind: 'undecodable' } | { readonly kind: 'too-small'; readonly size: Size; readonly limit: 'long-edge' | 'master-short-side' } | { readonly kind: 'too-large'; readonly megapixels: number } | StoreError;   // too-small: the oriented photo's size and which limit of section 12 refused it, a long edge under 512 px or a master short side under 64 px, the long-edge limit when both refuse it (R7-19); the Library shows section 12's sentence for each kind and limit (R7-28)
```

### 5.10 Diagnostics, the test surface, and the composition root (`platform/`, `app/`)

`platform/` declares no domain type (R7-B3): its ring buffer is generic over the event type, and each leaf declares the part of the event vocabulary it logs and takes a sink of only that part.
The composition root (`main.tsx` and `app/`, section 4) declares the vocabulary as the union of those parts, creates the page's one `Diagnostics<DiagnosticEvent>`, and hands it to each leaf, whose narrower sink accepts it because a logger that takes the whole union takes any part of it.
P0.3 builds the generic buffer and the root's shell; each later part adds its leaf's events to the union and its members to the test surface once the types they name exist, so no part types `platform/` against modules that do not exist yet, and round 6's cycle, in which `platform/` named the types of the very modules that import its `Diagnostics`, is gone.

```ts
// platform/: generic, no domain type (R7-B3, R7-F4)
export interface DiagnosticSink<E extends { readonly event: string }> { log(e: E): void }
export interface Diagnostics<E extends { readonly event: string }> extends DiagnosticSink<E> { export(): string; readonly events: readonly (E & { readonly t: number })[] }   // a 500-event ring buffer; t is clock.now()
export function createDiagnostics<E extends { readonly event: string }>(clock: Clock, capacity?: number): Diagnostics<E>;
export interface Clock {                                 // the real one wraps performance.now, Date.now, and window.setTimeout and window.clearTimeout, never the bare globals, whose return type @types/node widens to NodeJS.Timeout in the test project (section 5, R7-8); the harness of P3c injects one it can advance (R7-F4)
  now(): number;                                         // monotonic milliseconds (performance.now): watchdogs, cooldowns, debounces, event times
  wallNow(): number;                                     // wall-clock milliseconds (Date.now): the late-grace test of 7.1 rule 7 compares it across a suspension, which the monotonic clock need not count
  setTimeout(callback: () => void, ms: number): number;
  clearTimeout(handle: number): void;
}
export interface VisibilitySource {                      // the real one wraps document.visibilityState and the two events; the harness scripts it (R7-F4)
  readonly hidden: boolean;
  onChange(listener: (hidden: boolean) => void): () => void;   // visibilitychange
  onPageHide(listener: () => void): () => void;                // pagehide (7.1 rule 9)
}
export type DeviceClass = 'phone' | 'desktop';           // 'phone' when the user agent names iPhone or iPad, or the primary pointer is coarse with no hover; Safari exposes no deviceMemory, so this heuristic (INFERRED) selects the RenderPolicy row and is recorded so Diagnostics show which row ran (FRESH-3)

// Each leaf's part of the vocabulary, declared and logged by the module named (R7-B3)
export type AnalysisEvent =                              // analysis/service.ts
  | { readonly event: 'worker.spawn' | 'worker.terminate'; readonly worker: 'sequence' | 'decode'; readonly reason: string }
  | { readonly event: 'stage.start' | 'stage.end'; readonly op: AnalysisOp; readonly ms: number; readonly wasmHighWaterBytes: number | null }
  | { readonly event: 'stage.failed'; readonly op: AnalysisOp; readonly error: AnalysisError }
  | { readonly event: 'fetch.start' | 'fetch.end'; readonly model: ModelId };   // a model file's download and verification, so "no fetch inside an encoder stage" is readable on Safari and the phone (R5-SEAM-5)
export type RenderEvent =                                // render/
  | { readonly event: 'render.first' | 'render.frame'; readonly ms: number; readonly mode: RenderMode; readonly passes: Readonly<Partial<Record<PassName, number>>> }   // ms per pass where timing is available; the render gate (U5b) reads these
  | { readonly event: 'render.compiled'; readonly ms: number; readonly programs: number }   // boot-time program compilation, once per page and again after a context restore (R4-FRESH-13)
  | { readonly event: 'render.derived'; readonly fillMs: number }   // logged by load each time it refills the background depth on the CPU, after a depth product or a change of the hole (9.2): the fill's time, which no render.frame carries; P7.1's phone row adds it to the next render's normals time (R7-23)
  | { readonly event: 'render.resident'; readonly bytes: number }
  | { readonly event: 'context.lost' | 'context.restored'; readonly losses: number };
export type PassName = 'geometry' | 'lights' | 'quantize' | 'outline' | 'cells' | 'camera' | 'present' | 'simplify' | 'normals';   // render/; normals is the normals pass of 9.2, reported in the render.frame of the render that rebuilds the normals texture, whole or in part, and absent from every other (R7-23)
export type StoreEvent = { readonly event: 'store.reconnect'; readonly cause: string };   // store/
export type FrameSource = 'direct' | 'drawImage' | 'readPixels';   // export/mp4.ts; RenderPolicy.frameSource names it, since session may import export and not the reverse (R7-D9)
export type ExportEvent =                                // export/, logged through the sink session/export.ts passes to exportStill and exportLoop, which is SessionDeps.diag (5.9, R7-7)
  | { readonly event: 'worker.spawn' | 'worker.terminate'; readonly worker: 'gif'; readonly reason: string }
  | { readonly event: 'export.frame'; readonly frame: number; readonly ms: number; readonly queue: number }
  | { readonly event: 'export.estimate'; readonly bytes: number; readonly frameCount: number }   // the GIF size the job paused on (section 14), so a phone row can compare it with the file (R7-D3)
  | { readonly event: 'export.video'; readonly codec: string; readonly size: Size; readonly bitrate: number; readonly frameSource: FrameSource; readonly frameColorSpace: VideoColorSpaceInit | null; readonly configColorSpace: VideoColorSpaceInit | null };   // once per MP4 export: the path its frames left the canvas by (section 14, R7-D9), and what the first VideoFrame and the encoder's decoder configuration declared (R5-FRESH-8)
export type SessionEvent =                               // session/
  | { readonly event: 'import.start'; readonly ms: number; readonly megapixels: number }
  | { readonly event: 'import.done'; readonly ms: number; readonly megapixels: number; readonly path: 'resize' | 'fallback' }   // path: whether createImageBitmap decoded at the master size, or the full decode and drawImage ran; photo's decodePhoto reports it and the library logs it (section 12, R7-D9)
  | { readonly event: 'liveness.found'; readonly op: AnalysisOp; readonly scene: SceneId; readonly verdict: 'alive' | 'background' | 'crash' }   // the startup sweep, which main.tsx runs through the driver's module
  | { readonly event: 'visibility'; readonly state: 'hidden' | 'visible'; readonly action: 'grace-started' | 'job-cancelled' | 'grace-late' | 'resumed' }   // job-cancelled: the grace fired on time and cancelled the heavy job; grace-late: the callback fired after a suspension and re-armed (R3-FRESH-2); resumed: visible again, inside the grace or after it (R5-OBS-3)
  | { readonly event: 'save.failed'; readonly error: StoreError }
  | { readonly event: 'export.start'; readonly kind: 'png' | 'mp4' | 'gif' }   // the job's first render, after its wait for quiet analysis (section 14)
  | { readonly event: 'export.end'; readonly kind: 'png' | 'mp4' | 'gif'; readonly outcome: 'ok' | 'failed' | 'cancelled' | 'hidden'; readonly ms: number };   // with export.start, the span in which P8.1's row finds no analysis worker spawned (a GIF job's own worker spawns inside it) and no product applied, read from a Diagnostics export alone (R6-OBS-1)
export type LivenessMarker = { readonly scene: SceneId; readonly op: AnalysisOp; readonly build: BuildId; readonly tab: TabId; readonly startedAt: number; readonly hiddenAt: number | null };   // session/analysis-driver.ts, which owns the markers; hiddenAt is set while a job runs inside the hidden grace (7.1 rule 7); stored in one slot per worker kind, `noisy.job.<tab>.heavy` for a sequence stage and `noisy.job.<tab>.decode` for a decode, chosen by op (7.1 rule 9, R4-FRESH-5)

// app/: the composition root's types, which may name every module's (section 4, R7-B3)
export type DiagnosticEvent =                            // the enumerated vocabulary is TEST-18's
  | AnalysisEvent | RenderEvent | StoreEvent | ExportEvent | SessionEvent
  | { readonly event: 'csp.violation'; readonly directive: string; readonly blocked: string }
  | { readonly event: 'env'; readonly snapshot: EnvironmentSnapshot };
export type EnvironmentSnapshot = { readonly userAgent: string; readonly build: BuildId; readonly deviceClass: DeviceClass; readonly crossOriginIsolated: boolean; readonly webgl2: GlReport | null; readonly storage: { readonly quota: number | null; readonly persisted: boolean }; readonly standalone: boolean; readonly hardwareConcurrency: number };   // webgl2: render's report of what createRenderer found, from the renderer or from its error (9.1, R7-F1)

// Dev builds only; a bundle-guard test asserts the production bundle has no `__noisyTest` (TEST-1)
export type TestSurface = {
  openScene(id: SceneId): Promise<Result<EditorSession, OpenError>>;
  library(): SceneLibrary;
  session(): EditorSession | null;
  config(patch: ConfigPatch): void;
  diagnostics(): readonly DiagnosticEvent[];
  forceContextLoss(): void;
  plantLivenessMarker(marker: LivenessMarker): void;
  storeAudit(): Promise<{ readonly scenes: readonly SceneSummary[]; readonly orphans: number; readonly unownedRanges: number; readonly dangling: readonly AssetId[] }>;   // unownedRanges: asset key ranges with no scenes record; always 0 by construction since scenes are created in one transaction, and asserted so (R4-FRESH-8)
  gpuResident(): ResidentReport;
  renderAt(request: RenderRequest): Promise<ImageData>;   // renders the request in full mode at divisor 1, as an export does, whatever the scheduler's current mode, the policy, or the forceMode lever holds, and neither reads nor changes the scheduler's mode; gbufferAt and labelTextureAt render the same way, since only full mode builds what they read (R7-30)
  gbufferAt(request: RenderRequest): Promise<{ readonly size: Size; readonly color: Uint8Array; readonly depth: Float32Array; readonly surface: Uint8Array; readonly frame: Uint8Array }>;   // pass 1's color, depth, surface (id, normal x, normal y, texture-edge value), and frame attachments, the last read in the same render before its target returns to the pool (u and v as 16-bit fixed point, 9.3, R7-14), for the registration and coverage checks and for exact checks of what the simplified textures hold (R3-AUDIT-3, R3-AUDIT-6, R5-SEAM-1); the R16F depth attachment is read with readPixels(RGBA, FLOAT) into four floats per pixel and R kept, because (RED, FLOAT) is INVALID_OPERATION in WebKit and RGBA with FLOAT is the pair every float color buffer allows (R7-F5)
  labelTextureAt(request: RenderRequest): Promise<{ readonly size: Size; readonly data: Uint8Array }>;   // the label texture as the request's render left it, after any rebuild that render made (9.2), one byte per texel holding the index in `RenderScene.layers` of the layer 5.4's labelAt rule names there (0 for the back layer), as the surface attachment's id does; built in P3b.5, whose texel-for-texel row and reorder row read it, and kept out of production like every member here (R7-4)
  simplifyGridAt(request: RenderRequest): Promise<readonly { readonly source: 'master' | 'back-layer'; readonly size: Size; readonly data: Uint8Array }[]>;   // runs the simplify stage's downsample pass alone, over the whole working grid, for each source the request's scene has, and returns that RGBA8 target: rgb the label-aware average, alpha the label of the block's label texel (9.2); the P3b.3 label row reads it (R6-DEF-5)
};
export type ConfigPatch = TimingPatch & Partial<RenderPolicy> & {
  readonly forceUnsupportedCodec?: boolean;
  readonly forceStoreUnavailable?: boolean;              // the FRESH-5 fault
  readonly delayAssetWriteMs?: number;                   // the FRESH-16 fault
  readonly forceMode?: RenderMode | null;                // the render scheduler uses this mode in place of the policy's decision while it is set, null clears it; it is how P3b.4 renders low mode before P3c.1 builds the policy (R7-B5); it never applies to an export job's frames, which render in full mode at divisor 1 whatever it holds (section 14 rule 3, R7-9), nor to renderAt, gbufferAt, or labelTextureAt, which render the same way (R7-30)
};
export type TimingPatch = { readonly [K in keyof TimingTable]?: TimingTable[K] extends number ? number : Partial<TimingTable[K]> };   // deep for the per-op and per-stage tables, so config({ watchdogMs: { depth: 1 } }) type-checks and leaves the other ops alone (R7-F4)
```

Every generated control, the status chip, and every banner carries a `data-testid` derived from its control-table key.

## 6. Data flow from import to export

```
pick photo ──> library.importPhoto (main thread; no session open)
                 oriented size from an undrawn image element (naturalWidth, naturalHeight): reject a long edge under 512 px, a master short side under 64 px (R7-19), and more than 64 MP, and fix the master size before any decode (R5-FRESH-7)
                 createImageBitmap(file, { resizeWidth, resizeHeight, resizeQuality: 'high', imageOrientation: 'from-image' })  <- decode-time downscale to that master size (PHONE-8)
                 fallback: full decode then drawImage when the options are rejected; an image element that fails to load is HEIC-in-Chrome or undecodable; import.done records which path ran (R7-D9)
                 master PNG (2048 long edge, both sides even, R7-C), the master at 256 px as the first thumbnail, k-means 16 + tone stats on a 128 px copy
                 hash source and master; store.createScene({ document with empty products, assets: source and master, thumbnail }, 'create')
                 one readwrite transaction over scenes and assets, so an import commits whole or leaves nothing; no lock, because nothing can see the id before the commit (R4-FRESH-8)
openSession
                 withSceneLock (ifAvailable, held until close) -> load
                 -> store.sweepOrphans(scene) before anything else writes: the stored record's own references, one transaction, nothing deleted when the record was stored at an older version (13.2, R5-AUDIT-3) -> asset cache fills (bytes of every referenced asset) -> decode master -> renderer.load (the page's one renderer, programs compiled at boot, R4-FRESH-13)
                 -> driver: object.depth for any object whose produced.depth is null while depth exists (R7-D8); needs = reconcile(doc, cache, blocked, intent) -> render: full mode, or on the phone low mode while an encode need is listed or an encoder-bearing sequence is alive, so a new photo's first frame is already low (R4-FRESH-1)
                 target: first look within 2 s of picking (FIT-11)
                 driver: plan one heavy sequence from the listed heavy needs, [depth?, encode?, inpaint?], which is [depth, encode] for a new photo -> wait out the cooldown of the page's last heavy termination -> fetchModels (download phase, outside the watchdog)
                 runSequence -> onDepth: putAsset(map) (asset cache too) -> applyProduce(depth) -> applyProduce(object.depth) for each object whose produced.depth is null (R7-D8) -> save now -> renderer.load with the depth -> render (outlines on at once in full mode; on the phone when the sequence ends and the mode returns to full)
                             -> onEmbeddings: driver keeps them for the open scene -> cache.put -> (sequence ends unless a plate is due) -> terminate
                 auto-subject (depth exists, no objects), once no encoder-bearing worker is alive: fetchModels(['decode']) -> openDecoder({ embeddings from the driver, guide from the asset cache }) -> seedFromDepth -> decoder.decode -> acceptSubject -> putAsset(mask) -> applyProduce(auto-subject) -> save now -> render (subject clarity on)
                             -> decoder.release() at once, unless the Objects tab is active or a tap is pending, in which case it is the resident decoder of 7.1 (R6-DEF-7)
                 plate when plateNeeded: deferred while intent.objectsActive or a tap is pending; then after cooldownMs[largest stage of the last terminated worker] and plateDebounceMs since the last mask change -> sequence [inpaint] (decoder stays, mode stays full) -> putAsset(plate) -> applyProduce(plate) -> save now
tap              -> the canvas shows the still, so the canvas point is the frame point (viewToFrame) -> labelAt over stored refined masks -> selected
                    else a Decoder is alive ? decoder.decode(points) : embeddings = the driver's copy ?? cache.get(scene, master, encoder model)
                         none: the tap is queued (tapPending; state.analysis.decode queued, the chip's "Getting objects ready"), reconcile lists encode, and the tap's promise stays pending until the decode after the encoder applies it, in tap order (R7-D4); found: fetchModels(['decode']) -> openDecoder -> decoder.decode(points)   (a reopened scene never reruns the encoder, R3-AUDIT-2)
                    -> the first non-empty candidate in score order (all three empty: the tap resolves `empty` and nothing is written, R6-3) -> refine -> putAsset(mask) -> applyEdit(object.add | object.refine) -> save debounced -> render
                    cycle (a repeated tap on the selected object): the driver's last candidates if they are that object's, else decoder.decode(its stored selection) -> the next non-empty candidate after the stored index, modulo 3 (both others empty: the tap resolves `selected` and applies nothing) -> putAsset(mask) -> applyEdit(object.refine with that index) (Q5 a, R6-3)
                    -> the first user object while autoSubject is pending: applyProduce(auto-subject, found null) -> save now (R4-FRESH-11)
                    -> depth exists and the new object's produced.depth is null (it decoded during a depth retry): applyProduce(object.depth) after this apply, as after every apply (R7-D8)
slider           -> applyEdit (clamped, coalesced) -> render on the next animation frame -> save after saveDebounceMs, and at once on hidden and pagehide
swatch           -> the Look tab's strip shows state.palette; a tap on an unpinned swatch opens the picker -> applyEdit(palette.target for the entry's lowest-numbered cluster) -> the palette re-resolves (the entry takes the target, the harmony re-anchors and, under `auto`, may change template) -> LUT upload -> render (Q4)
                    a tap on a pinned swatch (a subject's own cluster, no index into analysis.colors) opens that object's recolor picker -> applyEdit(object.set recolor), the Objects tab's own edit; no palette target is stored (R6-2)
hidden           -> flush save (document only) at once; hiddenAt set in each occupied marker slot; watchdog paused; grace timer hiddenGraceMs
                    grace fires on time, still hidden -> cancelHeavy + decoder.release + clear both marker slots; chip paused-hidden
                    grace fires late (the process was suspended with the timer pending) -> re-arm, cancel nothing (R3-FRESH-2)
                    visible inside the grace, or after a suspension the job survived -> hiddenAt cleared, watchdog resumed, nothing lost; visible after a cancel -> reconcile again, no failure recorded
pagehide         -> flush save (document only) + clear both marker slots (a reload or navigation is not a crash, FRESH-7)
export still     -> export phase (section 14): input ignored; wait until analysis is quiet (reconcile lists nothing, no decode in flight, no tap pending, so a tap made before the call is applied first; a deferred refill runs now); then the driver holds and any resident decoder is released; export.start
                    -> render(still) into the canvas -> canvas.toBlob(png) -> export.end -> the phase ends and the driver reconciles again
export loop      -> same phase -> mp4: for frame in 0..N-1: render(loop frame) into the canvas -> a VideoFrame by policy.frameSource (section 14, R7-D9) -> VideoEncoder -> Mediabunny
                    gif: render every fifth frame and build the palette -> frame 0 mapped and LZW-encoded in the GIF worker -> progress 'estimate' with bytes, export.estimate -> wait for confirm() (cancel() settles `cancelled`) -> every frame with that palette (R7-D3)
                    hidden at any point, the GIF's estimate pause included -> the job settles `hidden`; export.end in every case
export scene     -> writeSceneFile(parsed in-memory doc, assets from the asset cache) -> share sheet (`deviceClass` phone) or download (desktop); markExported (a store failure here is logged, not an export failure)
close            -> flush, cancel an export and analysis, settle every pending tap `failed` with `cancelled` (R7-D4), await worker termination and every in-flight putAsset, thumbnail from frame 0, renderer.unload, release lock; nothing is swept here (13.2); a produce or decode result arriving after close is dropped
```

Only one scene is open at a time.
Opening another scene, importing a photo, or leaving the editor closes the session; queued analysis for a closed scene is dropped and `reconcile` picks it up on the next open.
The renderer, the analysis service, and with it the record of the last heavy termination outlive the session, so the next scene's first heavy worker still waits out the cooldown (7.1 rule 3, R4-FRESH-4).

## 7. Analysis scheduling and memory release

The mechanism rests on one fact about WebAssembly: a module's linear memory never shrinks, and `session.release()` returns memory to ORT's allocator, not to the operating system.
The way to hand a stage's peak back is to drop the JavaScript realm that owns it, which for a dedicated worker means `terminate()`.
Terminate ending a worker's nested pthread workers is implementation behavior, not a specification guarantee (CLAIM-2); it is VERIFIED on macOS Safari 26.6.2 (threads back to 3 to 6 within about 10 s, footprint back to about 60 MB within 1 to 2 s, six cycles with flat peaks) and INFERRED for iOS until P1's twenty-cycle soak.

### 7.1 Two workers per open scene

1. **The heavy sequence worker** (`analysis/sequence-worker.ts`) runs whatever heavy stages the driver hands it, in order, in one ORT wasm heap: depth, then encode, then inpaint when a plate is already due.
   It releases each session before creating the next, posts each product as it lands, and is terminated when the list ends.
   Its peak is the largest stage, not a timing-dependent sum of stages (PHONE-2), and that stage is the encoder at every aspect ratio because the depth input is bounded by token count (5.2, R5-FRESH-4); the heap reuse was VERIFIED on the Mac (290 MB after depth, still 290 MB after the encoder session was created, 791 MB after the encoder ran).
   One spawn and one release per sequence instead of one per stage also saves two worker starts of 0.35 to 0.45 s each on import.
2. **The resident decode worker** (`analysis/decode-worker.ts`) holds the SAM decoder, one scene's embeddings, and the 1024 px guide; about 76 to 91 MB of wasm.
   It lives while the Objects tab is active or a tap is pending, and is released when the tab is left, after `decoderIdleMs`, when the hidden grace elapses, when an encoder-bearing sequence starts, when an export leaves its wait (section 14 rule 3), or on close.
   The driver opens it through `openDecoder` with the scene's embeddings, from its own memory or from `store.cache`, and the guide from the asset cache, so a reopened scene whose embeddings are cached decodes without an encoder run (R3-AUDIT-2).
   Release on leaving the tab is deliberate (R3-AUDIT-4): the decoder's 100 to 150 MB is held only while it can be used, and the respawn on return (one worker start and one session, under a second) begins when the tab becomes active, before the owner's first tap.
   The decoder opened for the **automatic subject** has the same lifetime by the same rule (R6-DEF-7): it is released as soon as that one decode has been composed, whether or not it produced an object, unless the Objects tab is active or a tap is pending at that moment, in which case it simply is the resident decoder above.
   Holding it to `decoderIdleMs` instead would keep 100 to 150 MB alive for two minutes after every import for a tap that may never come, beside the first plate refill; a return to the Objects tab respawns it in under a second, which is what leaving the tab already relies on.
   Each decode's three candidate masks stay in the driver's memory until the next decode (Q5 a): a `cycle` tap on the object they belong to applies the next non-empty candidate after the stored `selection.candidate`, modulo 3, as an `object.refine` with the new index; a `cycle` on any other object first decodes that object's stored selection again, which is why `Selection` carries the index (5.2).
   A `cycle` on the automatic subject works the same way: the refine keeps `kind: 'auto-subject'` and its seed and stores the new index, so the stored selection re-runs to the cycled mask on the other device (R7-D5); round 6 said the index was always 0 there, which no cycle could keep true.
   A candidate is **empty** when no texel of its mask is at or above 128, the level `bounds` is the bbox of, so an empty candidate has no bounds and can never enter the document (R6-3): `pick`, `new`, and `refine` take the first non-empty candidate in score order and resolve `empty`, writing nothing, when all three are; `cycle` skips empty candidates and resolves `selected`, applying nothing, when the two it would move to are both empty (the stored candidate is the object's current mask, so it is never empty); the automatic subject reads candidate 0 alone and its own acceptance gate rejects an empty one (5.5, section 16).
   SAM's second and third outputs can be near-empty for some points (UNKNOWN how often on the owner's photos; P4.2's ten-photo row records it), and without this rule a `cycle` was the path by which an object with no bbox reached `layers`, `objectMotionBound`, the hole mask, and the LUT row on both devices.

Rules the driver (`session/analysis-driver.ts`) enforces:

1. At most one heavy worker exists in the page.
   The decode worker never coexists with an **encoder-bearing** sequence; a tap during one is queued, its promise pending, and is applied when the decoder is up, in tap order, while the chip reads "Getting objects ready" from `state.analysis` (R7-D4).
   It may stay resident, and answer taps, during a depth-only or inpaint-only sequence: MI-GAN at 2048 (474 MB wasm, 659 MB RSS) plus the decoder (76 to 91 MB wasm) is about 0.8 GB RSS, below the encoder's own measured 1055 MB (AUDIT-2; the set is a row in 8.1).
   Because the decoder is released when the Objects tab is left and refills are deferred while it is active (rule 4), this set arises when the owner returns to the Objects tab while a refill or a depth retry is running: the decoder respawns and answers taps without waiting for the sequence (R3-AUDIT-4).
2. Nothing else heavy overlaps the encoder: no model prefetch, no photo decode, no PNG encode, no export (PHONE-1).
   For exports this holds in both directions by structure, not by a guard per case: an export starts only when analysis is quiet, and the driver starts nothing until it has settled (section 14, R5-FRESH-3).
   The render scheduler applies `policy.modeDuringEncode` (`low` on the phone until P1 says otherwise, `full` on the Mac) from the moment `reconcile` lists `encode` until the sequence that runs it has ended, whether that run is planned, downloading, waiting on a cooldown, or running (R4-FRESH-1).
   On the phone a new photo's first frame is therefore already low, and the mode does not flip between download, depth, and encoder; keyed on a live worker alone, as in round 3, the phone would have opened in full mode and dropped to low mid-import.
   The scheduler stays in full mode during depth-only and inpaint-only sequences everywhere, so a plate refill never changes the look and the Mac never degrades (FRESH-3, section 8).
3. A heavy worker never starts within `cooldownMs[stage]` of the previous heavy worker's termination, where `stage` is the largest stage that worker actually ran; a worker terminated before any stage ran (during fetch or session creation) imposes no cooldown (AUDIT-5).
   The encoder default is 20 s from the Mac RSS drain curve (encoder residual 775 to 835 MB for 13 s, baseline at 23 s); the footprint measurement says the drain may be 1 to 2 s, so P1 measures the phone with stepped gaps (0.5, 2, 5, 10, 20 s) between an encoder-containing sequence and a MI-GAN stage, and between two MI-GAN sequences, and sets each constant to the smallest gap that survives ten times with margin; depth and inpaint default to 5 s until then.
   The termination time and the largest stage are recorded by the page's one `AnalysisService` (`lastHeavyTermination`, 5.5), not by the per-session driver, and every driver reads that record when it plans (R4-FRESH-4).
   Closing a scene mid-encoder and opening or importing another therefore waits out the same cooldown; as driver state, which is what round 3 had, the next session knew nothing of the termination and section 16's own case (a second photo imported while analysis runs) bypassed this rule.
   A reload still starts clean; that case stays with U2 (R3-FRESH-4).
   The status chip shows `waiting-cooldown` so the wait is never silent.
   In practice the encoder runs once per photo per device (embeddings are cached), so the encoder cooldown is paid once, before the first plate after import; later refills pay only the inpaint cooldown.
4. The plate stage joins a sequence only when `plateNeeded(doc)` was already true when the sequence was planned; otherwise it is its own sequence, debounced `plateDebounceMs` after the last mask change (A's gate: the plate exists only for motion, JUDGE-A-1, JUDGE-B-4).
   A refill is **deferred** while `intent.objectsActive` or a tap is pending, and runs when the owner leaves the Objects tab, presses play in the Motion tab, or starts an export, so a run of taps costs one refill, not one per tap (AUDIT-2).
   While a refill is deferred nothing shows the stale plate: the Objects tab renders the still, where every object covers its own hole (9.3, R3-FRESH-3); the doubled edge of section 16 can appear only in the seconds between leaving the tab and the refill landing.
5. Model files are fetched by `fetchModels` before the sequence starts or a decoder opens, outside any watchdog, with a stall timeout (`fetchStallMs` with no bytes) and Range resume into a temporary Cache entry promoted only after the SHA-256 check (FAIL-3, JUDGE-A-11); the chip shows the `download` phase.
   Prefetch of later models runs only when no heavy worker is alive and no export is running.
6. Watchdogs cover the `session` and `infer` phases only and count visible time: they pause on `visibilitychange` to hidden and resume the remaining budget on visible (PHONE-3, FAIL-3).
   This pause is reachable because rule 7 keeps the job alive through the grace.
7. On hidden: the save is flushed at once (document only), `hiddenAt` is set in each occupied liveness-marker slot, the watchdog pauses, and a grace timer of `hiddenGraceMs` (4 s) starts.
   If the tab becomes visible inside the grace, `hiddenAt` is cleared and the watchdog resumes; nothing was lost.
   The grace callback cancels only when it fires **on time and still hidden** (R3-FRESH-2).
   If the wall clock shows it fired more than `hiddenGraceLateMs` (2 s) late, the content process was suspended with the timer pending (INFERRED: iOS suspends a backgrounded Safari's web processes within seconds), the job made no progress and lost nothing, so the callback re-arms the grace, logs `grace-late`, and cancels nothing; the `visibilitychange` that follows a resume then clears it, in whichever order the two arrive.
   A job that survived a suspension therefore continues on return with its watchdog budget intact, which is the best outcome and costs nothing.
   If the grace fires on time while still hidden (a tab switch inside Safari, or a process iOS left running), the running heavy job is cancelled and its worker terminated, the decode worker is released, and both marker slots are cleared; on visible the driver reconciles again and reruns the missing stage silently, recording no failure (PHONE-3, PERSIST-2, AUDIT-5).
   While the process is suspended no script runs, so the cancel cannot release memory on that path; a tab reclaimed in that state still carries `hiddenAt` and scores as a background reclaim under rule 9.
   The chip shows `paused-hidden` while hidden.
   What an interruption costs: inside the grace, nothing; after a suspension the job survived, nothing; after a cancel, the elapsed work of the running stage (up to 5.8 s for the encoder) plus that stage's cooldown before the rerun (20 s on the encoder default, 1 to 2 s if P1 confirms the footprint reading of U2, 5 s for depth or inpaint).
8. A stage that failed in this session is not retried automatically, except a `download` failure with status `stalled` or `offline`, which is retried once when `online` fires.
9. **Liveness markers** (FAIL-1, JUDGE-A-2, JUDGE-B-1): at startup the page requests and holds the Web Lock `noisy-tab-<tabId>` for its lifetime.
   Before posting a heavy stage the driver writes `localStorage['noisy.job.<tabId>.heavy']`, and before a decode `localStorage['noisy.job.<tabId>.decode']`, each `{ scene, op, build, tab, startedAt, hiddenAt: number | null }` (the `LivenessMarker` type, plantable through the test surface, TEST-14); each slot is cleared by its own worker's completion, failure, or cancel, and both by the hidden grace elapsing and by `pagehide`.
   One slot per worker kind, because rule 1 lets a decode run beside a depth-only or inpaint-only sequence: with the single slot of round 3 the decode overwrote the sequence's marker and its completion cleared the slot while the stage was still running, so a kill in exactly that concurrent set went uncounted or was blamed on decode (R4-FRESH-5).
   On hidden, `hiddenAt` is set in every slot that is occupied.
   `pagehide` fires on reload, navigation, and tab close, all of which release the tab lock, so clearing there is what keeps a reload from scoring as a crash (FRESH-7); a jetsam kill fires no `pagehide`, so its marker survives.
   At startup `main.tsx` reads every `noisy.job.*` marker, scoring each slot by itself, and asks `navigator.locks.query()`: if `noisy-tab-<tab>` is held, the tab is alive and the marker stays; if `hiddenAt` is set, the kill happened inside the hidden grace, so it was a background reclaim and the marker is deleted with no count; otherwise it was a foreground death and `noisy.crashes` gains one for `(scene, op, build)`.
   Two counts block that op for that scene (`blocked-after-crashes` in the chip and in `TapResult`); `retry(op)` clears the count; counts are keyed by build so a fixed build inherits nothing.
   Reconcile handles resumption; the marker exists only for the repeat-kill guard.
   Residual (INFERRED): a page killed after `visibilitychange` but before its `pagehide` handler ran during a reload would count once; two such events in one build are needed to block anything.
10. Workers run ORT's prebuilt `ort.wasm.min.mjs` and its threaded wasm from `/vendor/ort/` on the same origin, `numThreads` at ORT's default (2 on the phone, 4 on the Mac), `graphOptimizationLevel: 'all'`, the `wasm` execution provider only.
    `numThreads: 1` is a documented lever for the sequence worker (1.6x slower, measured), not a memory lever (the encoder's peak is 791 to 796 MB at 1, 2, and 4 threads).

### 7.2 Model files (`analysis/models.ts`)

- `models.lock.json` pins each file: source URL at a commit, SHA-256, byte length, license, and the served path `/models/<name>-<sha8>.onnx`; `ModelId` is `<name>@<sha256>`, so embeddings cached under one encoder never meet a different decoder (PERSIST-3).
- Cache Storage `noisy-models-<lock hash>` holds verified files; at startup caches with another lock hash are deleted, and embeddings whose encoder `ModelId` is not in the lock are deleted with them.
- Model bytes total about 117 MB (27.3 depth, 52.6 plus 0.4 encoder, 8.7 plus 0.3 decoder, 28.1 MI-GAN), served from the app's own origin; loading from a CDN was rejected (A section 18).
- Model files follow the same Cache Storage eviction as scenes on iOS, so a Safari-tab user who skips a week re-downloads them, which needs the Mac reachable and running noisy's start command (Q21), as loading the app itself does: there is no service worker and no offline shell in v1 (Q11, settled), and the AWS host at release is what makes noisy reachable away from the Mac (PHONE-10).

### 7.3 Phone timeline for a new 24 MP photo

INFERRED from the bench's per-stage numbers plus one worker start: first look about 1 s after picking, in low mode; `reconcile` lists depth and the encoder together (5.4), so one heavy sequence runs depth (3.3 s) and the encoder (5.8 s) with one start (0.4 s) and two session creations (about 0.6 s), about 10 s, after which the mode returns to full; the decoder spawns and the auto subject lands about 0.5 s later, so subject-aware features light up at about 11 s, and that decoder is released at once unless the owner is already in the Objects tab (R6-DEF-7); each tap after that about 0.1 s, the first one in the Objects tab after a respawn of under a second that begins when the tab opens.
A plate, when motion is first turned on, waits out the encoder cooldown (up to 20 s after the sequence ended, typically already elapsed) and takes about 1.5 s; later refills wait the inpaint cooldown (5 s default) and the debounce, run without evicting the decoder or lowering the preview, and are deferred while the owner is tapping in the Objects tab.

## 8. Resolutions and the phone memory budget

| Quantity | Value | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Master (canonical grid, still and MP4 export) | long edge `min(2048, photo)` floored to even, short edge to the nearest even pixel, both devices (section 12, R7-C) | the look erases detail by design; 2048 is the 3 MP frame of the camera the look imitates; MI-GAN passed 30 runs at 2048 on the phone; the cap stands on texture memory and the target look, not on MI-GAN, whose peak is input-size-insensitive (PHONE-6); the kept source makes a later cap reversible (Q2) |
| Depth plane | the depth input size of 5.2: short side 518 px unless the bound of `DEPTH_MAX_TOKENS` = 1813 patches scales both sides down (686 x 518 at 4:3, 1190 x 294 at 4:1), f32, at most 1.4 MB | joint-bilateral upsampled on the GPU at load, and kept on the GPU as an R32F texture read only with `texelFetch`, from which the normals pass takes its slopes in f32 (9.2, R7-1); full-res depth would cost 12 MB for no visible gain; the bound holds the depth stage's memory at the 4:3 figure for every aspect, measured 277 to 348 MB of wasm from 4:3 to 4:1 against 1103 MB for an unbounded 4:1 input (`experiments/depth-token-bound/`, R5-FRESH-4) |
| Masks | long edge 1024, u8, refined by a guided filter against the master | SAM's 256 px logits carry no more; the renderer, the hit test, and the hole all read the same pixels (RENDER-3) |
| Plate | master size PNG | lossless, read directly as a layer texture; composited only inside the hole (RENDER-4) |
| Thumbnail | 256 px JPEG, a field of the scenes record: the master at import, then the current look rendered on close and after export | the Library shows the owner's work, never inside the hidden flush (FAIL-6, JUDGE-B-18); it is device-local and regenerated, so it is not an asset and no scene file carries it (R4-FRESH-7) |
