| Simplification working grid | the master divided by `SIMPLIFY_DIVISOR` = 4 per axis (512 x 384 for a 2048 x 1536 master), a pipeline constant on every device | the Kuwahara's cost grows with area times radius squared, so a fixed coarse grid bounds a rerun by construction: measured 5 to 11 ms per source at the default radius on the M3 against 1.6 to 1.7 s at master size (`experiments/simplify-cost/`, R4-FRESH-2); the result is upsampled to master size within labels, with the master's own fine detail added back by the weight of section 10 |
| Preview | rendered at master size into the canvas, CSS-scaled | preview equals PNG and MP4 pixel for pixel; block-structured effects (cells, crunch, glyphs) are computed once, on the output pixel grid (RES-1, FIT-6, DET-1); `policy.previewDivisor` 2 is the named fallback (8.2) and marks the preview approximate |
| GIF | long edge 480 and frame delay 10 cs (10 fps) by default, both settable in the Export tab (Q8, settled); rendered at master size through pass 5, box-downsampled to the GIF grid, then the camera stage on that grid with grain and clipping and no crunch, with its own `frameCount(motion, 100 / delayCs)`; a size estimate the job reports and waits on before the owner confirms (section 14, R7-D3) | size budget under 10 MB for 3 s, with MP4 presented as the faithful format (Q8); the loop closes at any delay because the frame count is derived from the GIF's own rate (AUDIT-12, FRESH-10) |
| Loop crop | fixed zoom `LOOP_ZOOM` = 1 / (1 - 2 x `PARALLAX_MARGIN`), about 1.0417, on loops whose effective orbit is above 0, applied as the geometry pass camera's scale, so such a loop shows the central 96% per axis; stills, and loops whose effective orbit is 0, use the identity camera (Q19 a, settled) | settled by the owner (about 4%); `parallaxClamp` keeps every displacement within `PARALLAX_MARGIN` (0.02) per side, which is exactly the margin the zoom hides, so the crop never shows past the frame edge by construction; a loop with no camera orbit has nothing to hide, because `objectMotionBound` keeps every moving object inside the frame, so it keeps the still's framing and a grain-only loop is exactly the still with moving grain (Q19, R4-FRESH-6); both constants and `effectiveOrbit` live once in `core/motion.ts`, because the rounded 1.04 of the earlier text hid only 0.0192 per side (R3-AUDIT-6); because the zoom is a camera parameter, the G-buffer already holds the cropped view at master size and no later pass resamples grain, cells, or crunch blocks (FRESH-2) |

Every quality choice is a pipeline constant applied on every device; no runtime budget decision changes the stored scene's rendering (DET-1).
`low` mode and the integer-divisor preview fallback below are transient preview states; export always renders the full pass set at master size with divisor 1.
The 2048 output ceiling on both devices is the owner's decision (Q17, settled; FRESH-6): this table implements it.

### 8.1 Content process (wasm and JavaScript), measured

Planning figures use the Mac Safari process footprints for the phone's binary and thread count (STRONGLY SUPPORTED for wasm, INFERRED for process totals).
A's "about 300 MB per model worker" was Chrome's per-realm metric for the smallest stage; the Safari process footprint for the same worker is 350 to 500 MB, and the encoder is 2.7x larger again (PHONE-1, CLAIM-3).

**The ceiling, re-derived (FRESH-1).**
The only phone kill point on record is the 24 MP MI-GAN run in `experiments/iphone-bench/bench.js`, which passed its first inference and died on the second (`summary-2026-09-28.txt`, run `wspz`, `photo/migan/ort-wasm CRASH@warm:inpaint#0`).
Its JavaScript-side allocations at 24.5 MP, from the source: a CPU-backed 24 MP `OffscreenCanvas` kept at module level (98 MB), the `getImageData` array (98 MB), a CHW copy kept as `orig` (73 MB), the mask (24 MB), the result copy (73 MB as uint8), and the RGBA `out` (98 MB), about 464 MB beside the 557 MB wasm high-water, about 28 MB of model bytes, and a page baseline of about 60 MB, so the first inference touched about 1.1 to 1.2 GB (1.2 to 1.3 GB if the source `ImageBitmap` was CPU-backed rather than in the GPU process, UNKNOWN) and passed.
The second inference allocated a fresh `getImageData` array, CHW copy, and `out` (269 MB) while `orig`, the previous `out`, and uncollected garbage were still alive, so it touched about 1.4 to 1.5 GB and died.
The kill point therefore lies somewhere in **about 1.15 to 1.5 GB of touched content-process memory** (INFERRED: the arithmetic is from the source, the garbage collector's timing is not observable), not "near 1.3 to 1.5".
Two further facts widen the band: the encoder's 1055 MB is macOS RSS while jetsam uses `phys_footprint`, which ran 22 to 45% below RSS for the depth stage (`experiments/worker-release-footprint/README.md`: RSS 611 to 637 MB against footprint 343 to 496 MB), so the encoder stage is about 0.65 to 0.9 GB in footprint terms (INFERRED from that ratio); and the bench ran ORT on the main thread, so the design's topology, ORT in a dedicated worker with nested pthread workers, has never run on the phone.
Read together: the encoder estimate's RSS figure touches the bracket's lower end, and its footprint figure leaves 0.25 to 0.85 GB of margin.
Which reading is right is the first thing P1 measures, by headroom rather than by absolute values (no phone API reports the footprint): the sequence worker runs the encoder beside a **memory ballast** stepped in 100 MB increments, and the largest ballast that survives ten runs is the margin.
The ballast must be written, not merely allocated (R3-AUDIT-1): a large typed array's pages are zero-fill-on-demand and add nothing to `phys_footprint` until they are touched (VERIFIED on the Mac under JavaScriptCore, `experiments/ballast-footprint/`: 512 MB allocated and untouched left the footprint at 4.8 MB, and touching each page once brought it to 518 MB; INFERRED for iOS), so an untouched ballast of any size would survive and report a margin that does not exist.
Each step is therefore a list of 100 MB `Uint8Array` chunks, filled with incompressible pseudo-random words before the encoder starts and summed after it finishes, with the chunk count and the checksum in the harness row, so the pages are resident for the whole stage and the buffer provably stayed alive.
The same ballast with no model loaded, stepped until the tab reloads, measures the kill point itself and replaces the inferred bracket with a number (section 20, U1).
Two rules keep the headroom number honest (R4-AUDIT-2).
The harness page holds none of what the app's main thread holds (the master bitmap, planes, asset bytes including a kept source, the document, and later 17 MB of embeddings: the main-thread row below), so every headroom row runs with a **main-thread stand-in**, a filled and checksummed 100 MB ballast on the page's main thread held from before the sequence starts; the recorded headroom is then already net of the app's own main-thread set, and the 300 MB line of U1 is read against it as it stands.
And in every ballast row, calibration and headroom alike, a step whose chunk allocation throws is recorded as `throw at N MB`: it is neither a survival nor a kill, and the step below it is the last one that counts.

| Concurrent set | wasm high-water | process footprint or RSS | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Import with decode-time resize: 2048 canvas plus PNG encode | none | about 40 MB transient (INFERRED); 96 MB (24 MP) or 192 MB (48 MP) more on the fallback path | phone-lens probe; arithmetic |
| Main thread idle: master bitmap, planes, asset bytes (about 15 MB), document, UI, Preact; once the encoder has run, the scene's embeddings (17 MB) and the 1024 px guide (2.4 MB) | none | about 75 MB, about 95 MB with embeddings (INFERRED) | A section 8; FRESH-5; R3-AUDIT-2 |
| Sequence worker, depth stage, any aspect under the token bound | 290 to 348 MB at 4:3; 277 to 292 MB at 3:2, 16:9, 2.65:1, and 4:1 | 343 to 496 MB footprint, 507 to 637 MB RSS (Mac Safari, 4:3) | phone-lens, worker-release-footprint, depth-token-bound |
| Sequence worker, encoder stage (budget-defining) | 794 to 796 MB | 890 MB RSS isolated, 1055 MB RSS in sequence (Mac Safari), 987 MB (Chrome); about 0.65 to 0.9 GB footprint (INFERRED from the depth stage's ratio) | phone-lens; FRESH-1 |
| Sequence worker, MI-GAN at 2048 | 474 MB | 659 MB RSS (Chrome) | phone-lens |
| Resident decoder | 76 to 91 MB | about 100 to 150 MB (INFERRED) | phone-lens |
| Inpaint-only sequence with the decoder resident and the renderer in full mode (rule 1 exemption; reached when the owner returns to the Objects tab during a refill) | 474 + 91 MB | about 0.8 GB RSS (INFERRED: 659 plus 100 to 150), below the encoder's own 1055 MB; P1 measures the set (U1) | AUDIT-2; R3-AUDIT-4; R3-FRESH-5 |
| Peak during import analysis | encoder stage plus main thread | about 1.1 to 1.2 GB RSS-equivalent, 0.7 to 1.0 GB footprint-equivalent (both INFERRED) against a kill point of 1.15 to 1.5 GB (INFERRED) | derived |

Levers on the content-process peak: the sequence worker and the no-overlap rule (in the design), and a smaller encoder (Q9, open); nothing else.
Thread count and ORT session options are not levers (measured: 791 to 796 MB at 1, 2, and 4 threads, no option below 794 MB).
`low` render mode and no GL context during the encoder act on the **GPU process** and on system-wide memory pressure, not on the content process, because WebKit hosts WebGL in its GPU process on iOS (8.2); P1 measures three GL conditions (a full-mode texture ballast, a low-mode texture ballast, and no GL context) so the phone constant is measured, not assumed (FRESH-3 corrects the round-1 ranking).
`modeDuringEncode` is set to `full` or `low` from those rows; "no GL context" is a measurement and not a policy value, because shipping it would mean showing the plain photo for the whole import sequence, which is a product change: if it is the only condition that survives, it goes to the owner as a row of Q9's memo (R3-FRESH-5).
P1's headroom row has three outcomes (R5-FRESH-6): the encoder fails with the sequence worker in place, where no constant saves it and Q9 goes to the owner with the numbers; it survives with at least about 300 MB of headroom, and the design proceeds as written; or it survives with less, and Q9 also goes to the owner, with the measured headroom and the same memo, while P3c waits for the answer.
A thin margin means occasional foreground kills in the field and scenes that end `blocked-after-crashes`, and whether to ship that is the owner's call, not a constant.

### 8.2 GPU process (WebGL2), arithmetic at 2048 x 1536

WebKit hosts WebGL in its GPU process on iOS (STRONGLY SUPPORTED), which has its own kill threshold (reports of 316 to 522 MB highwater kills, device and context unknown), so this table is budgeted separately.

| Resource | MB | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| master, simplified master (RGBA8; the simplified texture's alpha carries the texture-edge value) | 25.2 | always in full mode |
| refined depth (R16F), plate depth (R16F), normals (RGBA8 half: surface and background pairs), label (R8); the depth plane and the filled background depth at depth resolution (R32F, read only with `texelFetch`, the normals pass's inputs, R7-1) | 6.3 + 6.3 + 3.1 + 3.1; 1.4 + 1.4 | after depth |
| plate, simplified plate | 25.2 | motion on with objects |
| mask array (R8, 1024 long edge, up to 32 layers) | up to 25 | per object 0.8 |
| simplification working grid at a quarter of the master per axis (per source one RGBA8 downsample with labels; two RGBA16F tensor targets and two RGBA8 ping-pong shared) | 6.3 with two sources | while a simplification reruns; released by the pool's ordinary rule after (31.5 in round 3, when the filter ran at master size) |
| G-buffer (color RGBA8, depth R16F, surface RGBA8: id, normal, texture-edge value; and frame RGBA8, the fragment's frame UV that pass 2 reads) | 31.5 | per frame; 9.4 more than an R8 id, the price of registration by construction (R3-AUDIT-3); the frame target lives only from pass 1 to pass 2 of one render and comes from the pool key of the two ping-pong targets, which pass 3 takes after it returns, so it adds nothing to the frame's peak (9.3, R7-14) |
| variant target (RGBA8: entry, band, tone) and the cell table (two RGBA8 at cell resolution) | 12.6 + at most 0.7 | symbols on (R4-FRESH-3) |
| lit and ray sums (RGBA16F half, RGBA16F quarter: contribution and its light-weighted a and b) | 7.9 | lights on; half float so pass 3's tint is the arithmetic of `core/color.ts` on the same numbers (R6-DEF-3, section 10 Lighting): 4.0 more than RGBA8 targets, whose 8-bit step over the sums' range would put the written hue 3 to 4 degrees off, and 6.9 more than two R8 targets, which could not carry two lights' colors (R4-FRESH-10) |
| two RGBA8 ping-pong targets | 25.2 | per frame |
| crunch intermediates: two R16F at master size for luma, two RG16F at half size for chroma | 12.6 + 6.3 | per frame while `crunch` is above 0, full mode only; the DCT's coefficients are signed and reach about 8, which 8-bit targets cannot hold at a step finer than the quantizer (R5-FRESH-5) |
| canvas back buffer (compositor holds up to two more) | 12.6 to 37.8 | always; `resident()` files the one back buffer under its own key, `canvas`, apart from the pool's `RGBA8 2048x1536`, so a count of master-size targets can take it or leave it (5.6, R7-27) |
| **Full mode total, everything on** | **about 213 to 238** | target at most 225 for the renderer's own residency, which is about 213: the pool plus one back buffer, what `resident()` reports; the top of the range adds the compositor's two copies of the canvas and is 13 MB over only with all 32 mask layers in use (R5-FRESH-5, R6-DEF-3); round 7's audit pass added the two R32F depth textures, 2.8 MB, and nothing for the frame target (R7-1, R7-14) |
| **Low mode: master, two ping-pong, canvas** | **about 50**, up to 76 with the compositor's copies | passes 3, 6 without crunch, and 7, so no crunch intermediates; phone only, while an encoder run is planned or its sequence is alive, per `policy.modeDuringEncode` (JUDGE-A-14, JUDGE-B-5, FRESH-3, R4-FRESH-1) |
| Export | same as full mode; the encoder pipeline's frames live in the GPU process on WebKit and are uncounted here | P8 measures on the phone (PHONE-5) |

Budget rules the implementation must keep: the renderer allocates through a pool keyed by (format, size) and releases whatever the latest render and the live caches do not reference; `resident()` reports the pool by key and the back buffer under `canvas` (5.6, R7-27); export keeps at most two frames queued in the encoder; no full-resolution photo bitmap survives import.

**The integer-divisor fallback (FIT-6, AUDIT-1).**
If the render gate (U5b: the phone runs of P3b, P5, P6a, and P7) shows the phone cannot sustain the full pass set at master size within the 25 ms frame target, `policy.previewDivisor` becomes 2 on the phone: passes 1 to 6 run at half the master size (a quarter of the fill), and Present upscales by nearest-neighbour 2x into the master-size canvas so cells and grain stay crisp.
Export ignores the divisor and renders at master size, so the exported pixels never change; the preview's block effects are then twice their exported size, which is what the UI's "preview is approximate" line means.
The divisor is an integer so cell and block boundaries stay aligned to the master grid; an arbitrary scale was rejected (FIT-6).
The divisor does not reach the simplify stage, which runs in texture space on its own fixed working grid (9.2): its cost is bounded by construction, measured on the Mac, and measured on the phone by P1.5 before P3b commits it (U5b, R4-FRESH-2).
Round 3's half-resolution rule for slider drags is gone with the policy field that carried it.
## 9. Renderer

### 9.1 Surface: WebGL2 through an in-repo harness

The renderer uses WebGL2 through `render/gl.ts` (about 400 lines: program cache, framebuffer pool keyed by format and size, ping-pong pairs, fullscreen quad, grid meshes, uniform blocks, residency accounting).
Every pass is vertex and fragment work: two mesh draws, fullscreen filters, a short ray march, a radial blur, a separable DCT; nothing needs compute.

WebGPU lost on these grounds (JUDGE-A-20, JUDGE-B-15 corrected the challenger's rationale): iOS 26 is WebGPU's first iOS release; GPU-process highwater kills are reported without an API attribution; a device created without `requiredLimits` gets defaults that trap compute-heavy passes (PHONE-7); the pass list needs no capability WebGL2 lacks; and a WebGL2 renderer is the cheapest reversal if WebGPU misbehaves, whereas the reverse is A's most expensive reversal.
The ORT WebGPU execution-provider crash is not a rendering fact and is not cited.
WebGPU stays the named upgrade path; the pass list is written so that porting is a shader-language change, not a pass change.

```ts
export const CAPABILITIES: Capabilities = {
  required: [['EXT_color_buffer_float', 'EXT_color_buffer_half_float']],   // either makes R16F, RG16F, and RGBA16F color-renderable in WebGL2, and every float target is 16-bit (R7-F1)
  optional: ['KHR_parallel_shader_compile', 'EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2'],
  renderable: [
    { name: 'gbuffer', attachments: ['RGBA8', 'R16F', 'RGBA8', 'RGBA8'] },   // pass 1's MRT: color, depth, surface, frame (R7-14)
    { name: 'RGBA16F', attachments: ['RGBA16F'] },                        // the lit and ray sums, the Kuwahara's tensor targets
    { name: 'R16F', attachments: ['R16F'] },                              // refined and background depth, the crunch's luma planes
    { name: 'RG16F', attachments: ['RG16F'] },                            // the crunch's chroma planes
  ],
  limits: { MAX_DRAW_BUFFERS: 4, MAX_COLOR_ATTACHMENTS: 4, MAX_TEXTURE_SIZE: 2048, MAX_ARRAY_TEXTURE_LAYERS: 32, MAX_FRAGMENT_UNIFORM_VECTORS: 256 },   // 4 and 4 are WebGL2's own minimums, so the frame attachment refuses no WebGL2 context (R7-14)
};
```

`createRenderer` calls `getExtension` on each required entry's names in order and fails `missing-extension` when none returns an object, because WebGL2 makes the 16F formats color-renderable only once one of those extensions has been enabled by `getExtension`, not when it is merely listed (WebKit enforces that for renderbuffers and is lenient with textures on iOS, which nothing here relies on); it then builds each `renderable` framebuffer and fails `incomplete` with its name unless `checkFramebufferStatus` answers `FRAMEBUFFER_COMPLETE`, and fails `limit` with the name when a limit is short (PHONE-7, TEST-15, R7-F1).
In every case it reports what it found (`GlReport`, 5.6), which the environment snapshot carries; P1.5's probe, which runs before `createRenderer` exists (P3a.2), writes a record in the same shape on the phone with the calls this section names over this table, which R4 checks once the type exists (R7-10): both float extensions, `EXT_float_blend`, `OES_draw_buffers_indexed`, every completeness result, and `IMPLEMENTATION_COLOR_READ_FORMAT` and `_TYPE` with an R16F attachment bound.
Accepting either extension is a hedge, not a correction: the interrogation's claim that iOS Safari lacks `EXT_color_buffer_float` was refuted by all three verifiers (WebKit's source exposes it whenever ANGLE's Metal backend reports it, Apple's own triage of WebKit bug 240919 added it to that backend in 2022, and web3dsurvey lists it on 100% of its iOS WebGL2 samples; section 2), but every float target here is 16-bit, so requiring the 32-bit extension bought nothing, and the half-float one is the extension Apple and Khronos name for 16-bit targets.
Two rules, which every pass keeps, make either extension sufficient and keep 32-bit data out of every render target.
**No pass blends into a float target**: every blend is an RGBA8 blend.
This is this design's own choice and not a platform limit: `EXT_float_blend` governs blending into 32-bit float targets only, and blending into the 16-bit targets is allowed under either float color-buffer extension (`verification.md` 2.2 found it legal on Chrome); the rule is kept for simplicity, and so that no device ever needs `EXT_float_blend`, absent on iPhones below A17 Pro, should a 32-bit target ever be added (R7-17).
**No 32-bit float texture is filtered or rendered to**: the f32 depth plane and the filled background depth are uploaded as R32F textures and read only with `texelFetch`, so `OES_texture_float_linear`, absent on the same iPhones, is never needed, and no pass writes a 32-bit target, so the 32-bit half of `EXT_color_buffer_float` is never needed either (R7-F1, R7-1).
Pass 1's one geometry path (9.3, R7-F2) rests on its own reasons: it depends on no extension, it is one path on every device, and so every Mac golden runs the phone's path.
The one-draw variant that `OES_draw_buffers_indexed` would allow would also blend into the R16F depth attachment as a selector, which the first rule declines, but that follows from this design's choice and is not why the variant lost (R7-17).
The context is created with `alpha: false`, `antialias: false`, `premultipliedAlpha: false`, `preserveDrawingBuffer: true` (exports read the canvas after a render).

**One renderer per page (R4-FRESH-13).**
WebGL programs belong to one context, and a context belongs to one canvas, so "compiled on the Library screen" and "created by each session on the editor's canvas" cannot both hold; round 3 said both.
The app shell calls `createRenderer` once, at boot, on a canvas it owns for the page's lifetime, and hands the resulting `Renderer` to every session through `SessionDeps`; the Editor screen mounts `renderer.canvas`.
Every program is compiled there, while the Library is showing, with `KHR_parallel_shader_compile` where present and the low-mode programs (quantize, camera stage, present) first, so shader compilation is paid once per page, during the seconds the owner spends on the Library screen and in the photo picker, and not inside the 2 s first-look target (FIT-11); a first import that still arrives before compilation has finished waits for the low-mode programs only.
A session calls `load` at open and again whenever the open scene's assets change, and `unload` at close; `unload` drops every scene texture and target and keeps the programs, and `dispose` exists for page teardown and tests.
While no scene is loaded the canvas is 1 x 1, so the idle context holds programs and no master-size back buffer.
Context loss is the renderer's to handle either way (9.3); on restore it recompiles, logs `render.compiled` again, and the scheduler reloads the open scene if there is one.
A renderer owned by each session lost on the compile cost at every open, on context churn across scene switches, and on having to measure that compile inside the first-look target; `render.compiled` is logged from P3a.2 on, and P3b.4, the first package where the low-mode programs all exist, records it on the phone.

### 9.2 Derived textures, texture space, cached by input identity

Built by `load` and rebuilt piecewise when a later `load` or a render brings a changed input; the cache key is the tuple of asset ids and the settings that shaped the texture, and for everything that depends on labels also the layer order (documents are immutable, so identity changes exactly when values do; R7-D7).

- Refined depth: the f32 plane uploaded at model size, joint-bilateral upsampled to master size guided by the master (two passes, radius 4, sigma from the depth histogram), written R16F.
  The uploaded plane stays on the GPU as an R32F texture, read only with `texelFetch`, because the normals below take their slopes from it and not from the 16-bit copy (R7-1).
- Background depth: the union hole (`derive.holeMask`'s rule at the depth map's size) filled on the CPU, in f32, at depth resolution, uploaded as a second R32F texture read only with `texelFetch`, then upsampled like the refined depth, written R16F; the plate mesh reads it and it is the shadow receiver behind objects (RENDER-1).
  The fill is a plane plus a filled residual, per connected component of the hole (R7-32), by the pure `fillHole(depth: Plane<'f32'>, hole: Plane<'u8'>): Plane<'f32'>` of `render/fill.ts`, tested under node.
  Each component's plane is the least-squares fit `z = a + b x + c y`, with `x` and `y` in depth texels, over the known depth texels of a ring 4 texels wide around the component; the residual, the depth minus that plane over the known texels of the component's bounding box grown by 8 texels, is filled into the component by 8 push-pull pyramid levels restricted to that box; the plane is added back, and the result is clamped to 0..1.
  Components whose grown boxes overlap are merged first and share one plane; a ring with fewer than 3 known texels, or whose fit is singular, takes the constant plane at the ring's mean, which is push-pull alone; a hole with no known texel anywhere fills with the median of `depth` over all its texels, which is the product's `stats.median` (5.2), computed by `fillHole` itself.
  The plane is what makes the fill exact wherever the surroundings are planar, so the background pair inside the hole, and in the ring of the hole's dilation around an object where the back layer shows in the still, is that surface's normal; push-pull alone reproduces only a constant, which put the pair off by a median of 8 to 19 degrees in that ring around a disc on planes tilted 17 to 45 degrees (`reviews/round7.md` part 7, noticed item 1; VERIFIED arithmetic for a box pull and a bilinear push).
  The fill runs on the CPU because a GPU pyramid would write its levels into float targets, 16-bit ones losing the precision the normals need and 32-bit ones being forbidden (9.1); a fill at 686 x 518 took about 7 ms on the M3 in Node 25 (VERIFIED in round 7's audit pass, `reviews/round7.md` part 6), it runs once per depth product or change of the hole, and its phone time is INFERRED to sit well inside the simplify stage's rerun class and is measured by P7.1's render-gate row (R7-1), which reads it from the `render.derived` event that `load` logs for each fill (5.10, R7-23).
  Its upload is the one a change of the depth, or of the masks while depth exists, adds beside the changed asset from P6a.1 on, where the fill lands; a plate change uploads the plate alone (5.6, R7-29).
- Normals: one RGBA8 texture at half size holding two pairs, the surface pair in R and G and the background pair in B and A (R7-22), each the x and y of a unit normal whose z is rebuilt as `sqrt(1 - x^2 - y^2)` (a height field's normal always faces the camera).
  Each component `v` is stored as the byte `round(127 v) + 127`, 0 to 254, so a flat component stores 127 exactly and float noise around zero cannot flip it between two bytes; the G-buffer's surface attachment carries the pair in the same encoding (R7-1).
  The **surface pair** is the normal of the height field `z = relief * d` in the lighting space of section 10 Lighting, where `x` and `y` are measured in long edges of the master: with `sx` and `sy` the slopes of the depth along x and y in disparity per long edge, `N = normalize(-relief * sx, -relief * sy, 1)` (R7-D1).
  The slopes come from 32-bit data and never from the 16-bit refined depth, whose half-float step at disparity 0.25 to 0.5 (0.000244) is more than three times what the 0.9 tile of P7.1, at a slope of 0.138 per long edge, changes per master texel; differenced from that copy, the tiles read `N.L` up to 0.08 off, 0.14 with a 16-bit smoothed copy, and gentle surfaces speckled at the `banded3` thresholds (round 7 part 5 item 1, section 2, R7-1).
  **The slope rule (R7-1).** One pass writes the normals texture, and for each of its texels, at frame point `f`, it reads the f32 depth plane (the refined-depth bullet above) with `texelFetch` at depth resolution, where `f` lies at `q = (f.x w - 0.5, f.y h - 0.5)` for a `w x h` plane.
  Of the 7 x 7 depth texels around the texel nearest `q` (clamped into the plane), it counts those inside the plane that carry the output texel's label, each depth texel's label and the output texel's own being the label texture's by the integer rule of the simplify stage below (`floor((2k + 1) * labelSize / (2 * size))` per axis, for texel `k` of a texture of `size` texels).
  A counted texel weighs `(1 - r^2 / 12.25)^2` for its distance `r` from `q` in depth texels, below 3.5, times `(1 - (dd / 0.05)^2)^2` for `dd`, below 0.05, the difference of its depth from that of the counted texel nearest `q` (ties in row-major order), so a texel across a depth step of 5% of the range, the outline's own threshold, drops out, which is what the earlier text's bilateral smoothing was for.
  The slopes `(sx, sy)` are those of the weighted least-squares plane through the counted texels placed at their centers in the lighting space, texel `(i, j)` of a plane over a `W x H` master at `((i + 0.5) W / (w L), (j + 0.5) H / (h L))` with `L = max(W, H)`, from centered sums and a 2 x 2 solve in f32.
  They therefore come out in disparity per long edge at any plane size, including a depth plane whose texels are not square in long edges (686 x 518 for a 4:3 master), where one long-edge pitch for both axes would miss the y slope by 0.7%.
  Where fewer than three texels carry weight, or the solve's determinant is at or below `0.05 (S p^2)^2` for the weight sum `S` and the plane's x pitch `p` in long edges (a window one texel wide measures 0, two texels wide 0.42, a full window 2.3), the pair is the flat normal (0, 0, 1).
  On a plane the fit is exact whatever its window holds, so it is a smoothing differentiator over a baseline of up to six texels that stays exact beside a label edge, where a smoothed copy differenced across the edge is biased: it is R7-1's preferred mechanism with its smoothing and its differencing done by one estimator, which the simulation of `reviews/round7.md` part 6 chose over smoothing and then differencing (section 21).
  The **background pair** is the same rule over the filled background depth (the background-depth bullet above, the second R32F texture), with every texel counted, since its hole is filled and it has no label edge.
  Neither pair renders to a 32-bit target or filters a 32-bit texture (9.1).
  `surfaceSlope` in `render/style.ts` (5.6) is the rule's pure reference and `surfaceNormal` the normal's; P7.1's node rows and the tiles fixture's generator use them.
  The units are part of a saved scene's meaning (section 10): per-texel differences of a 0..1 disparity would give slopes near 1e-4 and flat light, where per long edge a slope of 1.73 tilts a surface 60 degrees; round 6 said only "scaled by `lighting.relief`", which any self-consistent scale satisfied, P7.1's tiles fixture included, because that fixture is checked by reading its normals back.
  Within about four depth texels (about 12 master px at 2048) of an object's edge, where the depth model's own output blends the object's depth with what lies behind it, no derivation from that depth gives the object's normal; in the simulation the fit was exact again from five texels in, a texel sooner than smoothing and then differencing, and on an edge the depth follows sharply it was exact from one texel in, where smoothing and then differencing stayed up to 1.17 off in `N.L` for four texels and 0.13 off at the fifth, which is the range weight at work (R7-1).
  The fit is label-aware, so the surface pair is rebuilt with the label texture when the layer order changes (R7-D7).
  A change of labels rebuilds the surface pair, a draw that writes its two channels alone (`colorMask(true, true, false, false)`), scissored like the simplify stage's below, to its dirty rectangle dilated by 1% of the long edge (the hole's own dilation) and by the window's reach of 3.5 depth texels.
  A change of the hole rebuilds the background pair over the whole texture (R7-22), which is exact whatever the fill does: R7-32's fill is local to merged components, but a new hole whose grown box meets another component re-fits that component's plane and moves the fill, and with it the background pair, inside the other hole and outside the new one's rectangle (the plain push-pull before R7-32 moved it too: `verification.md` 13.3 item 9 flipped up to 3,983 background-pair bytes in a simulation, and an audit of the round-7 fix pass reproduced it).
  A rebuild scissored to the grown boxes of the components a change touches, dilated by 3.5 depth texels, would be exact as well under R7-32, and is the simpler form of section 20 U5b's first lever if the whole rebuild misses its target.
  That rebuild is one draw with `colorMask(false, false, true, true)`, which writes the background pair's two channels alone and needs no extension because the pass has one attachment, and it reads 49 depth texels per output texel, 38.5 million for a 1024 x 768 texture, about a third of a whole rebuild.
  A mask added, refined, or removed changes both the labels and the hole, so it costs that draw plus the surface pair over its rectangle, 99 reads per texel there; a change of order alone leaves the hole as it was and costs the surface pair's rebuild only.
  A new depth product or a change of `relief` rebuilds the whole texture, both pairs.
  Every rebuild runs inside the first render after the change that needs the normals, as the simplify stage's rerun does (a low-mode render does not), before pass 1 and after the label texture's own rebuild, and that render's `render.frame` reports its time under `normals` (5.10, R7-23).
  The pass reads 148 texels per output texel, 49 depth and 49 label reads for the surface pair, 49 depth reads for the background pair, and the output texel's label, so 116 million for a whole 1024 x 768 texture, about the reads of the simplify stage's whole-frame rerun with two sources at the default radius (INFERRED from the counts of this section); P7.1's render gate measures the rebuild on the phone against the stage's 60 ms rerun target, and a miss goes to the lead with the numbers before P7.2 is dispatched (9.3, section 20 U5b, R7-23).
  Each layer reads the pair of its own depth source in pass 1 (objects the surface pair, the back layer the background pair), so a plate band revealed by motion is lit with the background's normals, at the fill's disparity, and not with those of the object that covered it in the photo (R3-AUDIT-3); a loop pixel in that band therefore need not equal the still's pixel at its frame point, where the still shows the object (R7-31).
- Mask array: R8 texture array, 1024 long edge, one layer per object, keyed by mask asset id; a mask whose `PlaneRef.size` differs from the array's is resampled on upload (13.4 rule 6).
  Pass 1 samples it with a LINEAR sampler and `CLAMP_TO_EDGE`, which is 5.4's bilinear rule on the GPU (R7-6).
- Label texture: R8 at 1024 long edge from the `derive.labelAt` rule (the last layer in `layers()` whose mask >= 0.5, else plate), built from the mask array in the order of the request's `RenderScene.layers`; its key is that **ordered list of (object id, mask asset id)**, so the render that brings a new order rebuilds it, and the normals and the simplify cache keyed on it, before pass 1, including when an `object.depth` product reorders two overlapping objects without any asset changing (R7-D7).
  Round 6 keyed it by mask asset ids alone, so that reorder, which section 15's own recovery path reaches (depth failed, the owner tapped, then Retry or the laptop produced depth), left the old winner in every overlap for the texture-space work while pass 1 drew the new one.
  Its texels are the masks' own, both 1024 long edge at the master's aspect, so each label texel reads its masks at their texel centers, where 5.4's bilinear rule returns the stored byte, and the texture is exact on every device; the master-size id that pass 1 writes reads the masks between texel centers, where GPU filtering is not bit-exact (9.3, R7-6).
  `TestSurface.labelTextureAt` reads it back after a render's rebuild (5.10, R7-4).
  The texture-space work reads it (simplification and the normals' label-aware fit), and no per-frame pass does: passes 2 to 7 take the label from the geometry pass's id, which agrees with this texture by construction because the same masks in the same layer order produce both (R3-AUDIT-3).
- Back-layer source: the master outside the hole and the plate inside it, blended by the hole mask, so lossy or generated bytes never leave the filled region (RENDER-4).
- Simplified back layer and simplified master (R4-FRESH-2): two master-size RGBA8 textures, color in rgb and the texture-edge value of section 10 Outline in alpha, produced by the **simplify stage**.
  The stage filters on a **working grid** of `ceil(W / 4) x ceil(H / 4)` working texels for a `W x H` master (`SIMPLIFY_DIVISOR` = 4 per axis; 512 x 384 for a 2048 x 1536 master, 375 x 282 for the 1500 x 1126 master that import makes of a 1500 x 1125 photo, section 12), a pipeline constant on every device (R6-DEF-4).
  Working texel `(x, y)` covers the master texels from `(4x, 4y)` to `(min(4x + 3, W - 1), min(4y + 3, H - 1))`, so a master side that is not a multiple of 4 ends in a partial block, of two texels since master sides are even (section 12, R7-C), and its **label texel** is `(min(4x + 2, W - 1), min(4y + 2, H - 1))`, which always lies inside the block.
  Per source texture it runs: a downsample to the working grid that averages only the block's existing master texels carrying the working texel's own label, the label of its label texel (`(4x + 2, 4y + 2)` in every full block), which also goes into alpha (R5-AUDIT-2, R5-CA-6); a Sobel structure tensor smoothed by a separable Gaussian; `SIMPLIFY_ITERATIONS` = 3 iterations of a label-aware anisotropic Kuwahara (eight sectors, polynomial weights, the weights windowed to zero at the kernel edge so the result is continuous in the radius) of radius `6 * simplify` working texels, scaled by `1 - subjectClarity` inside subject labels; a signed difference of Gaussians of the result's luminance; and one master-size pass that upsamples with a four-tap bilinear, at working coordinate `(m + 0.5) / 4 - 0.5` per axis with the four taps clamped to the grid, over the working texels carrying the master texel's own label and adds back the master's fine detail by the weight `d` of section 10.
  The downsample is a pass of its own, so the dev-only `TestSurface.simplifyGridAt` can run it alone over the whole grid and return its target, which is how P3b.3's label row is read (R6-DEF-5).
  The downsample averages within a label, in the Kuwahara each tap is weighted by label agreement with the center texel, and the upsample reads only working texels of the master texel's own label (a master texel with no such neighbor keeps the master's color), so objects and the back layer are filtered within their own regions and no background color rides along with a moving object (RENDER-5).
  Every label lookup in the stage is a `texelFetch` at an integer coordinate computed in integer arithmetic: master texel `m` reads label texel `floor((2m + 1) * labelSize / (2 * masterSize))` per axis, which for a 2048 px master over the 1024 px label texture is `floor(m / 2)` (R5-CA-6).
  The closing revision took the working texel's label "at its center", which for a 4 x 4 block over a half-size label texture is the corner shared by four label texels, where a nearest lookup is decided by float rounding and two GPUs could average different sides of an object's edge (INFERRED that they differ there; not measured); `(4x + 2, 4y + 2)` is one fixed texel of the block's central four, so the choice is the same on every device.
  A plain box downsample, the round-4 shape, put up to half background color into an edge texel under the object's label: on a two-color fixture it moved 2,077 object texels toward the background at the default radius, the worst by 49 of 255, where the label-aware one moves none (`experiments/simplify-cost/`, run R5-1).
  The cost is fixed by construction: at most 113 taps per texel over 0.2 MP for three iterations, a few small fixed passes on the same grid, and one master-size pass of about ten fetches, whatever the radius, and no single draw is long enough to risk a GPU timeout.
  Measured on the M3 (`experiments/simplify-cost/`, VERIFIED): 5 to 11 ms per source at the default radius across the round-4 and round-5 sessions and 12 to 42 ms at the maximum; the label-aware downsample's sixteen extra label fetches per working texel are not measurable beside the plain box in the same session.
  The round-3 shape, the same filter at master size, measured 1.6 to 1.7 s at the default radius there, and the round-4 verifier measured it independently at 1.7 to 1.8 s per pass at radius 24; the half-size grid that round 2 named as the contingency measured 88 to 132 ms at the default radius, and a third-size grid 36 to 109 ms at the maximum.
  The quarter grid is the finest integer divisor whose rerun stays at about one frame at the default radius with both sources on this Mac (10 to 23 ms across both sessions, against 16.7 ms for a 60 Hz frame).
  The phone is INFERRED two to three times slower, which puts one source at about 10 to 35 ms at the default radius and 25 to 125 ms at the maximum; P1.5 measures it with the spike's shaders before P3b commits the meaning (U5b).
  This is A's texture-space cache: its key is (`simplify`, `subjectClarity`, the set of subject object ids, the ordered list of (object id, mask asset id) that keys the label texture, and the plate's asset id), so it reruns when any of those changes, including a subject toggle (AUDIT-13), every object add, refine, or remove, and a change of layer order alone (R7-D7), and never per frame (JUDGE-A-5, JUDGE-B-10).
  Only the master is a source until a plate exists, because without one the back layer is the master.
  A rerun takes a **dirty rectangle**, so what it costs follows what changed: the whole frame for `simplify`; the subjects' bounds for `subjectClarity`; one object's bounds for a subject toggle; the union of an object's old and new bounds for an add, a refine, or a remove; the union of the bounds of the objects whose place in the order changed, for a change of order alone (R7-D7); the hole's bounds, on the back-layer source only, for a plate's arrival.
  Every pass is scissored to the dirty rectangle dilated by the combined reach of the passes after it: up to 1.5 kernel radii for each Kuwahara iteration, because of its anisotropy, four working texels each for the tensor blur and the difference of Gaussians, and one for the upsample.
  The working-grid targets are shared scratch, so whatever lies outside a pass's scissor is stale, and those margins are what guarantee that every texel a later pass reads was written in this rerun and that the upsample writes exactly the master texels whose result can have changed.
  VERIFIED in the spike: with unrelated contents left in every working target, a rerun scissored this way to the bench object's rectangle equals a whole-frame rerun byte for byte at radius 6, 12.5, and 24 (0 of 12.6 million bytes differ), and with the margins cut to 30% it does not (597 to 24,659 bytes differ); both results held again in round 5 with the label-aware downsample, and in the round-5 audit with the integer label lookups; P3b carries the same test.
  That rerun took 2 to 5 ms at the default radius and 6 to 9 ms at the maximum on the M3, against 5 to 11 ms and 12 to 42 ms for the whole frame, so a tap in the Objects tab costs an object's area at any radius, and only a drag of `simplify` itself pays for the frame.
  A rerun happens inside the render that needs it, before pass 1, so the canvas keeps its previous frame until the rerun and that render have finished and never shows a half-updated state.
  Round 3 ran the filter at master size over both textures on every key change and halved it for slider drags only, which left every tap, toggle, and release at full cost.
- Glyph atlas: five glyphs (`.`, `-`, `+`, `*`, `#`) drawn procedurally as signed-distance fields at 64 x 64 into one R8 texture; no font, identical on every device.
- Palette LUT: `resolvePalette` (section 10) uploaded as an RGBA8 texture of `entries x bands x (1 + objects)` whenever style, harmony, recolor, or objects change.

### 9.3 Pass list, per render

Sizes are master size unless noted.
A dirty set decides what reruns: geometry when the frame changes with motion on, when layers, motion, or the derived textures change, and whenever the lights pass reruns, because pass 2 reads the frame attachment that pass 1 writes in the same render (R7-14); lights when geometry or lights change; passes 3 to 7 on every render.
A light edit on the still therefore costs a geometry pass per frame, which playback already pays on every frame.

| Order | Pass | Reads | Writes | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Geometry | `layers`, simplified back layer, simplified master, mask array, refined and background depth, normals, motion at `frame`, camera | G-buffer MRT: color RGBA8, depth R16F, surface RGBA8 (id in R, normal x and y in G and B in the byte encoding of 9.2's normals, the texture-edge value in A), and frame RGBA8 (the fragment's interpolated frame UV, `u` and `v` each as 16-bit fixed point `round(65535 u)`, high and low bytes of `u` in R and G and of `v` in B and A, R7-14) | Back layer as a 192 x 144 grid displaced by background depth; each object as a 64 x 64 grid over its bounds displaced by refined depth plus its motion; back to front in `layers` order; the back layer in **one draw** that writes all four attachments with no blending, since it covers every pixel, and each object in **two draws**: depth, surface, and frame with an alpha test at 0.5 and no blending (draw buffers NONE, 1, 2, 3), then color alone blended by mask coverage (draw buffers 0, NONE, NONE, NONE) (JUDGE-A-6, JUDGE-B-9); the alpha test reads the object's mask by 5.4's bilinear rule through the mask array's LINEAR sampler, whose filtering is not bit-exact, so within one mask texel of a 0.5 crossing the id may be either of the two layers the CPU rule gives on either side (R7-6); this is the one path on every device for its own reasons, no extension dependence, one path everywhere, and Mac goldens that run the phone's path; the one-draw variant `OES_draw_buffers_indexed` would allow saves one color-only draw over each object's bounds and would blend into the R16F depth attachment as a selector, which 9.1's rule, a choice of this design, declines (R7-F2, R7-17); every fragment samples its layer's frame-space textures at the mesh's interpolated frame UV and writes that layer's own depth (background depth for the back layer, refined depth for objects), its id (its index in `RenderScene.layers`, 0 for the back layer; the attachment is cleared to 255), its own normal (the background pair for the back layer, the surface pair for objects, rotated by the layer's sway angle), its simplified texture's texture-edge value, and its own interpolated frame UV, which is the registration rule below (R3-AUDIT-3, R7-14); the camera is a 2D similarity (scale, offset): the still renders with the identity camera and zero deformation, a loop whose effective orbit is 0 with the identity camera and its object motion, and a loop with orbit with scale `LOOP_ZOOM` about the frame center plus the orbit offset (Q19 a), so still and loop share one path and one soft edge (RENDER-2) and the G-buffer already holds the cropped view on the output grid (FRESH-2); the meshes cover the full master and no displacement exceeds `PARALLAX_MARGIN`, the margin the zoom hides, so the crop reveals nothing outside the frame (R3-AUDIT-6) |
| 2 | Lights, half size | G depth, G surface (id, normal), G frame, lights, per-id table | lit RGBA16F half and ray RGBA16F quarter, each holding three sums as half floats: the contribution, and the contribution-weighted a and b of the lights' colors (R6-DEF-3) | Section 10 Lighting; skipped with no lights; a scalar tone could not say which way to tint a pixel reached by two lights of different colors (R4-FRESH-10); distances and directions are measured in the lighting space at each pixel's frame point, read from G frame, with each light's `at` converted like a frame point, so the camera moves no lighting (R7-14); the shadow march and the rays run in view space toward each light's screen position, which the geometry pass's `project` function gives at the light's own disparity; the receiver behind objects is the back layer's own depth, which G depth already holds wherever the back layer shows |
| 3 | Quantize | G color, lit, ray, G surface (id), per-id table, palette LUT, tone stats | color; with symbols on, also variant RGBA8 (entry index, band index, tone) as a second draw buffer | Palette entry, band, recolor row, subject clarity, and with symbols off the light tint and its gamut bound, evaluated per fragment (R5-CA-2; with symbols on, pass 5 rebuilds every cell from the variant and the palette, so a light shows as bands only: section 10 Lighting, R5-AUDIT-1); the variant target is what gives pass 5 each pixel's entry, band, and tone (R4-FRESH-3) |
| 4 | Outline | G depth, G surface (id, normal, texture-edge value), quantized color | color | Section 10 Outline; its crease source is computed here, per pixel, from G surface's normals and ids at points a stated span apart, and no texture or channel stores it (R7-24): about 17 reads of G surface per pixel, the pixel's own, four for the direction, and six points between pixel centers, each a filtered read for the normal and one for the id of the pixel it lies in, so about 53 million per frame at 2048 x 1536, which P7.2's render gate measures (INFERRED cost); skipped with symbols on, where every cell is one color or one glyph |
| 5 | Cells and symbols | variant, G surface (id), per-id table, symbols, palette LUT, glyph atlas | cell table (two RGBA8 at cell resolution), then color | Section 10 Symbols; two steps: a reduction at cell resolution, where one fragment per cell loops over that cell's pixels (at most 48 x 48) with small histograms to find the majority label, the majority variant, and the mean tone, in total a few fetches per master pixel per frame; then the draw at master size, which looks up each pixel's cell, its ink and paper in the LUT, and the glyph atlas; passthrough when off (R4-FRESH-3) |
| 6 | Camera stage | color, G surface (id), per-id table, frame, seed, grain settings | color | Grain (its amplitude scaled inside subject ids), then clipping, then 8 x 8 DCT crunch (four separable passes each for luma, on two R16F targets, and for half-size chroma, on two RG16F targets; skipped at `crunch` 0 and in low mode, R5-FRESH-5); on the GIF grid the id is the nearest G-buffer sample at the pixel's center |
| 7 | Present | color | canvas | `master` output: a plain blit for still and loop (the loop's crop already happened in pass 1); divisor 2: nearest-neighbour 2x; `gif` output: pass 5's color is box-downsampled to the GIF grid and pass 6 then runs on that grid (Q8), so Present is again a blit |

**The registration rule (R3-AUDIT-3).**
Two coordinate spaces exist.
*Frame space* is the master grid, where every stored and derived texture of 9.2 lives.
*View space* is the output grid after pass 1's mapping: the loop zoom, each layer's parallax displacement, and object motion; for the still the two coincide.
Frame-space textures are sampled in pass 1 only, at the mesh's interpolated frame UV, the one place where the mapping exists; passes 2 to 7 read only the G-buffer, earlier passes' outputs, the per-id table, and tables that are not in frame space (the palette LUT, the glyph atlas).
Depth, id, normal, and color at a view pixel therefore always describe the same surface point, under the zoom, under parallax, under object motion, and under any deformation added later, and no pass carries inverse-camera math.
The frame attachment carries that point's frame UV the same way, so pass 2 places the point in the lighting space at its frame point without inverting the camera; it uses the coordinate as a position, never to sample a frame-space texture (R7-14).
The round-2 text had passes 2 to 4 read the frame-space normals, label, and background depth at view coordinates, which the zoom alone put off by `0.0385 (v - c)`, about 39 px at the frame edge, with parallax and object motion on top.
Per-object facts come from the **per-id table**, a 33-row uniform block built by `resolveStyle` and indexed by the surface attachment's id (row 0 is the back layer): the subject flag (passes 3, 5, and 6; it also fixes the band count), the palette LUT row (passes 3 and 5), the symbols mode (pass 5), and the shadow thickness in disparity, `thickness` times the object's `bounds.height` in long edges (pass 2, section 10 Lighting, R7-2).
Lights are frame-space data too: for distances and directions pass 2 converts each light's `at` like a frame point into the lighting space, beside each pixel's frame point from the frame attachment, so the camera moves no lighting (section 10 Lighting, R7-14); for the shadow march and the rays it needs the light's screen position, which it takes from the same GLSL `project` function the geometry vertex shader uses (the parallax of that disparity, then the zoom), so the mapping has one implementation and a light stays put relative to the geometry at its depth.

Low mode (`policy.modeDuringEncode` on the phone while an `encode` need is listed or an encoder-bearing sequence is alive, FRESH-3, R4-FRESH-1): passes 3, 6 without crunch, and 7 from the master directly, into two ping-pong targets; no geometry, simplification, lights, outlines, cells, or crunch; the camera is the identity, so the loop shows uncropped and unmoving until full mode returns; with no G-buffer, passes 3 and 6 treat every pixel as id 0, the back layer's row.
On the phone the first look of a new photo is therefore already in low mode and shows palette, bands, and grain within about a second, and the chip says "full look after analysis"; sliders for the missing passes still move the document and take effect when the mode returns to full.
Depth-only and inpaint-only sequences never lower the mode, and the Mac never leaves full mode.
There is no mode without a GL context: P1 measures that condition, and shipping it would be a product change that goes to the owner through Q9 (R3-FRESH-5).
Low mode and the integer-divisor fallback are preview states; `read()` for thumbnails and every export render in full mode at divisor 1.

Device loss: on `webglcontextlost` the renderer drops every resource and reports `recovering`; on `webglcontextrestored` the scheduler calls `load` again from its decoded assets; three losses within `contextLossWindowMs` report `failed` and the editor shows "The GPU stopped responding. Reload noisy." while saving continues.

Frame pacing and targets: the render scheduler coalesces to one render per animation frame; the canvas shows loop frames only while playing, at 30 fps, and shows the **still** (identity camera, zero deformation) whenever playback is not running: before play, after `play(false)`, and for as long as the Objects tab is active, which stops playback; nothing renders while hidden (R3-FRESH-3).
During an export the canvas shows the job's frames and the scheduler draws nothing else (section 14).
A stopped loop is never frozen on a loop frame, so every tap and drag lands on a canvas whose view space is frame space: `viewToFrame` is the identity there and returns null while a loop frame is showing, and the canvas pointer handler answers null by stopping playback and dropping the touch.
The visible cost is a change of framing of about 4% each time playback stops or starts on a loop with camera orbit, none on a loop without one (Q19 a), and that a pause cannot hold a particular motion frame; the owner settled both points (Q19).
Targets checked in their packages are one full frame within 25 ms and a simplification rerun within 60 ms on the phone (both INFERRED for the phone; the rerun is VERIFIED on the M3 at 5 to 11 ms per source at the default radius and 12 to 42 ms at the maximum, 9.2).
These are measured by the **render gate** (U5b, AUDIT-1): each package that owns a pass runs it on the phone and records ms per pass in `render.frame` events against the 25 ms target, so P3b measures the real simplify stage, the quantize and outline passes, and the camera stage, P5 the cell reduction and draw, P6a the two-draw geometry at master size, and P7 the light march, pass 3 with the tint's gamut bound, pass 4 with its crease source (R7-24), a light drag's frame time on the still, which since R7-14 is the geometry pass plus the light march, and the normals rebuild (9.2, R7-1, R7-23); any per-frame pass may set `policy.previewDivisor` to 2 on the phone (8.2).
The normals rebuild is the exception: it runs in texture space, where the divisor does not reach, and is held to the simplify stage's 60 ms rerun target after a depth product and after a tap, its time read from the `normals` entry of the render that rebuilds it and the CPU fill's from `render.derived` (5.10, R7-23).
A miss of that target does not set the divisor; it goes to the lead with both numbers before P7.2 is dispatched, with the two levers of section 20's U5b row named, and P7.1's other rows stand.
The simplification rerun is the one row that runs ahead of its package, in P1.5, because its shaders already exist in `experiments/simplify-cost/`; its working grid is a pipeline constant the divisor does not touch, so what that row can change is a pipeline constant, the iteration count, before P3b lands (section 20, U5b).
P1's placeholder row (twelve fullscreen passes over master-size RGBA8 targets, plus the crunch's shape, four passes over R16F master-size targets and four over RG16F half-size targets) is a bandwidth upper-bound proxy for passes 3 to 7 only and is labeled as such; it cannot see the geometry draws, the cell reduction, or the light march.

Determinism (DET-1): frame indices, not wall time, drive every periodic term; grain is an integer hash of (seed, `floor(frame / grainHold) mod ceil(frameCount / grainHold)`, output-grid cell), so it is bit-identical across GPUs; the still has no loop frame and uses frame 0 of that hash, so the still's grain is loop frame 0's (R5-OBS-5); when `frameCount` is not a multiple of `grainHold` the last grain frame is held for the remainder and the loop still closes, since frame 0 follows frame `frameCount - 1` with an ordinary grain change; every quality choice is a constant; the same scene file renders the same on both devices up to GPU float differences, and the verification plan diffs PNG stills of one file across devices within a stated tolerance.

## 10. Look semantics the stored settings commit to

These rules define what a saved scene means; changing any of them is a change of meaning under the version rule (section 13.4).
Each setting states its zero (TEST-13, LOOK-5).
The math lives in `core/color.ts`, `core/motion.ts`, `core/fry.ts` (pure, tested under node) and `render/style.ts` (settings to uniforms, pure); shaders only apply uniforms (TEST-3).

- **Palette candidates.** The asset's clusters (`analysis.colors`, 16 for any photo with at least 16 distinct colors and fewer otherwise, section 12), plus for each subject object its top `round(1 + 2 * subjectClarity)` color clusters from `produced.color.clusters`, marked pinned.
  Pinned entries never merge; `colors` counts unpinned entries, so the background keeps `colors` hues and every subject keeps at least one of its own (LOOK-1).
- **Palette merging.** Weighted Ward merging in Oklab over the unpinned candidates, deterministic order, nested: lowering `colors` by one merges exactly one pair and leaves the rest in place (JUDGE-A-19).
- **Palette targets (Q4 a).** A palette target names a source cluster (an index into `analysis.colors`) and a color.
  The entry that contains a targeted cluster after merging takes the target as its (L, C, h) exactly, before Bands and in place of Saturation and Harmony, which leave it alone; when one entry contains several targeted clusters the lowest-numbered cluster's target wins, so the result is a function of the document alone.
  Assignment is unchanged, by source color, so the pixels that were the entry's stay the entry's and render the target with their shading as bands; a targeted entry is a harmony anchor like a recolored object.
  The Look tab's swatch strip shows the resolved entries; a tap on an unpinned one stores the target for the entry's lowest-numbered cluster, which is its heaviest, and the target follows that cluster through every change of `colors`, so it survives merges, splits, and a scene-file round trip.
  A pinned entry is a subject's own cluster and holds no index into `analysis.colors`, so it cannot be targeted (R6-2): the strip shows it with its object's name, and a tap on it opens that object's recolor picker, which is the one way an object's color is set (Recolor below, the Objects tab's `object.set` edit), so the strip never has a swatch whose tap does nothing and never needs a second color mechanism for objects.
  The **target in effect** for an entry is the one it renders: the lowest-numbered targeted cluster's; a target whose cluster shares an entry with a lower-numbered targeted cluster is stored, undoable, and dormant until a change of `colors` splits them (section 16), and only targets in effect anchor the harmony (below).
  A target names a cluster index below `analysis.colors.length`, which the reducer and `parseDocument` enforce (5.2, 5.3), so a target can never name a cluster the photo does not have.
  The zero: no targets, and the palette is the merged clusters as before.
- **Saturation.** For an entry at (L, C, h) with the chroma weight `w = clamp((C - 0.01) / 0.03, 0, 1)`: `L' = mix(L, Lcusp(h), 0.6 s w)`, `C' = mix(C, Cmax(L', h), s^0.7 w)`, with the sRGB cusp `Lcusp(h)` (the lightness of largest in-gamut chroma at hue h) and `Cmax(L', h)` evaluated on the CPU; s = 0 keeps the source chroma and lightness, s = 1 lands an entry of weight 1 exactly on `Cmax` at the moved lightness, and for every entry of weight above 0 `C'` is monotone in s with no dead zone (LOOK-2, FRESH-9; VERIFIED arithmetic in `oklab_gamut2.txt`, which tabulates both this rule and the round-1 rule `min(C (1 + 2 s), Cmax)` on five reference colors: the round-1 rule did reach the cusp at s = 1 but clipped there by s = 0.5 to 0.75 for every pale source, leaving the upper half of the slider flat).
  `oklab_gamut.txt` records why constant-lightness scaling alone was rejected.
  The chroma weight is what keeps a neutral neutral (R5-FRESH-1).
  An entry whose source chroma is 0.01 or less has no hue worth the name, so it keeps its lightness and chroma at every `s`; that is the rule's second zero, beside `s` = 0.
  The weight rises linearly to 1 at chroma 0.04, so the rule is continuous in the source color, and every reference color of `oklab_gamut2.txt` (the palest has chroma 0.048) renders exactly as before.
  Without the weight the mix toward `Cmax` gave every neutral entry a vivid color at a hue that is noise: at the default `saturation` asphalt `#464648` became `#5035ae`, a black shadow `#291961`, a cloud `#abb7ed`, and a clipped white `#faeecc` (VERIFIED arithmetic, `experiments/render-review/neutral_rule.txt`).
- **Assignment.** A pixel maps to the candidate whose source Oklab is nearest by `0.5 dL^2 + da^2 + db^2` (lightness at half weight so hue and chroma decide but dark water and light sky still separate), whatever its chroma; there is no separate rule for neutral pixels (LOOK-3, JUDGE-A-10, R5-FRESH-1).
  A neutral pixel therefore goes to the entry nearest in lightness among those of similar chroma: a black shadow and a white sky land in different entries whenever the palette has a dark and a light one, and a grayscale photo has `colors` gray entries spread over its tone range, so `colors` works on it as on any photo.
  Rounds 1 to 4 sent every pixel below chroma 0.02 to the single lowest-chroma entry, a rule written for an assignment by hue direction, where a neutral pixel has no direction; on a synthetic outdoor palette it rendered a black shadow and a clipped white 0.22 to 0.30 L apart and a gray ramp in three or four tones, against 0.57 to 0.80 L apart and a ramp spanning 0.84 to 1.00 L without it (VERIFIED arithmetic, `neutral_rule.txt`); both files clamp each variant to 0..1, and under the band-window rule of Bands the round-5 figures are 0.52 to 0.78 L apart and a ramp spanning 0.84 to 0.89 L at `bands` 4 (section 2, R7-B7).
- **Bands.** Every lightness `L` has a **band window** `[W(L), W(L) + 0.45]` with `W(L) = min(max(L - 0.25, 0), 0.55)`: it is `[L - 0.25, L + 0.20]` whenever that lies inside 0..1, and near black or white it slides to stay inside 0..1, keeping its 0.45 width, so band spacing is preserved (the band-window rule, `decisions.tsv` gate4 row 2026-09-30T19:44:12Z, written here by R7-B7); `s(L) = (L - 0.25) - W(L)` is how far it slid, 0 for every `L` from 0.25 to 0.80.
  Tone `t = (L_pixel - L_entry + s(L_entry)) / 0.45 + 0.55`, clamped to 0..1, so the pixels an entry's bands classify span the entry's window; lighting adds to `t` before quantization; the band index is `floor(t * bands)` clamped, and the variant lightness is `W(L') + 0.45 * (index + 0.5) / bands`, which is `L' - 0.25 + 0.45 * (index + 0.5) / bands` wherever the window does not slide; `bands` 2 is the minimum.
  The window slides on both sides, where pixels are classified (around the entry's source lightness `L_entry`) and where variants are placed (around `L'`), because the window the rule moves is the one this bullet defines, the one an entry's bands span.
  Sliding only the variants would put a near-white entry's own pixels in a middle band rendered up to 0.17 L darker than themselves (a white at 0.97 rendering 0.83 at `bands` 4) and fail a row P3b.1 already carries (a gray ramp spans at least 0.7 L; 0.66 at `colors` 7 and 12 under it), where sliding both keeps every pixel of a gray ramp within about half a band of its own lightness at `colors` 4 and above (0.06 L at `bands` 4) and keeps P3b.1's rows (VERIFIED arithmetic, `reviews/round7.md` B7).
  Variants are therefore `0.45 / bands` apart, at least 0.075, before gamut clipping, which keeps lightness, so `resolvePalette`'s guarantee, each entry's variants strictly ordered in lightness and at least 0.02 apart after gamut clipping, holds by construction however dark or light the entry, and two bands of one entry are never the same color (R4-FRESH-3); the rule applies to every row of the palette LUT, a recolored object's row and a targeted entry included.
  `experiments/render-review/light_tint.py` uses a stand-in where a variant's lightness leaves 0..1: it clamps each variant and spreads the clamped ones 0.02 apart from the clamped end, which reaches only an entry whose top variant passes 1, where the rule slides the whole window as soon as the window passes 0 or 1; at that file's default `saturation` the rule therefore moves the variants of four of its eight surfaces (by up to 0.13 L for the warm white wall, 0.017 for bright green, 0.011 for pale sky, and 0.003 for sky blue, over `bands` 2, 4, and 6) and leaves the other four exactly as the file has them (`verification.md` 13.3 item 1).
  The file's tint arithmetic, the shift and the gamut bound applied to a given variant, does not depend on how variants are placed, so P7.1 reproduces `light_tint.txt` by feeding the file's own variants to the tint function, and holds the variants `resolvePalette` makes under this rule to the row's properties (the lit bands of an entry distinct and ordered, each within 0.005 L of its variant, the hue within 3 degrees), which those variants meet: 0 of 288 cases collapsed and the hue at most 2.2 degrees off (VERIFIED arithmetic, `reviews/round7.md` B7).
  Gamut clipping, here and for the light tint below, keeps a color's lightness and hue and reduces its chroma to `Cmax(L, h)`, the function Saturation uses; the text named no method before (R5-CA-2).
- **Lighting.** For each light: `c = intensity * banded3(N.L) * attenuation * shadow`, `attenuation = 1 / (1 + (dist / radius)^2)` with `dist` measured in the **lighting space**, where `x` and `y` are in long edges of the master (a frame point at fractions `(u, v)` of a `W x H` master lies at `(u W, v H) / max(W, H)`) and a disparity `d` lies at depth `d`, so one long edge, the unit `Light.radius` is measured in, spans the whole disparity range; `t' = t * (lights.length ? ambient : 1) + 0.45 * sum(c) + rays_term`; light color shifts the band's chroma by the lights' own color: with `C = sum(c_i)` and `(A, B) = sum(c_i * (a_i, b_i))` over the lights, rays included by their own term, the variant's `(a, b)` becomes `(a, b) + (A, B) / max(1, C)` (R5-FRESH-2).
  `banded3(x)`, with `x = max(0, N.L)`, is the three-level step `0` for `x < 0.25`, `0.5` for `0.25 <= x < 0.75`, and `1` for `x >= 0.75` (R6-DEF-2): a surface is fully lit within about 41 degrees of facing the light, half lit out to about 76 degrees, and unlit beyond, so a light's facing shows as a cel edge and not as a gradient, inside the tone bands the contribution then moves.
  The levels and thresholds are pipeline constants and part of this meaning.
  A light lies in the lighting space at its `at`, converted like any frame point, `(at.x W, at.y H) / max(W, H)`, so an `at` off the frame in -0.5..1.5 lies beyond the frame's edges by the same rule, and at depth `depth` (R7-14), and `N.L` takes the unit vector from the surface point to the light in that space and `N`, the normal of the height field `z = relief * d` of 9.2, whose slopes are in disparity per long edge; `relief` therefore steepens or flattens the shading without moving any point, and neither `dist` nor the light's direction depends on it (R7-D1).
  Round 6 named "a pseudo-camera space whose depth axis spans one frame width", which is the long edge of a landscape master and would have made a portrait's light fall off by its short edge, and gave the normal no units at all; `surfaceNormal` and `lightDirection` in `render/style.ts` (5.6) are the pure references of this paragraph.
  A light's `(a_i, b_i)` is `c_i * (cos h_i, sin h_i)` from its color's chroma and hue; the color's lightness is not read, and `core/ranges.ts` bounds the chroma at 0..0.25 (R6-OBS-2), so a shift never exceeds 0.25 and every light of `light_tint.py` (chroma up to 0.20) is inside the range.
  **The sums' encoding (R6-DEF-3).** Pass 2 writes `C`, `A`, and `B` as half floats, in RGBA16F targets at half and quarter size, R, G, and B, alpha unused.
  `C` reaches at most 12 (four lights at intensity 2, each with a ray term of at most 1) and `|A|` and `|B|` at most `0.25 C`; a half float carries every value in that range with a relative step of 2^-11, about 0.0001 in a and b at a shifted chroma of 0.2, under a tenth of a degree of hue, so pass 3's tint is the arithmetic of `core/color.ts` on the same numbers and P7.1's rows (reproduce `light_tint.txt` within 1 of 255 per channel; the written hue within 3 degrees of the shifted hue) hold for the render as they hold for the reference, with no separate tolerance.
  An 8-bit encoding over that range would step by about 0.02 in a and b, 3 to 4 degrees of hue at a shifted chroma of 0.2 and more below it (INFERRED arithmetic, `verification.md` 13.3 item 3), so the targets are half float at 4.0 MB more than RGBA8 (8.2); a 16-bit float target is renderable under either float color-buffer extension 9.1 requires, and nothing blends into it (R7-F1).
  The zero: a light of chroma 0 has `(a_i, b_i) = (0, 0)`, so it moves tone bands and leaves every surface's chroma and hue exactly as they were, which is what the default white light does.
  A colored light adds its own (a, b) in proportion to its contribution up to a contribution of 1; beyond that the shift is the lights' contribution-weighted mean color, so it never exceeds the chroma of the most saturated light.
  A gray surface takes the light's color, a colored surface keeps its own and leans toward the light's, and two lights of different colors each shift the pixels they reach and add where both do, which is why pass 2 writes the three sums `C`, `A`, and `B` and not a scalar tone (R4-FRESH-10).
  A shifted color that leaves sRGB is brought back by the **gamut bound** (R5-CA-2): it keeps the variant's lightness and the shifted hue, and its chroma becomes `min(C_shifted, Cmax(L_variant, h_shifted))`, the clipping Bands defines.
  Pass 3 evaluates the bound per fragment, only where the shifted color is outside sRGB, by twelve halvings of a scale on the shifted `(a, b)` between 0 and 1, a pipeline constant that lands within 1 of 255 of the exact bound (eight leave a channel up to 8 of 255 away); the same function in `core/color.ts` is the reference pass 3 is tested against, and a color already inside sRGB is untouched, so the zero above stands.
  The lit variants of an entry therefore keep the lightness order and spacing of the unlit ones, so a lit region keeps its bands, and the written hue is the shifted hue.
  The cost is that a light's color shows only as far as a band's lightness has room for it: fully on midtone grays, less on a surface already near the gamut edge, where a light of the surface's own hue changes little, and hardly at all on a band near white (a white band stays white); how that reads is judged with the rest of the tint at P7 (Q12).
  The closing revision clamped the shifted color per channel when it was written, which moves lightness and hue by a different amount in each band.
  Over every band of eight reference surfaces under three colored lights the clamp brought two bands of one entry within 0.01 L of each other or out of order in 9 of 288 cases, a red entry's two upper bands both written `#ff0000` under a red light among them, and put a sky blue's written hue up to 46 degrees off across its bands under a blue light; the bound does neither: 0 of 288, each band within 0.002 L of its variant, and the hue within 1.6 degrees wherever the written chroma is at least 0.04 (VERIFIED arithmetic, `experiments/render-review/light_tint.txt` part 5, which also checks that the chroma sRGB holds at one lightness and hue is an interval from 0); with the variants of the band-window rule in place of the file's stand-in, 0 of 288 and 2.2 degrees (Bands, R7-B7).
  Rounds 3 and 4 mixed `(a, b)` toward `(A, B) / C` by `min(1, C)`, which under a white light multiplies the surface's chroma by `1 - min(1, C)`: a bright green went to `#85cf7e` at a contribution of 0.5 and to gray at 1, under the light the Lights tab adds first (VERIFIED arithmetic, `experiments/render-review/light_tint.txt`).
  With symbols on, a light shows as bands only (R5-AUDIT-1): pass 5 builds every cell from its majority variant and the palette, so a lit cell takes a lighter band of its own entry and its glyph follows the lit tone, and no shift is applied; cells stay inside the palette, as the Symbols rule promises, and the alternative is in section 21.
  `N` is the G-buffer's normal, and every distance and direction is measured in the lighting space at each fragment's frame point, the mesh's interpolated frame UV, which pass 1 writes into the G-buffer's frame attachment and pass 2 reads (9.3), so the camera moves nothing: under the loop camera every surface keeps the lighting it has in the still, whatever its disparity (R7-14).
  The one exception is the plate band that parallax reveals: a back-layer pixel whose frame point lies inside the dilated hole is lit with the background pair at the fill's disparity (9.2), while the still may show the object at that frame point, so such a pixel need not equal the still's pixel there (R7-31).
  Round 7 had left the earlier sentence that measured every distance in view space beside the new lighting space; under the loop camera view space means the zoomed and displaced positions, which would have changed every distance by up to the 4% zoom and moved a light's facing across surfaces at other disparities than its own as the orbit turned.
  The camera enters lighting only where the screen does: the shadow march and the rays run in view space toward the light's screen position, the projection of its `at` at its own `depth` like the geometry at that disparity, so a shadow stays put relative to the surfaces around it (R3-AUDIT-3).
  Shadows: a 24-step screen-space march from each half-size pixel toward the light's screen position; **occluders are object layers only**, each counting within `thickness * objectHeight` behind its front surface; the background only receives, at the back layer's own depth (the filled background depth, which the geometry pass writes wherever the back layer shows); a slope-scaled bias and a 3-tap jitter widened by `softness` (LIGHT-1).
  **`relief` does not enter the march (R7-2).** Its depth comparisons are made in the lighting space of this section, where a disparity `d` lies at depth `d`: at each step the ray's disparity is interpolated between the receiver's disparity and the light's `depth`, and an object's front surface at that pixel occludes when it is nearer than the ray by at most `thickness * objectHeight`, with `objectHeight` the object's `bounds.height` in long edges, which is a disparity extent because one long edge spans the whole disparity range.
  The bias is scaled by the receiver's slope in disparity per long edge, `-(N.x, N.y) / (relief N.z)` from the G-buffer's normal, which divides `relief` back out, so no result of the march depends on it; `relief`, which moves no point (R7-D1), changes the shading through `N` and never which surfaces are in shadow, and what changes a shadow is the object's `thickness` and the light's place.
  Rays: an occlusion texture (light disc bright, nearer pixels dark) blurred radially in 32 taps at quarter size, added into `t'` scaled by `rays` before quantization and into `C`, `A`, and `B` with the same weight, so shafts render as bands in the light's color (LIGHT-2).
  `ambient` 1 keeps the photo's own shading; 0 lets lights alone shape the tone.
- **Harmony.** Anchors are the targets of recolored objects, in document order, then the palette targets in effect, one per targeted entry, in cluster order (Q4 a; a stored target that a lower-numbered one displaces inside its entry renders nothing and anchors nothing, R6-2); with neither, the one anchor is the hue of the unpinned entry with the largest product of its weight and its chroma weight, where the weight is the entry's share of the photo (the sum of its clusters' `weight`) and the chroma weight is `w` of Saturation, a tie going to the entry that holds the lowest-numbered cluster of `analysis.colors`; the template's sectors rotate to fit the anchors; every other entry's hue moves toward its nearest sector by `strength * w`, so an entry of source chroma 0.01 or less never moves, an anchor never moves, and a photo in which every product is 0, a grayscale one, has no anchor and is left alone; `off` keeps hues.
  The product is continuous in the source color, so an entry cannot become the anchor by crossing chroma 0.01: a nearly gray region counts toward the choice only in proportion to the little chroma it has (R5-CA-1).
  The closing revision took the largest unpinned entry whose chroma weight was above 0, a hard threshold at chroma 0.01, the shape section 21 rejects for saturation.
