| UI | `preact` 10.29.8 (MIT, zero runtime dependencies, about 4 KB) | the surface is two lists with selection, an Oklch picker, twelve sliders plus a macro, per-stage status, export progress, banners, and dialogs; a hand-rolled DOM binding layer with focus and drag preservation would be a 400 to 1000 line framework the project maintains untested (ENG-5); replaceable with vanilla DOM at any time | no framework with a keyed DOM diff written in-repo |
| Schema | `zod` 4.6.5 (MIT, zero dependencies, released 2026-09-13, CLAIM-5) | one schema owns the document's shape and validation, with `z.infer` types so no drift (ENG-8 f); used at the file, store, and worker boundaries; replaceable behind `parseDocument` | `valibot` 1.5; a hand-written parser duplicates every type |
| MP4 muxing | `mediabunny` 1.60.0 (MPL-2.0 file-level copyleft, no obligation for unmodified use; two `@types` runtime dependencies; released 2026-09-25) | supersedes the deprecated `mp4-muxer`; write-only use on our own frames; hidden behind `export/mp4.ts`; the in-repo alternative is a 400-line ISO-BMFF writer whose Photos and QuickTime compatibility (avcC, ctts, edit lists, colr) the owner would maintain | hand-written muxer |
| GIF encoding | in-repo GIF89a and LZW encoder (about 200 lines) plus the sampled Oklab palette | frozen format; the palette code exists for the look; output verified by decoding through `createImageBitmap` | `gifenc` 1.0.3 (MIT, last release 2021; its generic quantizer fights the palette we own) |
| Zip | in-repo central-directory reader and writer (about 350 lines) over `CompressionStream` and `DecompressionStream('deflate-raw')` | a stable specification; the tolerance rules of 13.4 are the part where hand-written parsers accumulate bugs, so the two Finder re-zips of a written scene gate it (13.4); if the reader passes 400 lines, switch to the runner-up rather than growing it (ENG-4) | `fflate` 0.8.3 (MIT, zero dependencies, updated 2026-07-20) |
| HEIC decoding in Chrome | none (Q10, settled) | avoids a 1.4 MB wasm and LGPL-3.0 obligations at release for one narrow path | `libheif-js` 1.23.2 (LGPL-3.0, active) |
| Rendering | in-repo WebGL2 harness (about 400 lines) | two mesh draws and fullscreen filters; three.js or regl would add a dependency larger than the harness | `regl` |
| Type checking (dev) | `typescript` 7.0.2 exact (Apache-2.0) | native compiler with per-platform binaries; its `lib.dom.d.ts` already includes WebGPU (CLAIM-4), moot under WebGL2; 5.9 remains a drop-in for type checking if 7.0 misbehaves, since esbuild does the emit (ENG-7); three projects, one per lib, because `dom` and `webworker` cannot share a program (section 5, R7-A1) | `typescript` 5.9 |
| Node typings (dev) | `@types/node` 25.9.8 exact (MIT, DefinitelyTyped; typings only, no runtime code; approved 2026-10-01) | without it every `node:test` file fails the type check with TS2591, or the tests are type-stripped and never type-checked; only `tsconfig.test.json` loads it (`types: ["node"]`), so the app and the workers never see Node's globals (VERIFIED under TypeScript 7.0.2, section 2) | excluding the tests from the type check |
| Bundling (dev) | `esbuild` 0.28.2 exact plus a dependency-free dev server | one native binary, no JavaScript dependencies; four entry points: the app and three workers (sequence, decode, GIF), all present from P0.1, the workers as stubs until their parts (ENG-8 e, R7-B6); production builds pass `drop: ['console', 'debugger']`, so no bundle esbuild writes carries a `console.` call, first-party or bundled third-party (`mediabunny`'s logger among them), while the vendored ORT files under `/vendor/ort/` are not bundled and are served byte for byte as pinned (section 19, R7-E); the dev server serves `headers.json` so COOP, COEP, and CSP match production, and in dev mode adds fault state and a results sink (TEST-6); its production mode serves the production bundle and nothing else (Q21) | `vite` |
| Unit tests (dev) | Node 25 `node:test` running `.ts` directly for `core`, `analysis/checks.ts`, `analysis/subject.ts`, `photo/stats.ts`, `render/style.ts`, `render/fill.ts` (R7-32), `scenefile`, and the GIF and zip codecs, type-checked by `tsconfig.test.json` with `@types/node` | no runtime dependency; requires `verbatimModuleSyntax`, `erasableSyntaxOnly`, `.ts` import extensions, and no enums (ENG-7, VERIFIED in `experiments/node-strip-types/`); `DecompressionStream('deflate-raw')` exists in Node so the codecs test there | `vitest` |
| Browser end-to-end (dev) | dependency-free harness: Chrome DevTools Protocol against headless Chrome on the real GPU, plus macOS `safaridriver` (Q14, settled) | headless Chrome 154 exposes WebGPU and WebGL2 on `apple/metal-3` from an `http://127.0.0.1` origin (VERIFIED); Safari needs the owner's one-time "Allow remote automation" (TEST-16); the phone runs owner checklists that post rows to the dev server | `playwright` |
| Model weights | Depth Anything V2 Small `model_quantized.onnx` 27.3 MB (Apache-2.0); SAM 2.1 hiera-tiny encoder and decoder `_quantized` 52.6 + 0.4 and 8.7 + 0.3 MB (Apache-2.0 per Meta's release); MI-GAN `migan_pipeline_v2.onnx` 28.1 MB (MIT: `LICENSE-WEIGHTS` added 2026-09-14, issue 25 closed; CLAIM-1) | fetched at build time by `tools/fetch-models.mjs` from the pinned Hugging Face commits used in the bench (`experiments/phone-lens/worker.js`, the `HF(repo, sha, file)` calls), verified against `models.lock.json`, gitignored; the MI-GAN residual is whether the authors could grant MIT given the Co-Mod-GAN teacher's non-commercial license, a legal question the maintainer declined, irrelevant for personal use and the owner's at release (Q15) | LaMa (Apache-2.0, 200 MB) for inpainting if MI-GAN must go |

Rejected outright: `@huggingface/transformers` (hides session lifetime and memory, which this design must control per stage), a runtime model CDN (ties every analysis to a third party's availability and shares the owner's IP), `@webgpu/types` (moot).

## 19. Security, headers, observability

- Photos and scenes never leave the device; the app makes no third-party requests at runtime.
- Untrusted inputs are photo files (decoded by the browser) and scene files (bounded zip parsing, schema, hash checks); scene-file strings are length-bounded and rendered as text.
- `headers.json` is the one response-header contract for the dev server and the AWS response-headers policy (JUDGE-B-16); it carries COOP `same-origin`, COEP `require-corp`, the CSP `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'wasm-unsafe-eval'; worker-src 'self'; img-src 'self' blob: data:; media-src blob:; connect-src 'self'`, `application/wasm` for `.wasm`, immutable caching for hashed files, and `no-cache` for `index.html`.
  ORT 1.30's pthread workers are same-origin module workers, so `worker-src 'self'` should hold (CLAIM-9, STRONGLY SUPPORTED); P0's acceptance runs the real headers against the same-origin ORT bundle and records `numThreads`, and a `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` copy during P1 makes any `blob:` fallback visible without breaking the soak (ENG-6).
  The section 2 spike claims are VERIFIED for a CDN-served bundle and INFERRED for the same-origin path until then.
- Diagnostics: the 500-event ring buffer of `DiagnosticEvent` (section 5.10) plus the environment snapshot, exported as JSON from the Library's Diagnostics panel (a long press on the title) through the share sheet, so a phone failure six months from now arrives with evidence; no event contains photo bytes.
- No `console.` call ships: production builds pass esbuild's `drop: ['console', 'debugger']`, which removes every such call from the bundles esbuild writes, the project's and the bundled third-party code's alike (`mediabunny`'s logger has four, VERIFIED, section 2), so the bundle guard checks those bundles for none (R7-E).
  The vendored ORT files under `/vendor/ort/` are outside that check: they are not bundled, they are served byte for byte as the pinned package ships them, and editing them would break that pin; what they print at runtime is ORT's, and the evidence for a failure is the event ring buffer, not the console.
  Dropping the calls was chosen over scoping the check to first-party sources because the scoped check would ship third-party logging the design does not read, and one build flag removes it.
- The build id shows in the UI, in exported diagnostics, in every scene file's `writer`, in every local record, and in every liveness marker.
- Dev-only surfaces (`window.__noisyTest`, `/dev/fault`, `/api/log`, `/dev/soak.html`, `/dev/checklist.html`, P2d.1's `/dev/import.html`, and the harness build's `--migrations` substitution of 13.4) are excluded from the production build, and a bundle-guard test proves it; the production build refuses `--migrations`.
  The dev server's production mode, which the owner's start command runs (Q21, P0.4), serves only the production bundle, `/models/`, and `/vendor/ort/` with `headers.json`, and has no route for `/dev/`, `/api/`, the fault state, or the results sink, so a dev page added later cannot leak through it.
  What the verification plan needs of them is named here, so no package has to guess (R5-SEAM-2, R5-SEAM-3).
  The fault state set through `/dev/fault` covers `/vendor/ort/` as well as `/models/` (an HTTP error, corrupt or truncated bytes, a stall, a delay) and has a `no-isolation` switch that drops COOP and COEP.
  The results sink behind `/api/log` takes rows and binary captures (PNG renders, exported files) from the phone.
  `/dev/checklist.html` lists a package's phone rows, runs the automatic ones through the test surface, shows NOW when the `stage.start` a manual row is waiting for fires, and has "Clear model cache" and "Simulate two crashes".
- GPU pass timing uses `EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2` where present (Chrome) and CPU-side timing around `gl.finish()` in the dev harness where it is not (Safari); both write `render.frame` events (TEST-7).
  The normals rebuild is timed the same way under its own `normals` entry, in the render that rebuilds the texture, and the CPU fill of the background depth, which runs in `load` and in no render, logs its own time in `render.derived` (5.10, R7-23).

## 20. Empirical unknowns, ranked by how badly each could invalidate the design

| Id | Unknown | Would change | Smallest test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| U1 | Whether the heavy sequence (depth then encoder, and encoder then a 2048 MI-GAN) survives on the phone inside a dedicated worker with nested pthread workers, a topology the bench never ran (it used the main thread), with the renderer resident; where the content-process kill point actually lies; and how much headroom remains above the encoder | **The phone product**: if the encoder fails with the sequence worker in place, no constant saves it and Q9 goes to the owner, whose options change mask behavior or move encoding to the Mac with embeddings in the file (FRESH-1); if it survives with less than about 300 MB of headroom, Q9 also goes to the owner, with the measured headroom, and P3c waits for the answer (R5-FRESH-6); otherwise the `modeDuringEncode` policy and the cooldown constants | P1, run first and read before anything else, in this order. **Calibration** (R3-AUDIT-1): an untouched 1.5 GB memory ballast with no model loaded, expected to survive as it did on the Mac (`experiments/ballast-footprint/`); then a filled memory ballast with no model loaded, stepped by 100 MB until the tab reloads, which measures the kill point itself. **Survival**: the real sequence worker at phone speed, ten times, under three GL conditions: a full-mode texture ballast (about 240 MB of textures, the top of 8.2's full-mode range as round 7's audit pass left it, R7-1, uploaded with data and sampled every frame so they are backed), a low-mode texture ballast (about 75 MB), and no GL context. **The panorama row** (R5-FRESH-4): the same sequence on a 4:1 master, whose depth input the token bound makes 1190 x 294, ten runs, with the depth stage's wasm high-water recorded against the 4:3 figure. **Headroom**: the filled memory ballast in place **before** the encoder starts, stepped in 100 MB increments, ten runs per step, the largest surviving step recorded as the headroom with its chunk count and checksum; every headroom row also holds the **main-thread stand-in**, a filled and checksummed 100 MB ballast on the page's main thread that stands for the app's own main-thread set (8.1), which the bare harness page does not have (R4-AUDIT-2). **The exemption set**: a 2048 MI-GAN stage with the decode worker resident and the full-mode texture ballast, ten runs (R3-FRESH-5). `numThreads` 1 versus 2 as a timing row, not a memory row (PHONE-1, PHONE-6, JUDGE-A-7, JUDGE-B-2). The memory ballast is 100 MB `Uint8Array` chunks filled with incompressible pseudo-random words and summed after the stage (8.1); in every ballast row a step whose allocation throws is scored `throw at N MB`, neither a survival nor a kill (R4-AUDIT-2). Disconfirmation of the risk: headroom at or above about 300 MB with the full-mode ballast and the main-thread stand-in in place, so the number is already net of the app's main-thread memory. **Three outcomes** (R5-FRESH-6): the encoder fails, and Q9 goes to the owner; it survives at or above that line, and the design proceeds; it survives below the line, and Q9 goes to the owner with the measured headroom. The two rows that decide this, the kill point and the encoder headroom, also run from the Home Screen app, whose process limits may differ from a Safari tab's, and the harness holds a screen wake lock and reports after its first kill whether the liveness marker survived and the tab lock was released (R5-SEAM-4) |
| U2 | How fast a heavy worker may follow a heavy terminate on the phone, per stage | `cooldownMs.encode`, `cooldownMs.inpaint`, `cooldownMs.depth`; the cost of a hidden interruption (7.1 rule 7); and, if the encoder gap turns out to be several seconds, whether the cooldown must survive a reload (R3-FRESH-4: the termination record lives in the page's analysis service, so it survives a scene switch, R4-FRESH-4, but not a reload, and a reload mid-encoder can restart the encoder at once; the contingency is writing the termination time and stage into the `noisy.job` slot on `pagehide` and honoring it at startup) | P1: encoder sequence, terminate, MI-GAN sequence after gaps of 0.5, 2, 5, 10, 20 s, ten times each; MI-GAN, terminate, MI-GAN at the same gaps; pass is no reload (PHONE-2; CONFLICTING Mac metrics in section 2) |
| U3 | Whether twenty back-to-back terminate-and-respawn cycles leak on iOS (nested pthread workers) | Release mechanism; `numThreads: 1` lever | P1: twenty cycles of the depth stage with the liveness marker armed; the failure signature is a reload or growing per-stage time (CLAIM-2) |
| U4 | Whether iOS suspension and jetsam behave as designed: watchdog pause inside the grace, cancel after an on-time grace, continuation after a suspension, silent rerun, marker verdicts, `pagehide` on reload, Web Lock release on kill | Rules 6, 7, 9 of 7.1; `hiddenGraceMs`, `hiddenGraceLateMs` | P3c phone checklist, run in the app and not in the P1 harness, because these are app behaviors: P3c.1 wires depth and the encoder as one import sequence, so P3c.3 and P3c.4 can interrupt a real encoder stage (R4-FRESH-12). Import, pull down a notification for 2 s mid-encoder and confirm the stage finishes with no rerun; switch apps for two minutes mid-encoder, return, and record which of the two accepted outcomes occurred, **continued and finished** (Diagnostics show `grace-late`) or **rerun silently with no Retry** (the grace fired on time, or the tab was reclaimed), together with the wall time hidden (R3-FRESH-2); force a background reclaim with Camera and confirm no count; pull-to-refresh twice mid-encoder, reopening the scene at once each time, and confirm no count and no block, recording whether each rerun survived (R3-FRESH-4); confirm `navigator.locks.query()` shows no stale tab lock after a reload (PHONE-3, JUDGE-A-11, JUDGE-B-3, FRESH-7) |
| U5a | Which WebGL2 capabilities the phone exposes (`EXT_color_buffer_float` and `EXT_color_buffer_half_float`, `EXT_float_blend`, `OES_draw_buffers_indexed`, the completeness of the G-buffer MRT and of RGBA16F, R16F, and RG16F targets, `IMPLEMENTATION_COLOR_READ_FORMAT` and `_TYPE` with an R16F attachment, the limits table of 9.1) and the raw fill bandwidth for master-size fullscreen passes | which float extension `createRenderer` enables, and whether the phone can render the 16-bit targets at all; an upper bound for passes 3 to 7; no longer the geometry path, since pass 1 has one path on every device (9.3, R7-F2) | P1.5: capability probe, which writes a record in the shape of `GlReport` with the calls 9.1 names over its table, since `createRenderer` lands at P3a.2, and which R4 checks once that type exists (R7-F1, R7-10), plus 300 frames of twelve placeholder fullscreen passes over master-size RGBA8 targets and, for the crunch's shape, four over R16F master-size targets and four over RG16F half-size targets (R5-FRESH-5), ms per frame, labeled a bandwidth proxy that cannot see the geometry draws, the cell reduction, or the light march (AUDIT-1) |
| U5b | Whether the real pass set sustains the 25 ms frame target, and the simplification rerun and the normals rebuild their 60 ms target, on the phone | For the per-frame passes, `policy.previewDivisor` (master size versus the integer-divisor fallback, 8.2). For the simplify stage, whose working grid is a pipeline constant the divisor does not reach (DET-1): if a whole-frame rerun at the maximum radius is above about 100 ms per source on the phone (INFERRED 25 to 125 ms from the Mac's 12 to 42 ms), `SIMPLIFY_ITERATIONS` goes from 3 to 2, which is a change to section 10's committed meaning and is therefore made before P3b lands and before any scene is saved with it; taps and toggles are not at stake, because a mask change reruns an object-sized rectangle (9.2). Round 3 said "nothing structural" of a filter the round-4 verifier measured at 30 to 180 times over its target on the Mac; the structural change has now been made, and what is left here is one constant (R4-FRESH-2). For the normals rebuild of 9.2, also a texture-space stage the divisor does not reach, held to the same 60 ms after a depth product and after a tap: a miss goes to the lead with the numbers, the `normals` entry and `render.derived.fillMs`, before P7.2 is dispatched, with two levers named, scissoring the background pair's rebuild after a change of the hole to the texels where the old and new fills differ, dilated by 3.5 depth texels (section 21's alternative to R7-22), and a 5 x 5 window, 76 reads per texel against 148, at twice the `N.L` noise and three times the texels in a wrong `banded3` level that R7-1's simulation measured, the second a change of section 10's meaning made before any scene is saved with it (Q18); a miss that is the fill's own shows in its own number (R7-23) | The **render gate**, measured on the phone with `render.frame` ms per pass by the packages that own each pass: P1.5 first, ahead of its package (the simplification rerun with the shaders of `experiments/simplify-cost/`, at the default and the maximum radius, with one and with two sources, read before P3b.3 is dispatched); then P3b (the real simplify stage, quantize, outline, camera stage), P5 (the cell reduction and draw at cell sizes 6 and 12), P6a (two-draw geometry at master size in a 3 s loop), P7 (light march with one light and with four, pass 3 with the tint's gamut bound under a colored light, pass 4 with its crease source, a light drag's frame time on the still, which since R7-14 is the geometry pass plus the light march, and the normals rebuild with its background fill after a depth product and after a tap, R7-1, R7-23, R7-24); the first package that misses the frame target flips the divisor and says so, and a normals rebuild over 60 ms goes to the lead as the other column says (JUDGE-A-5, JUDGE-B-10, AUDIT-1, FRESH-15, R7-23) |
| U6 | Whether `createImageBitmap` with resize options decodes 48 MP at reduced size on WebKit, and whether reading an undrawn image element's natural size holds a full decode there (R5-FRESH-7) | Import path for 48 MP; the fallback's transient; the dimension source of section 12 (fallback: a header parse for JPEG and HEIF sizes) | P1: time and survive a synthetic 48 MP JPEG with and without resize options, reading its size from an image element first, as import does (PHONE-8) |
| U7 | iOS storage: `persist()` grants, the Home Screen "Open as Web App" toggle's isolation and exemption, whether models in Cache Storage are shared, whether the seven-day deletion applies to noisy | Strength of the durability messaging, including whether 13.5's wording for a persisted tab is reachable (R5-CA-5) | Library package checklist: install with the toggle on, report `persisted()` in a Safari tab and in the Home Screen app, whether a Safari-tab scene appears, whether models are cached; leave a test scene untouched for eight days in both modes (PHONE-10, CLAIM-7) |
| U8 | Whether iOS 26 still drops IndexedDB connections after backgrounding | Depth of the reconnect logic | Store package checklist: background noisy for ten minutes, return, edit, confirm the save lands (PHONE-4) |
| U9 | Whether the same-origin ORT bundle under the production CSP spawns pthread workers and uses 2 threads on the phone | `worker-src`, thread count | P0 acceptance with report-only CSP (ENG-6, CLAIM-9) |
| U10 | Whether canvas PNG encode then decode is byte-exact on Safari and Chrome | Master storage (fallback: raw `rgb8` plane) | P2d.1: round-trip a gradient plus noise image through `convertToBlob` and a decode in both browsers and compare bytes (B's U3) |
| U11 | `CompressionStream('deflate-raw')` speed on iOS for a 20 MB plane | Zip writer (fallback: stored entries only) | P1 harness |
| U12 | H.264 grain retention on the phone's encoder at the default `grainSize` 2, `grainHold` 2, crunch 0.42, 28 Mbps (the Mac measured 46.5 to 46.9%, `h264_grain4.txt`) | Defaults (Q7) and the P8 acceptance threshold | P8: encode a grainy loop on the device, decode, compare flat-region standard deviation against the 40% line (EXPORT-1, AUDIT-7, FRESH-4) |
| U13 | `isConfigSupported` at the master sizes (2048 x 1536, 1536 x 2048, and the panorama's 2048 x 512, every side even) on iOS Safari 26; whether `new VideoFrame` accepts a WebGL2 `HTMLCanvasElement` there and makes a good frame (FRESH-8); the encoder pipeline's GPU-process cost during a 6 s export; and whether a decoded frame's flat colors match the rendered frame (R5-FRESH-8) | Whether MP4 is offered at every master size, where a refusal reopens a fallback only as a `RenderOutput` that runs the camera stage on a video grid, never encoder scaling (section 14, R7-C); `policy.frameSource` on the phone (direct, `drawImage` copy, or `readPixels`, R7-D9); export budget; whether the color space must be stated explicitly | P1.5: `isConfigSupported` at those sizes, and a VideoFrame built from a WebGL2 canvas, encoded, and decoded against the rendered frame, which sets the phone's `frameSource`; P8: a full 6 s export with Diagnostics showing no context loss, and the color row of section 14 with `export.video` recorded (PHONE-5, CLAIM-8) |
| U14 | `navigator.share({ files })` with a 15 MB MP4 and a 12 MB `.noisy` on iOS; and the return trip, picking a `.noisy` in iOS's file picker and importing it, which is how every scene comes back from the laptop (R4-FRESH-9) | Share flow (fallback: download to Files); the scene input's attributes (no `accept` filter as written, 13.4) | P1.5 harness button for the share; P2d.3 phone checklist for the return trip: AirDrop a `.noisy` from the Mac, save it to Files, pick it through Import scene, confirm it imports, and record whether the input needed no accept filter |
| U15 | Auto-subject quality from the depth-derived seed on the owner's photos, the seed rule and gate of 5.5 exactly (R7-D2) | Whether the heuristic stays (Q6, settled as conditional on this row); a score threshold only if the wrong guesses are the low-scoring ones | P4: ten of the owner's photos from `.local/photos/`, count correct first guesses, and record candidate 0's score beside each verdict (TEST-17) |
| U16 | Guided-filter refinement at 1024 over SAM's 256 logits on hair and thin structures | Mask resolution or a matting step: a model stage with its own weights, license, and phone bench, per-tap time, and, if masks outgrow 1024, the mask product's resolution (a migration, 13.4 rule 6) and the mask array's 25 MB of 8.2 (R7-F6) | P4 visual check on the same photos |
| U17 | Plate quality in motion after stylization | One plate per layer instead of one shared plate: `analysis.plate` becomes a plate per layer (a format change with a migration), each refill runs one inpaint per layer, the GPU budget gains 25 MB per extra plate and its simplified copy (8.2), and the back layer's composition and the hole rule change (R7-F6) | P6a: five photos, subject plus one animated object, orbit 1, judged after the full look |
| U18 | Depth-derived normals under banded lighting | A normals model stage (additive): a model in the heavy sequence whose phone memory needs a bench row against U1's headroom first, a normals product in the document and the file (a format change with a migration), and normals read from that product instead of derived from depth in 9.2 (R7-F6) | P7: five photos, moving light, judge band edges and shadows; if they fail, bench MoGe-2 ViT-S normal on the phone before any design change |

What can move if an unknown resolves badly (FRESH-1 corrects the round-1 claim that nothing could):

- U1 is the one unknown that can make the **phone product** infeasible as designed.
  If the encoder does not fit with the sequence worker in place, no constant in this design rescues it; the fallback is Q9, whose options degrade or remove tap-to-select on the phone or change the file format.
  A run that survives with less than the 300 MB line goes to the owner under Q9 too, with the number: a thin margin is a product decision, not a constant (R5-FRESH-6).
  That is why P1's U1 rows run first and alone, why Q9 carries a pre-written decision memo (section 23), and why P2a through P3b treat the master size and the cooldown constants as provisional until U1 is read, even though their code does not depend on the numbers.
  No module boundary changes under any Q9 option: the encoder stage is one `HeavyStage` and embeddings are one cache entry, so the Mac-only option is a `reconcile` rule plus a file field, and the smaller-encoder options are a `models.lock.json` change.
- U16, U17, and U18 are judged on the owner's photos, and each contingency changes more than a constant (R7-F6): a matting step adds a model stage with its own weights, license, phone bench, and per-tap time, and may grow the mask product; a plate per layer changes the document's plate product, the refill's cost, the GPU budget, and the back layer's composition; a normals model adds a model stage, a product the scene file carries, and a renderer input in place of 9.2's derivation.
  Each is a format change with a migration once the version rule binds, and the first two touch the phone's memory or time budget, so each goes back through this design's gates before it is built; the round-6 text named U1 alone as able to change the product, which understated these three.
- U2, U4, and U5b have a stated test and a contingency that changes a constant or a policy; U13's changes the phone's frame-source constant or, if the phone refuses a master size, adds a `RenderOutput` that runs the camera stage on a video grid (section 14, R7-C); none of them moves a module boundary.

## 21. Alternatives considered, and what was taken from each source design

Whole-shape alternatives that lost:

- **Per-stage workers, terminate then spawn** (A section 7, B section 6.3).
  Lost to the sequence worker: the peak becomes the largest stage instead of a timing-dependent sum, one release per sequence instead of three, two fewer worker starts on import; the objection to one realm ("wasm memory never shrinks") does not apply to a worker that dies when its list ends (PHONE-2).
- **One long-lived analysis worker holding every model** (B's A1).
  Lost: it would keep about 0.8 GB resident while editing.
- **Crash markers as one per-origin slot, or no crash detection at all** (A rule 8, B).
  Lost: the slot counts reloads and second tabs as crashes (FAIL-1); no detection loops a scene whose encoder kills the tab (JUDGE-A-2, JUDGE-B-1); the lock-tied marker with a background verdict is the smallest mechanism that distinguishes the three.
- **WebGPU renderer** (A).
  Lost on maturity and reversal cost (section 9.1); the pass list needs nothing it adds.
- **Per-frame anisotropic Kuwahara on the warped G-buffer** (B pass 2).
  Lost: INFERRED infeasible at 30 fps on the phone and prone to boiling under motion; A's texture-space cache, made label-aware, warps a fixed simplified texture instead.
- **Read-only second-tab mode** (A).
  Lost: permanent complexity in every control for a two-tabs edge case a refusal covers (ENG-9).
- **Whole-value edits with produced fields inside the replaced value** (B).
  Lost: a slider during an in-flight refine reverts the mask (JUDGE-B-6); patch edits over user fields with clamping keep the reducer small and the race impossible.
- **Undo over document snapshots** (B's implied shape).
  Lost: it strips analysis products and refinements landed after the edit (JUDGE-A-3); inverse edits carry only editable state.
- **Unsaved-asset map in the kernel** (JUDGE-A-4's remedy).
  Lost to the assets-first invariant, which makes the set empty by construction; export from memory still covers FAIL-2.
- **Per-k palette storage** (B).
  Lost: independent fits make the palette slider jump non-monotonically and store 77 centroids (JUDGE-A-19); A's 16 clusters with nested Ward merging win.
- **JPEG master** (ENG-10).
  Lost: identical pixels on both devices matter for analysis and the look; the cost is bytes and half a second at import.
- **JPEG plate** (A).
  Lost: lossy bytes flip band boundaries scene-wide when motion toggles (RENDER-4); PNG plate composited only inside the hole.
- **Dropping unknown document keys with a notice** (rounds 1 to 4), and **passthrough of unknown keys** (AUDIT-11's alternative).
  Both lost, in round 5, to strict rejection (A rule 6, R5-FRESH-9): unknown keys at a known version list the record as damaged with the key paths, and Export raw data keeps every field and asset.
  This reverses the round-1 decision, and the reason is cost against need.
  Once rule 4 of 13.4 binds, the state needs two parallel branches at one version, which that rule forbids, or a bug; drop-with-notice had grown to about ten contract points across five packages and needed a fix in each of rounds 2, 3, and 4; and after round 4 its default was already a refusal, so what it still bought over a damaged listing was one button.
  While Q18's development version is in force rule 4 does not bind, and the state is also what removing or renaming a field produces; there the strict rule costs every existing development scene at each such change, a cost the owner accepted with Q18 (section 23; R5-CA-7).
  The round-1 objection, that refusing turns a parallel-branch record into a damaged scene, stands and is accepted: nothing is lost, because the record is never rewritten and the build that wrote it opens the exported file.
  Passthrough loses for its old reason: it carries another branch's fields through every save for ever, and the schema stops owning the shape.
- **Layers by depth clusters; one plate per layer; stylize once and warp everything; SAM embeddings in the scene file; ORT's WebGPU EP on Chrome; a normals model in v1; JSON with base64 assets or a custom container; OPFS; a bundled glyph font; syncing through a service; native or cloud analysis.**
  All lost for the reasons A's section 21 and B's section 15 give, which this design adopts unchanged.

Alternatives weighed in round 2, and why each lost (`reviews/round2.md`):

- **The 4% loop crop as a present-pass crop and upscale** (round 1, FRESH-2).
  Lost to a camera scale in the geometry pass: the crop-and-scale resampled every grain cell, symbol cell, and DCT block by 1.0417 before the encoder saw them, breaking the design's own rule that block effects live on the output grid; the camera is already a parameter because the still renders with the identity, so the change costs nothing structural.
- **A single inverse `Edit` per edit** (round 1, AUDIT-3).
  Lost to an inverse list: the fry macro and the reducer's compound rules (motion enabled, and until round 7 a focus reset, R7-D6) have no single-edit inverse, and a whole-value `style.set` edit used only as an inverse would have been a second representation of the same state.
- **Measuring fill rate in P1 with a placeholder pipeline** (round 1, AUDIT-1).
  Lost to a render gate measured by the packages that own each pass: a placeholder cannot see the Kuwahara, the geometry draws, or the light march, and P1 cannot run shaders that do not exist yet without becoming a phase.
  Round 4 adds the one exception: the simplification shaders now exist as a spike, so P1.5 measures that row ahead of its package (R4-FRESH-2).
- **Low render mode whenever any heavy worker is alive, on every device** (round 1, FRESH-3, AUDIT-2).
  Lost to a per-device, per-stage policy: WebGL lives in the GPU process on iOS, so low mode never touched the content-process peak; applying it on the Mac and during plate refills degraded the detailed-work flow for no memory reason.
- **Cancelling the heavy job the instant the tab hides** (round 1, AUDIT-5, FRESH-7), and **clearing the marker on hidden with no `hiddenAt`** (FRESH-7's smaller mechanism).
  Both lost to a 4 s grace plus `pagehide` clearing: an instant cancel made a two-second interruption cost the encoder's elapsed work plus its cooldown, and it made `hiddenAt` unreachable; deleting `hiddenAt` would have left a kill during a short hide indistinguishable from a foreground crash, which is the one distinction the marker exists to draw.
- **A separate `exportRawData` for damaged records and no export for newer ones** (round 1, AUDIT-4).
  Lost to one verbatim path: the same writer entry serves newer and damaged records, the newer build reopens the file, and the Library only changes the label.
- **`withSceneLock` with a waiting `hold` mode** (round 1, AUDIT-9).
  Lost: no caller ever wants to wait on a scene lock when the UI's answer is "close it there first", and the waiting mode contradicted 13.3.
- **Exporting scene files from the store while a scene is open** (round 1, FRESH-5).
  Lost to the asset cache: the rescue path for a dead store cannot read the store, and the bytes are already read once to decode them.
- **Reading the ceiling as "near 1.3 to 1.5 GB"** (round 1, FRESH-1).
  Lost to the re-derived bracket of 1.15 to 1.5 GB with the arithmetic shown and a headroom measurement first in P1; the round-1 phrasing implied margin the evidence does not establish.
- **Adding `autoSubject: 'done'` as a side effect of the owner's first `object.add`** (this round's first draft of FRESH-11).
  Lost to a `reconcile` condition (`objects` empty) plus a reducer no-op for a late `auto-subject` produce: an edit that writes an analysis field would have broken the exact inverse round-trip that P2a proves.

Alternatives weighed in round 3, and why each lost (`reviews/round3.md`):

- **Sampling frame-space textures through the inverse camera in passes 2 to 4, with finite-difference normals from G depth and an analytic back-layer displacement** (R3-AUDIT-3's proposed remedy).
  Lost to sampling them once in pass 1 at the mesh's own UV: three separate mechanisms and an inverse mapping in every later pass would each be right only for the displacement it models, while the geometry pass already holds the exact mapping for every layer; the price is 9.4 MB of G-buffer.
- **A fourth G-buffer attachment carrying the frame UV.**
  Lost to writing the resolved id and normal directly: later passes need the values, not the coordinates, and a UV attachment costs 12.6 to 25 MB and another fetch in every pass that uses it.
  Round 7's audit pass added one for pass 2 alone, which needs the coordinate itself, as each pixel's position in the lighting space (R7-14); every texture lookup still reads values written in pass 1, and the attachment, packed into an RGBA8 target that shares the ping-pong pair's pool key and lives from pass 1 to pass 2, adds nothing to the frame's peak (8.2).
- **A frozen loop frame on the Objects tab, with taps resolved through the G-buffer id and an inverse mapping** (R3-FRESH-3's second remedy).
  Lost to showing the still: the inverse needs the depth and the object transform at the tapped pixel mirrored on the CPU, a second implementation of the motion math that must stay in step with the shader, where the still needs none; the owner kept the still (Q19).
- **`decode(scene, input, points)` with embeddings passed on every call, or a separate `ensureDecoder`** (R3-AUDIT-2's two remedies).
  Lost to a decoder handle: passing 17 MB of tensors per tap makes the service compare identities to decide whether to respawn, and `ensureDecoder` leaves "decode with no decoder" representable; a handle obtained only from embeddings makes that state unwritable and removes `waiting` from the service's errors.
- **Keeping the decoder for `decoderIdleMs` after the Objects tab is left** (R3-AUDIT-4's first remedy).
  Lost to release on leaving: it would hold 100 to 150 MB through playback and exports to save under a second on return, and the respawn already starts when the tab becomes active.
- **`no-gl` as a `RenderPolicy` value** (round 2, R3-FRESH-5).
  Lost: no `RenderMode` could express it, and shipping it means showing the plain photo through the import sequence, which is the owner's call under Q9, not a constant P1 may set.
- **A center-prior automatic subject when depth is missing** (the round-1 failure row, R3-FRESH-1).
  Lost: a second, weaker heuristic for a failure path, when the owner's first tap defines the subject anyway.
- **A memory ballast that is allocated but not written** (round 2, R3-AUDIT-1).
  Lost to a measurement: untouched pages never reach the footprint (`experiments/ballast-footprint/`).
- **Cancelling whenever the grace callback runs while hidden** (round 2, R3-FRESH-2).
  Lost to the on-time test: a callback that fires after a suspension would throw away a job the process kept and charge its cooldown.
- **Persisting the cooldown across reloads now** (R3-FRESH-4).
  Deferred, not rejected: it matters only if U2 finds a multi-second encoder drain on the phone, and U4's reload row now records the evidence.

Alternatives weighed in round 4, and why each lost (`reviews/round4.md`):

- **The Kuwahara at master size, with a half-size rule for slider drags** (rounds 2 and 3, R4-FRESH-2).
  Lost by measurement: 1.6 to 1.7 s per rerun at the default radius on the M3, and the drag rule reached neither taps nor releases.
- **A half-size or third-size working grid.**
  Lost by measurement (`experiments/simplify-cost/`): 88 to 132 ms per source at the default radius on the half grid, 36 to 109 ms at the maximum on the third; the quarter grid is the finest integer divisor that fits a frame at the default radius with both sources.
- **A working grid whose size follows the radius, so the kernel stays a constant number of texels.**
  Lost to a fixed grid with a detail weight: a grid that changes size with the slider re-phases its samples on every drag tick and churns the framebuffer pool, while the fixed grid reaches the same two ends (the exact master at 0, a bounded kernel at 1) with one size.
- **Rerunning the whole working grid on every key change**, with a scissor added only if the phone's numbers asked for one (this round's first draft of R4-FRESH-2).
  Lost to the dirty rectangle: the phone is INFERRED to need it at the top of the slider with a plate, the per-pass margins are a short reach table with an exact byte-for-byte test that already passes in the spike, and a design that forks on a later measurement would have left P3b's scope open.
- **Listing `auto-subject` only once depth exists** (round 3's conditions, and the shape of R4-FRESH-1's remedy).
  Lost to a plan in dependency order: the plan says what will run, the driver's rule is "perform a need when the needs before it have finished", and one invariant (every need's inputs exist or are listed before it) is testable over every fixture.
- **Ink one band toward the midtones when the cell is in the extreme band, with the paper unchanged** (R4-FRESH-3's first remedy), and **B's fixed paper, the darkest variant for shadow cells**.
  The first lost because it inverts tone inside the extreme band, where the densest glyph would read lightest; the second because it fails the same way as round 3 when the cell's own color is that variant.
  Ink as the more extreme of two adjacent bands keeps density monotone in tone everywhere.
- **Holding saves and skipping the sweep inside an open session while dropped keys exist** (R4-AUDIT-1's first remedy).
  Lost to refusing at open: a session that is open but not saving is a mode every save path would have to know, while a refusal leaves the session unaware and makes the rewrite the owner's explicit act.
  Moot since round 5, which removed the dropped-keys protocol (R5-FRESH-9).
- **`sweepUnowned` with a lock spanning the import and a re-check** (rounds 2 and 3, R4-FRESH-8).
  Lost to one transaction, which makes the state it swept for unreachable.
- **The thumbnail as an asset** (rounds 1 to 3, R4-FRESH-7), and **a document field that references it**.
  Both lost to a record field: a device-local image regenerated at every close does not belong in the content-addressed set or in the scene file, and as an unreferenced asset it was an orphan by definition.
- **A renderer created by each session** (R4-FRESH-13's second option).
  Lost to one renderer per page: it would compile every program inside each open and inside the first-look target, and churn a GL context per scene switch.
- **A single liveness slot holding a set of ops** (R4-FRESH-5's second remedy).
  Lost to one slot per worker kind: each worker clears its own key, with no read-modify-write of a shared value.
- **Scalar lit and ray tone** (round 3, R4-FRESH-10).
  Lost: it cannot say which way to tint a pixel that two lights of different colors reach.
- **Moving U4's mid-encoder rows to the Objects package** (R4-FRESH-12's remedy).
  Lost to wiring the encoder stage into the driver in P3c.1, where the import sequence needs it anyway (R4-FRESH-1), so the interruption checklists run in the packages that own the behavior.
- **Leaving `autoSubject` pending after the owner's first object** (round 2's FRESH-11 fix as far as it went).
  Completed, not reversed: the `reconcile` condition and the reducer no-op stay, and the driver adds a `found: null` product, which keeps the edit round-trip exact where round 2's rejected side-effect edit would not have.

Alternatives weighed in round 5, and why each lost (`reviews/round5.md`):

- **A neutral pixel maps to the nearest low-chroma entry by lightness, with Ward merging made to keep a dark and a light neutral candidate** (R5-FRESH-1's first remedy), and **one neutral entry whose band window spans the asset's tone range** (its second).
  Both lost to having no neutral rule in assignment: the full-Oklab distance already sends a neutral pixel to the entry nearest in lightness, so either remedy would be a second mechanism beside one that does the job, and the second would make one entry's bands mean something different from every other's.
  Where a neutral does need a rule is where a hue is used, in saturation and harmony, and one chroma weight serves both.
- **A chroma threshold for neutral entries** in place of the ramp.
  Lost: two clusters of one faintly tinted wall, on either side of a threshold, would render one gray and one at 70% of the gamut; the ramp costs one more constant.
- **Mixing toward the lights' color with the mix weighted by the light's chroma** (R5-FRESH-2's first remedy).
  Lost to the shift: at full contribution a saturated light would still replace a surface's own color, where the shift keeps it and adds the light's; both have the same zero and read the same three sums.
- **Carrying the lights' mean shift per cell through the cell reduction, so symbol cells take a light's color** (R5-AUDIT-1's first remedy).
  Lost to bands only: cells that are palette colors are the Symbols rule's own promise, the spec asks for lighting inside the banded shading, and the alternative adds two fetches per master pixel per frame to the reduction; it stays additive if the owner wants it after seeing the look (Q12).
- **Glyph bins over the whole tone range.**
  Lost: at the default `amount` only `*` and `#` would appear; bins over the slider's own window show all five at every `amount`.
- **Per-case export guards**: holding the encoder while an export runs, deferring products, refusing edits, each as a rule of its own (the shape of R5-FRESH-3's three triggers).
  Lost to one exclusive phase: a single entry condition and a single hold cover the cases found and the ones not yet found.
- **Pausing an export while the tab is hidden and resuming it**, and **the encoder's retry at half the bitrate** (B).
  Both lost: whether an encoder session survives backgrounding on iOS is unknown, so a resume needs a validity check and a restart path where a cancel needs neither; and a silent half-bitrate file misses the grain line MP4s are accepted against, so an encoder error now fails the job and the owner lowers the bitrate knowingly.
- **The depth input's long side scaled by aspect with no bound** (rounds 1 to 4, from the bench).
  Lost by measurement: 1103 MB of wasm for a 4:1 master against 277 to 348 MB under the token bound (`experiments/depth-token-bound/`).
- **Sweeping at close as well as at open, guarded by "the flush succeeded"** (R5-AUDIT-3's remedy), and **a reference set supplied by the caller**.
  Lost to one sweep at open whose reference set the store reads from the stored record in the same transaction: the guard would be a rule each caller must remember, and the close-time sweep only reclaimed a few megabytes sooner.
- **`replaceDepth` beside `replaceMask` and `replacePlate`** (R5-FRESH-7's first remedy).
  Lost to one idempotent `load`: the derived textures were already keyed by asset id, so three piecewise methods asked the caller to say what the renderer can see for itself.
- **"Drop original" as an undoable edit** (R5-FRESH-7's fourth item).
  Lost to a Library action on a closed scene: an undoable drop must keep the bytes while the scene is open, and an action whose purpose is to free space should free it; the owner kept the action (Q2).
- **Two RGBA16F master-size targets for the crunch** (R5-FRESH-5's remedy as written).
  Lost to a luma plane at master size and a chroma plane at half size, which is how the rule is defined anyway: 18.9 MB against 50.3 MB.

Alternatives weighed in the round-5 audit, and why each lost (`reviews/round5.md` part 8):

- **The harmony fallback anchor as the largest entry whose chroma weight is above 0** (the closing revision).
  Lost to the largest product of weight and chroma weight: the first is the chroma threshold the round-5 list above rejects for saturation, moved to the anchor, where one 8-bit step in a nearly gray region's mean changed which hue the whole palette harmonized around (R5-CA-1).
- **Clamping the shifted light tint per channel when it is written** (the closing revision).
  Lost to the gamut bound at the variant's lightness: the clamp collapsed or reordered the lit bands of saturated surfaces and skewed the hue by a different amount in each band (R5-CA-2).
- **A bound that lets lightness give way**, clipping the shifted color toward mid gray or toward the cusp, so that a band near white takes more of a light's color.
  Lost: it moves each lit band's lightness by a different amount, which is the defect being fixed; on the same 288 cases it brought two bands of one entry within 0.01 L of each other or out of order in 31 and 32, against 9 for the clamp and 0 for the bound at constant lightness (VERIFIED arithmetic, `light_tint.txt` part 6).
  What it would buy, color on highlights, is a taste value the owner judges at P7 (Q12).
- **`Cmax` for the bound from an L-by-hue table texture, or from an analytic intersection with the sRGB gamut** (R5-CA-2's two forms).
  Both lost to twelve halvings in pass 3: the table is a new texture whose filtering differs between GPUs, and the analytic form is six cubic solves for the same answer; the halvings need no resource, and their cost is measured at P7's render gate (INFERRED small: they run only on lit fragments whose shifted color is outside sRGB).
- **The working texel's label as the majority label of its block** (R5-CA-6's second form).
  Lost to one named master texel: a majority needs a tie rule for a block split two against two and four more fetches, for a choice that only has to be the same on every device.
- **Keeping the storage line hidden whenever `persist()` was granted, with a note that the condition waits on U7** (R5-CA-5's second form).
  Lost to showing the Safari sentence whenever the app is not standalone: the line is then present in the one case the design's own text says needs it, under either answer U7 can give.
- **Comparing the crunch against a float reference of the same rule within 1 of 255** (R5-CA-4's first form for the P3b.4 row).
  Lost by arithmetic: a coefficient that rounds to the other side of a quantization step in one of two correct pipelines moves pixels by up to 3 of 255 at `crunch` 0.05, 19 at 0.42, and 43 at 1, so the row would fail with half-float targets that are precise enough; closeness to the input at `crunch` 0.01, where the stage is close to an identity, measures the precision itself (`experiments/render-review/crunch_precision.txt`).

Alternatives weighed in round 6, the revision that applied the owner's answers and closed the missing definitions (`reviews/round6.md`), and why each lost:

- **Harmony sectors of unequal coverage** (Cohen-Or's templates, and a narrow 60 degree `analogous` beside three 30 degree `triadic` sectors).
  Lost to four templates that each cover 90 degrees: under Q4 b the automatic choice minimizes movement, and unequal coverage would make it a choice of the widest template rather than of where the hues sit (R6-DEF-1).
- **Fitting the rotation by a 1 degree search** over `theta`.
  Lost to the exact candidate set: the fit cost is piecewise linear with kinks only at anchor-on-feature rotations, so evaluating those is exact, needs no step constant, and puts one anchor precisely on the first sector's center.
- **Palette targets that pin their cluster** as a separate entry, and **targets stored per resolved entry**.
  The first lost because every target would grow the palette by one and duplicate colors when a tapped entry held several clusters; the second because a resolved entry has no identity across a change of `colors`, where a cluster index does (R6-DEF-1, Q4 a).
- **A stored harmony template written by the reducer whenever anchors change** (Q4 b).
  Lost to resolving `auto` at read time: a stored copy of a derived value would need a side-effect edit on every recolor and an inverse for it; `ResolvedPalette.template` shows the choice without storing it.
- **The three light sums in RGBA8 with a range and sign encoding**, and **pre-normalized shifts written by pass 2**.
  The first lost by arithmetic (a 0.02 step in a and b is 3 to 4 degrees of hue); the second because the ray target is added to the lit target in pass 3 and normalized sums do not add.
  Half floats cost 4 MB and make pass 3 the reference arithmetic (R6-DEF-3).
- **A smooth ramp for `banded3`**, or the raw `N.L`.
  Lost: the spec puts lighting inside the banded look, and a facing gradient would read as photoreal shading under the tone bands; three levels at 0.25 and 0.75 give one cel edge per light (R6-DEF-2).
- **A working grid of `floor(W / 4)`**, leaving the last one to three master columns and rows to clamped taps.
  Lost to `ceil` with a clamped label texel, which is what the spike measured and which gives every master texel a working texel of its own block (R6-DEF-4).
- **Restating P3b.3's label row on `gbufferAt`'s output.**
  Lost: no `simplify` value makes the upsampled color equal the unfiltered downsample, because the radius and the detail weight cannot both be neutral at once; a dev-only read of the downsample's own target is exact and costs one test-surface function (R6-DEF-5).
- **A runtime-injected migration table** (a codec passed into `openStore` and the file reader, or a mutable table behind the test surface), for the older-version rows.
  Lost to a build-time substitution of `core/migrate.ts`: injection would thread a parse chain through the store, the codec, the session, and the app shell for a dev-only need, and a mutable table would make `core` impure; the harness build changes one module and no production line (R6-DEF-6).
  The pure factory `parseDocumentWith` of 5.2 is not that alternative (R6-4): it threads nothing and mutates nothing, `parseDocument` is the factory applied to `MIGRATIONS`, and it exists because the node row of P2a.1 has no esbuild in its path; a loader hook or an import condition for node were the other ways to reach the chain there, and each is a second substitution mechanism where a function argument is none.
- **Keeping the automatic subject's decoder to `decoderIdleMs`.**
  Lost to releasing it after its one decode: two minutes of 100 to 150 MB beside the first plate refill, for a tap that a respawn of under a second serves as well (R6-DEF-7).
- **Making the export's "no decoder alive" true by reasoning about tabs alone.**
  Lost to a release at the end of the export's wait: one call makes the invariant structural whatever the UI's intent state, and its only cost is a respawn if the owner returns to the Objects tab after exporting.
- **Cycling SAM's candidates without recording which one was kept** (Q5 a).
  Lost: `Selection` promises a re-runnable mask, and without the index a re-run on the other device would pick the best-scoring candidate the owner had cycled away from.

Alternatives weighed in round 7, the interrogation fix pass (`reviews/round7.md`), and why each lost:

- **An MP4 at another size than the master's**: the size fallback to "the largest supported size at the same aspect", an odd master cropped through `visibleRect`, or masters rounded down to even only at the encoder (R7-C).
  All three lost to even masters at import: the first had no render path and could only run through the encoder's mandatory scaling, the second is ignored by WebKit's encode and still resampled in Chrome 154, and the third resampled every frame of an odd master; import already resamples the photo once, before any effect exists, so rounding there costs nothing visible.
  A `drawImage` copy into an even canvas does crop exactly, but it would add a master-size copy per frame to dodge a problem that need not exist.
- **The one-draw geometry path where `OES_draw_buffers_indexed` is present** (round 6, and the lead's first form of R7-F2).
  Lost to two draws on every device, for three reasons: the two-draw path depends on no extension, it is one path on every device, and the Mac's goldens therefore run the phone's path; what the one-draw variant saves is one color-only draw over each object's bounds.
  It would also keep the alpha-tested depth and surface writes apart from the coverage-blended color only by blending into the R16F depth attachment as a selector, which 9.1's no-blending rule declines; that rule is this design's own choice, since `EXT_float_blend` governs 32-bit targets only and blending into 16-bit targets is allowed under either extension, so it is a consequence of the choice and not a platform reason (R7-17).
- **Sliding only the variants under the band-window rule** (the ledger row read narrowly, R7-B7).
  Lost to sliding the window on both sides: sliding the variants alone left a near-white entry's own pixels in a middle band up to 0.17 L darker than themselves and failed P3b.1's gray-ramp row, where sliding both keeps every pixel within about half a band of its lightness and every P3b.1 row passing.
  Compressing the variants toward the clamped end, the other shape the ledger weighed, lost there because it weakens the spacing guarantee.
- **A separate estimate call for the GIF, or an estimate shown while the job runs on** (R7-D3).
  Lost to a pause inside the job: the estimate needs the palette, which needs frames rendered inside the export phase, so a separate call would have drawn them twice, and a streamed estimate arrives after the owner has committed, not before as Q8 says.
- **An export that drops the taps still waiting on the encoder** (one reading of R7-D4's dispatch).
  Lost to an export that waits for them: a dropped tap discards input made before the export, which the exclusive phase exists to include, and the export already waits for the encoder those taps wait on.
- **A focus stored as a reference to an object** (`{ kind: 'object'; id }`), so that removing the object could reset it (the interrogation's alternative to R7-D6).
  Lost to deleting the reset: no control sets focus from an object, and a disparity stays meaningful after its object is gone.
- **The event union and the test surface in `core`** (one reviewer's alternative to R7-B3).
  Lost to the composition root: `core` would then own the vocabulary of every module and name analysis, store, and render types it must not know.
- **`analysis/checks.ts` moved into P1.2** (the other half of R7-B2's choice).
  Lost to moving the known-answer halves of two rows to P1.3, which owns the checks, because P1.2 is already at the 500-line ceiling.
- **A score threshold in the automatic subject's gate** (the challenger's SAM score above 0.7).
  Lost to coverage and edges alone, with the score recorded at U15: the 0.7 was never measured, and an unmeasured constant would change the heuristic the owner's ten-photo count judges.
- **A runtime probe for the MP4 frame source, recorded in the environment snapshot** (round 6's wording).
  Lost to a per-device-class policy constant with a fallback on a throw at the first frame: the probe had no field, no event, and no moment, while the constant is set by the phone's measurement like the others and lets the Mac test every path.
- **Scoping the no-console check to first-party sources** (the other half of R7-E's choice).
  Lost to dropping the calls in production builds: one flag removes third-party logging the design never reads, and the check stays a check of what ships.
- **`--migrations` tested only from P2a.1, and the four build entries staged as their parts land** (R7-B6's alternatives).
  Lost to throwaway modules in P0.1's test and three worker stubs from P0.1: the code P0.1 writes is tested where it is written, and the worker type-check project has files from the first commit.

Alternatives weighed in round 7's audit pass, the bounded fix of `R7-1` to `R7-21` (`reviews/round7.md` part 6), and why each lost:

- **Normals differenced from the 16-bit refined depth** (the round-6 derivation, with the smoothed copy in R16F or smoothed in float in the same pass).
  Lost by simulation: worst `N.L` errors of 0.013 to 0.080 on P7.1's tiles, 0.036 to 0.136 with a 16-bit copy, against margins of 0.05, and 1.7% of a gentle dome's texels in the wrong `banded3` level away from a level's edge with no model noise at all (R7-1).
- **Smoothing the f32 depth at depth resolution and then differencing it** (R7-1's preferred mechanism read literally: a label-aware 5 x 5 smoothing, central differences one-sided at a label edge, the four gradients around the point interpolated).
  Lost to the weighted plane fit, which is the same mechanism with one estimator: equal on the tiles and on noise, exact from one texel inside a label edge the depth follows sharply where smoothing and then differencing stays up to 1.17 off in `N.L` for four texels and 0.13 off at the fifth, a texel sooner beside a soft edge, and 148 reads per texel against 193 or more (R7-1).
- **A plane fit over a 5 x 5 window.**
  Lost to 7 x 7: under white noise of 0.0001 in disparity it doubles the `N.L` error (0.017 against 0.009 RMS) and triples the texels in the wrong level (0.85% against 0.29%), for an edge band one texel narrower (R7-1).
- **Carrying the refined depth in more than 16 bits** (a split RG16F or a packed RGBA8 copy, round 7 part 5's third candidate).
  Lost: it adds a master-size target to 8.2 and still differences an upsample of the same depth-resolution data, whose detail the upsample does not add.
- **Normals computed on the CPU at depth resolution** and uploaded as an RGBA8 texture.
  Lost: exactly deterministic, but a heavier loop than the background fill on the phone's JavaScript for every change of labels (INFERRED), and its texels would be depth texels, coarser than the label texture at every label edge, where pass 1 would filter one object's normals into its neighbor's.
- **An 8-bit pair stored as `round(255 (v + 1) / 2)`**, the usual encoding.
  Lost to `round(127 v) + 127`: the usual one puts zero at 127.5, a rounding boundary, and in the simulation a second summation order flipped a byte on 27.5% of the sampled tile texels, all in the component that is zero there, against none with zero stored exactly (R7-1).
- **Distances measured in view space**, the sentence round 7 left in section 10.
  Lost to frame points (R7-14): under the loop camera view space is zoomed and displaced, so every distance changed by up to the 4% zoom and a light's facing moved across surfaces at other disparities than its own as the orbit turned.
- **Recovering each pixel's frame point in pass 2 by inverting the camera and the parallax at the pixel's own disparity.**
  Lost to the frame attachment: the inverse is approximate where a mesh cell's interpolated displacement differs from the pixel's own, it cannot see object motion, and it is the inverse-camera math the registration rule keeps out of passes 2 to 7 (9.3).
- **The frame attachment in RG16F, or kept across renders.**
  Lost to a packed RGBA8 target that lives from pass 1 to pass 2: RG16F has another pool key than the ping-pong pair, and a persistent target holds 12.6 MB more at the peak, against a renderer residency at about 213 of its 225 MB target, to save a geometry pass per frame of a light drag, which playback pays anyway (R7-14).
- **`relief` entering the shadow march as `z = relief * d`.**
  Lost to a march in the lighting space of R7-D1: scaling depth would move the points that rule keeps fixed, so a light's distance and direction would depend on `relief` after all, and a control for shading would retune every shadow, which `thickness` already sets (R7-2).
- **Pass 1's color row restricted to outside the hole on `synth.noisy`** (R7-5's second option).
  Lost to a plate-free variant: outside the hole the objects drop out of the row, which exists to show that objects drawn over the back layer reproduce the master.
- **`tsconfig.json` listing `src/main.tsx` and letting imports decide what it checks** (R7-8's second option).
  Lost to keeping worker-global calls in the worker entries: a node test that imports a worker-only helper would fail the test project in the same way, and the entry rule keeps all three projects whole.
- **Lazy refinement of candidates 1 and 2 adopted now** (R6-7, R7-16).
  Lost to 5.5's contract as written: nothing measured says the three refinements cost too much, and the contract changes only if P4.2's per-tap cost misses P4.3's 0.2 s target.

Alternatives weighed in the last fix-up, the bounded fix of `R7-22` to `R7-32` (`reviews/round7.md` part 7), and why each lost:

- **Scissoring the background pair's rebuild after a change of the hole to the texels where the old and new fills differ**, dilated by 3.5 depth texels (the auditors' other fix for R7-22).
  Lost to the whole rebuild that R7-22 chose: it needs the two fills compared texel by texel on the CPU and a rectangle that spans both holes whenever they touch, where the whole rebuild is one fixed draw at a third of a whole rebuild's reads and exact by construction; it stays the first lever if the phone misses 60 ms (section 20, U5b).
- **Filling each hole component from its own boundary only** (`verification.md` 13.3 item 9's third remedy).
  Lost: it changes what the fill computes wherever holes touch or nest, and a component's fill still moves whenever a new hole merges with it.
  Superseded for the fill itself by R7-32, which fills per merged component with a plane; the second half of the argument still holds against a rebuild scissored to the new hole alone, which is why R7-22's whole rebuild stands.
- **Moving the CPU fill into the render and counting it under `normals`** (R7-23's other option).
  Lost to a `render.derived` event from `load`: the fill needs only the depth and the masks, which `load` holds, and one number mixing CPU and GPU time would not say which lever a miss calls for.
- **A crease value computed at depth resolution in the normals pass and carried into the G-buffer** (R7-24's example).
  Lost: no channel can hold it, since the normals texture's four bytes hold the two pairs and the surface attachment's four hold the id, the pair, and the texture-edge value; pass 1 cannot put it in the color attachment's alpha, because an object's alpha-tested draw leaves that attachment out and a mask for one attachment's channels needs `OES_draw_buffers_indexed`, which 9.1 declines; and a new texture was outside the item's bounds, while pass 4 already reads every normal and id the rule needs.
- **Comparing neighbouring pixels**, the plain reading of the earlier text.
  Lost: across a 35-degree crease neighbours differ by at most 4.3 degrees at 2048 x 1536 (8.2 at 1024 x 768), so no crease ever draws, and the reading fires only where normals jump at an id edge (R7-24).
- **The turn across a span alone, with no condition on the crease's sides.**
  Lost: a surface bending steadily at 1.2 or 2 degrees per px filled a band 31 to 77 px wide with outline, since its middle condition holds at every pixel of a steady bend (R7-24).
- **Comparing normals across ids.**
  Lost: beside a disc whose depth edge is soft, as a depth model's is, it drew 1,604 to 3,354 px of line inside the disc along its rim, and 697 to 1,576 with a sharp edge, a second line standing off the silhouette, while the id source already draws every id edge (R7-24).
- **Filing the canvas back buffer under the pool's key of the same format and size** (R7-27).
  Lost to its own key, `canvas`: a count of master-size targets could not tell a pooled target from the back buffer.
- **`renderAt` in the scheduler's current mode** (R7-30).
  Lost to full mode at divisor 1: an oracle that changed with the moment it ran would not be one, and the rows about low mode and the divided preview read the canvas.
- **One sentence for both `too-small` refusals** (the audit's other fix for R7-28, dropping E1's clause).
  Lost: a strip thousands of pixels long refused as "too small" reads as wrong, where naming the short side says what to change.
- **Push-pull alone for the background depth, with the seam it leaves on sloped surfaces judged on J5** (R7-32's other option).
  Lost: under a light the ring around an object on a sloped surface could take another `banded3` level than the surface beside it, a halo in a feature built on light, while the plane costs one 3 x 3 solve per hole component on the CPU.
- **A harmonic fill (a Laplace solve with the ring as its boundary)**, which also reproduces planes exactly.
  Lost: it needs a multigrid solver to converge at depth resolution, where the plane plus a residual reuses the push-pull already written.

## 22. Implementation packages in dependency order

Each package is one commit's worth of reviewable change with its own verification interface and observed result; every package updates `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`, and `docs/CHANGELOG.md` in the same change.
No package is dispatched until the owner says go.

Package size (R4-FRESH-12).
A package is at most about 500 source lines by estimate, not counting tests, fixtures, and golden images.
Round 3's seventeen rows promised "a few hundred lines" and by this file's own figures held several times that: P3a alone carried the 400-line GL harness beside the kernel, the asset cache, the facade, and the Look tab.
Each of those rows is now a **group** with numbered **parts**, and the parts are what is dispatched.
**The table's order is the dispatch order**, and it is a dependency order: every part's contents and rows need only what the parts above it build (R7-B1, R7-B2).
A part's id is a frozen name, never a position: a part added later takes the next free number in its group and sits in the table where it is dispatched, and moving a part never renumbers it, so P2a.1 to P2a.3 come before P1.1, P0.4 follows P0.3, and P3b.5 comes before P3b.3 (round 7).
A bare id such as P3b still names the group, so earlier rounds' citations stay valid, and P1 still means the phone feasibility work as a whole.
The estimates are INFERRED from the module sizes this file names (9.1, section 18) and from comparable code; a part that turns out larger is split again before it is dispatched, not shipped as a phase.

Two gates, neither circular (AUDIT-1):

- **The memory gate is P1.** P2a.1 to P2a.3 now come before it, because P1.1 to P1.3 are typed with their types (`DepthStats` and `TapPoint` from `core/types.ts`, `AnalysisOp` from `core/derive.ts`); they are pure node code with no phone dependency, so they cost agent time before the owner's first long sitting and nothing else (R7-B2).
  P1.4's U1 rows run first in the owner's sitting and are read before anything else in P1; P1 gates P3c onward, everything that depends on the analysis memory numbers.
  The parts listed after P1.5 up to P3b.5, which sits just before P3b.3, may be built while the owner's P1 sitting is pending, because their code does not depend on its numbers; the master size and the cooldown constants stay behind `TimingTable` and `RenderPolicy` and are treated as provisional until U1 is read (FRESH-1).
  A U1 result that survives below the headroom line holds P3c until the owner has answered Q9 (R5-FRESH-6).
  P3b.3, the simplify stage, waits for P1.5's simplification row (R4-FRESH-2), and P3b.5's geometry path no longer waits for anything, since pass 1 has one path on every device (R7-F2).
- **The render gate is U5b**, measured on the phone by the packages that own each pass: P3b, P5, P6a, and P7, with the simplification rerun measured ahead of its package by P1.5.
  Each records ms per pass in `render.frame` events against the 25 ms frame target; the first that misses sets `policy.previewDivisor` to 2 on the phone and says so in its briefing.
  P7.1's normals rebuild is held to the simplify stage's 60 ms instead, in texture space where the divisor does not reach, and a miss goes to the lead with its numbers before P7.2 is dispatched (9.3, U5b, R7-23).
  Beyond that row, P1 contributes only the capability probe, which settles which float extension the renderer enables and the phone's MP4 frame source, and the bandwidth proxy (U5a, U13).

**Fixtures (R7-B4, R7-13).**
The fixture generator is `experiments/iphone-bench/scene.js` promoted into `test/fixtures/synth/` (section 17), and P1.2 promotes it, as the first part whose rows read it: `synth` at 2048 x 1536 and 512 x 384 and `synth-pano` at 2048 x 512, each with exact depth, masks, and plate.
Every other generated fixture of `verification.md` 2.6 is a flag or module of the same generator and lands with the first part whose rows open it, except three separate generators, the HEIC generators of `experiments/heicexp/`, the Swift generator of the EXIF orientation photos, and the size-limit images, whose parts 2.6 names.
A fixture that a renderer row opens is written as a `.noisy` file by one mechanism, the generator writing through the project's writer (P2c.2), which lands in P3b.2, the first part that renders one, with `synth.noisy` and `synth-minimal.noisy`; every later fixture's `.noisy` is written by the same mechanism in the part its generator lands with (`synth-noplate.noisy` in P3b.5, `tiles` in P7.1, the others where 2.6 says), so no `.noisy` waits on a function a later part builds (R7-13).
Each takes its statistics from exact geometry and the generated pixels by fixed rules the generator states, using `core/color.ts`'s Oklab conversions (P2d.1, R7-3), never P2d.1's k-means or P4.1's refinement, so no renderer row waits on those parts; the `.noisy` fixtures are frozen bytes thereafter, and regenerating them yields the same bytes.
P2c.2 writes its own minimal scene for the codec and its two Finder re-zips (13.4); the re-zips are frozen bytes exempt from that regeneration rule, like the experiment archives, because `ditto` embeds file timestamps (R7-15).
The freeze fixtures, `MIGRATIONS[0]`'s pair and the writer-stability check of the frozen scene, belong to the freeze task of Q18, which runs when the owner declares the first scene worth keeping, expected after v1 (R7-12), not to any part here.
Fixtures do not count against a part's estimate.
