P10, the offline app shell, is out of v1 (Q11, settled): no service worker, no update flow, and no package; the app loads from the Mac over Tailscale until the AWS host exists.

## 23. Owner decisions (USER-OWNED): nineteen settled, two open

The owner settled every question but Q9 and Q15 on 2026-09-30 (`decisions.tsv`, gate3 rows 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z; the ledger row each decision cites is that timestamp unless another is named), and on 2026-10-01 amended Q1 and settled Q21 (gate3 rows 2026-10-01T03:29:44Z).
Each settled question below reads as the decision, what it binds in this design, and its ledger row; the design's earlier recommendation is kept only where the decision departed from it or where the reasoning is still the reference.
Q9 and Q15 stay open and conditional exactly as before; the owner actions the plan needs close the section.

- **Q1, the dependency batch: approved as listed in section 18, and amended on 2026-10-01 to add `@types/node`.** `preact` 10.29.8, `zod` 4.6.5, and `mediabunny` 1.60.0 at runtime; `typescript` 7.0.2 and `esbuild` 0.28.x in dev, pinned at 0.28.2; the three model weight sets.
  The amendment approves `@types/node` as a dev-only dependency, pinned exactly to a version matching Node 25, 25.9.8: typings only, with no runtime code, loaded only by the node tests' tsconfig, without which the `node:test` files fail the type check or go unchecked (section 5, section 18, R7-A1).
  P0.1 pins these exact versions; newer releases (Preact 11.0.0, Mediabunny 1.61.0) are separate, later decisions (R7-E).
  Rejected, and the list stands: `gifenc`, `fflate` (unless the zip reader outgrows 400 lines), `libheif-js`, `@huggingface/transformers`, three or regl, Vite, Playwright.
  The shipped bundle has four runtime dependencies.
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q1 approved as listed"; `onnxruntime-web` gate3, 2026-09-29T02:43:27Z; gate3, 2026-10-01T03:29:44Z, "approved @types/node as a dev-only dependency, pinned exactly, amending Q1's list".
- **Q2, the picked photo's bytes: kept in every scene, with the Drop original action.** `frame.source` holds the picked file's bytes on both devices; the Library offers a per-scene Drop original, a closed-scene action under the scene lock and behind a confirm that saves `frame.source` null and sweeps at once (5.3 `dropSource`, 5.9 `dropOriginal`, 13.2, R5-FRESH-7); the storage line shows how much the kept originals cost (`SceneSummary.sourceBytes`).
  What it is not (FRESH-13): on the phone the Photos picker hands every browser a re-encoded JPEG with auxiliary data stripped (the bench photo: 3,971,900 bytes for 24 MP), so a phone scene's kept source is that JPEG, not the HEIC original; only the Files app path and a Mac import keep originals.
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q2 keep the picked photo with a Drop original action".
- **Q3, the phone's Look panel: Fry, Colors, Saturation, and Symbols, with the detailed sliders under Advanced; the laptop shows everything.** `fry` is the macro of section 10 over eight fried sliders (FIT-1); P3a.5 builds the grouping.
  The Look panel's Symbols control is the on/off switch, `symbols.enabled`, and the amount and the cell size live in the Symbols tab; this is the design's default, which the owner can overrule, since the answer named the control and not which of its settings (R7-F3).
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q3 shorter Look panel on the phone".
- **Q4, palette-level recolor and harmony: both.** (a) The Look tab's swatch strip over the resolved palette; tapping a swatch stores a palette target in `style.paletteTargets`, keyed by the source cluster index, which the entry containing that cluster takes as its color and which anchors the harmony like a recolored object (5.2, 5.3 `palette.target`, section 10 Palette targets; objects remain for spatial recolor).
  (b) Harmony on by default with `kind: 'auto'`, the template chosen at resolve time as the one that fits the anchors and then moves the palette least, the picker under Advanced showing the choice (section 10 Harmony, whose sector table is R6-DEF-1; section 11).
  The owner's frog-on-blue example uses (a) for the background half (FIT-2); with its two anchors 118 degrees apart, as section 10 states it, it lands on `triadic` under (b), the only template that fits both, while from 120 to 150 degrees apart `split-complementary` fits too (the sRGB blue of hue 264 against the section 3 call site's green of hue 142 is 122 apart) and the entries' movement decides (VERIFIED arithmetic on the sector table, R6-5).
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q4 palette-swatch recolor plus automatic harmony template".
- **Q5, tap ergonomics: both.** (a) The decoder's three candidate masks stay in the driver's memory and a repeated tap on the selected object cycles them through the `cycle` intent, as an `object.refine` that records the candidate index in `Selection` (5.2, 5.9, 7.1), skipping a candidate whose mask is empty (7.1 item 2, R6-3), with refine kept for real corrections.
  (b) When no subject exists the first created object becomes the subject, reversible in the Objects tab (the reducer rule of 5.3).
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "the defaults stand as recommended: Q5".
- **Q6, the automatic subject: kept, conditional on P4's ten-photo test.** The depth-seeded SAM guess lands about 0.5 s after the encoder with `analysis.autoSubject` in the document (5.2, 5.4, 7.3, U15); if the first guess is wrong on most of the owner's ten photos, it is removed with a one-field migration (ENG-11, JUDGE-B-11).
  Either way there is no guess when depth failed: the first tap defines the subject (R3-FRESH-1).
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q6 (conditional on P4)".
- **Q7, video grain: `grainSize` 2 and `grainHold` 2 as the defaults, with the bitrate slider.** Applied to the preview too, so what the owner sees is what exports; the slider is capped by `isConfigSupported` (section 11, section 14, EXPORT-1).
  The measurements the defaults rest on (`experiments/render-review/h264_grain*.txt`, Mac VideoToolbox; the phone's encoder is U12): per-pixel per-frame grain keeps 15% at 25 Mbps; 2 px cells held 2 frames keep 42.8% at 25 Mbps and 46.9% at 28 Mbps, and 46.5% at 28 Mbps with the default crunch 0.42; hold 3 keeps 46.7%; 3 px cells keep 46.8% (hold 2) to 55.0% (hold 3), and 56.6 to 62.7% with crunch.
  Hold 2 is the finer temporal texture and clears the 40% gate on the measured shape; `grainSize` 3 is the slider position that trades finer grain for 10 to 15 points more retention.
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q7".
- **Q8, GIF budget: 480 px long edge, 10 cs frame delay, grain and clipping on the GIF grid, no crunch, a size estimate before export, target under 10 MB for 3 s, MP4 presented as the faithful format.** The two rules that make it work stay (AUDIT-12, FRESH-10): the GIF renders with its own frame count so its loop closes at any delay, and the camera stage runs on the GIF grid after the downsample, because a 2048 to 480 box downsample averages 2 px master grain to 0.47 px (section 8, section 14).
  A 640 px GIF with live grain measured 21 MB for 3 s (`gif_size.txt`); 480 px is about half that.
  The estimate is a pause inside the GIF job: once the palette and frame 0 are built the job reports frame 0's encoded size times the frame count and waits for the owner to confirm or cancel, reusing the palette on confirm (5.9, section 14, R7-D3).
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q8 GIF at 480 px and 10 fps with grain on its own grid".
- **Q9, the encoder fallback: OPEN, decided only if P1's U1 rows fail at the encoder stage with the sequence worker in place, or survive with less than the 300 MB headroom line (R5-FRESH-6).** This is the one unknown that can make the phone product infeasible as designed (FRESH-1; section 20 names the three whose contingencies change it in other ways, R7-F6), so the memo is pre-written for a single sitting; the numbers column is filled from P1.

  | Option | Mask quality | Phone taps | Format impact | Phone time | Memory (INFERRED until benched) |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | Ship as designed on the measured margin (a row only when the encoder survived below the headroom line, R5-FRESH-6) | unchanged | unchanged | none | unchanged | none: the headroom stays what P1 measured; expect occasional foreground kills during the encoder on the phone, and a scene killed twice there ends `blocked-after-crashes` for tap-to-select until Retry, or until the laptop finishes it |
  | Keep SAM 2.1 tiny, `numThreads: 1` | unchanged | unchanged | none | encoder about 1.6x slower (9 s) | none: the peak is 791 to 796 MB at 1, 2, and 4 threads (measured), so this is not a rescue, listed only to close it |
  | Keep SAM 2.1 tiny, no renderer while the encoder runs (a row only if P1's no-GL condition survives where full and low mode do not, R3-FRESH-5) | unchanged | unchanged | none | analysis unchanged; on the phone a new photo shows as the plain photo until the sequence ends, about 10 s, once per photo per device | removes the renderer's GPU-process residency during the encoder; the content-process peak is unchanged |
  | Re-exported SAM 2.1 tiny at 512 input | worse edges on thin structures; same behavior otherwise | unchanged | none (`ModelId` changes, embeddings recomputed) | about a quarter of the encoder time | about a quarter of the activation memory; the 52.6 MB weights unchanged; needs a bench row |
  | EdgeSAM or MobileSAM class encoder | different mask behavior on the owner's photos | unchanged | none (`ModelId` changes) | faster | smaller by design; needs a bench row |
  | Mac-only encoding, embeddings in the scene file | unchanged | none until the scene has visited the Mac; the phone still gets depth, the look, motion, and lights | a format change: embeddings (about 17 MB) become an asset the file carries, `reconcile` lists `encode` only on the Mac | phone import drops to about 4 s | removes the budget-defining stage from the phone entirely |

  Recommendation: do not change the encoder pre-emptively; if U1 fails, bench the 512-input export and one EdgeSAM-class model on the phone with the same harness before the owner chooses, and treat Mac-only encoding as the fallback of last resort because it makes tap-to-select a laptop feature (PHONE-9).
  If U1 survives below the line, the same two benches run before the owner chooses, with shipping on the measured margin on the table beside them; how thin a margin is acceptable is the owner's call, and this design names no number below the line as safe.
  If the no-renderer row is in the memo it is the mildest option on the list, and it is still the owner's call, because it takes the look out of the first ten seconds of a new photo on the phone.
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q9 and Q15 stay conditional".
- **Q10, HEIC in Chrome on the Mac: the v1 limitation is accepted.** The message points to Safari or JPEG (section 12); no 1.4 MB LGPL decoder ships.
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q10".
- **Q11, the phone without the Mac: no.** No service worker and no offline shell in v1; the app loads from the Mac over Tailscale, photos are picked into it there, and model re-download and app load need the Mac reachable until the AWS host exists at release (7.2, 13.5); P10 leaves the package list (section 22).
  Durability (`persist()`, the notice, the install hint) was never part of this question and ships in P2d (PERSIST-1).
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q11 no offline shell in v1 ... P10 is out of v1"; revisit at release, when the host exists.
- **Q12, taste defaults: section 11's table ships as written, with the round-5 look rules.** Every value is a slider.
  The rules the owner judges on their photos in the packages that own them: the quarter-grid simplification and the subject's detail weight at P3b (R4-FRESH-2); neutral entries staying neutral under `saturation` and harmony (R5-FRESH-1); a white light lifting bands and leaving chroma alone, a colored light's shift held inside sRGB at each band's own lightness, so it shows most on midtones and hardly at all near white (R5-FRESH-2, R5-CA-2); and with symbols on a light shows as bands only (R5-AUDIT-1), which this decision confirms and which stays additive if the owner wants a tint under symbols after seeing it at P7 (section 21).
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q12 (lights tone-only with symbols on)".
- **Q13, old scenes after updates: accept the change, with an ADR per occurrence.** Settings are always preserved and migrated; where a look algorithm changes in a way no migration can reproduce, the saved scene renders with the new look and the exported PNG, MP4, and GIF are the permanent record (section 17).
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q13".
- **Q14, browser test tooling: the in-repo CDP driver plus `safaridriver`, not Playwright.** The owner enables "Allow remote automation" once (TEST-16; section 18).
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q14".
- **Q15, MI-GAN weights at release: OPEN, release-time.** MIT by their authors (`LICENSE-WEIGHTS`, CLAIM-1); whether MIT could be granted over a non-commercial teacher is a legal question the maintainer declined.
  Recommendation stands: proceed for personal use; before any public release, get a legal read or swap to LaMa (section 18).
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q9 and Q15 stay conditional"; gate3, 2026-09-29T02:43:27Z, "model-weight licenses must be checked before release".
- **Q16, undo: kept.** A bounded undo and redo history over inverse edit lists (5.3), Cmd-Z on the Mac and a button on the phone; it costs no format field (FIT-5).
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q16".
- **Q17, the 2048 px output ceiling: accepted on both devices.** The master is `min(2048, photo)` everywhere, the preview renders at master size, and stills and loops export at master size (section 8; FRESH-6); one canonical grid keeps one render path, one golden set, identical analysis on both devices, and preview-equals-export by construction.
  The kept source (Q2) makes a later Mac-only larger master a `SCENE_VERSION` bump with re-analysis, not a rewrite, and the phone would keep 2048.
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q17 2048 px output on both devices".
- **Q18, when the version rule starts binding: version 0 through development, adopted.** `MIGRATIONS` is empty and `SCENE_VERSION` its length, 0, until the owner declares the first scene worth keeping; at that freeze `MIGRATIONS[0]` is the identity onto the frozen shape, its fixture pair is captured (the last version-0 scene as the input and its version-1 reading as the reference, 13.4 rule 4, R6-5), and 13.4's rules bind from then on (5.2, 13.4, 17; FRESH-14, R3-AUDIT-5).
  The freeze is a task of its own, and it runs when the owner declares the first scene worth keeping, which is expected after v1; declaring one earlier is the owner's call under this decision and starts the freeze task then (R7-12).
  It writes `MIGRATIONS[0]`, captures that pair, and takes over the writer-stability check of the frozen scene, which round 6 had put on P2c.2, where no frozen scene can yet exist (R7-B4).
  Until the freeze a shape change promises no migration: a version-0 scene whose record no longer parses lists as damaged and is made again from its photo, and because unknown keys are rejected (13.4 rule 5) that includes every removal or renaming of a field (R5-CA-7).
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q18 (version 0 through development)".
- **Q19, what the canvas shows when a loop is not playing, and whether a loop without camera orbit is cropped: the still, and the identity camera at effective orbit 0.** First point: the canvas shows the still whenever playback is not running, including the whole time the Objects tab is active, so taps are exact for every layer and no inverse mapping exists (9.3, R3-FRESH-3); the visible cost is that a pause cannot hold a particular motion frame.
  Second point, option (a): the loop camera is the identity whenever `effectiveOrbit` is 0, so a loop with no camera orbit keeps the still's framing and a grain-only loop is exactly the still with moving grain; turning the orbit on changes the framing by 4%, and P6a.2 asserts both (5.4, section 8, 9.3, section 10 Motion, section 14; R4-FRESH-6).
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q19 (still when not playing; no crop for loops without camera orbit)".
- **Q20, what "never disappear" binds: option (a), a property of the look with outlines on.** The outline along every object edge is the mechanism (section 10 Subject clarity); at `outline` 0, and with symbols on, two same-colored neighbours can merge into one shape, which is recorded here and built around nowhere (`verification.md` 13.1 item 1; R5-OBS-6).
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-09-30T17:51:40Z, "Q20 (never disappear describes the look with outlines on)".
- **Q21, how noisy runs on the Mac once there is something to use: a start command the owner runs when wanted, not an always-on service, for now.** One command, `npm start` (`tools/start.mjs`), fetches and verifies any missing model file, runs the production build, starts `tools/serve.mjs` in its production mode (the production bundle, `/models/`, and `/vendor/ort/` with `headers.json`, and none of the dev surfaces), and brings up the Tailscale share in the foreground, so that stopping the command stops both; it refuses to start while a phone sitting's share is up, and the project `CLAUDE.md` gains a "Running noisy" section (section 19, P0.4, R7-A2).
  What it is not: a user launchd agent keeping the server and the share up whenever the Mac is on, which the interrogation recommended so that the phone works right after shooting; the owner chose the command, and the phone reaches noisy only while it runs.
  P0.4 builds it early, right after the build and the dev server exist, so the owner can run the current build at any point of the work, not only after v1.
  Scenes made before the freeze of Q18 may list as damaged after a later build and are made again from their photos, so the "Running noisy" section tells the owner to keep originals and names Export raw data as the way out of a damaged listing (13.4 rule 5, R7-12).
  Ledger: gate3, 2026-10-01T03:29:44Z, "noisy runs on the Mac by a start command the owner runs when wanted ... not an always-on launchd service, for now"; revisit if the owner wants it always on, or at release, when the AWS host exists.
